Integral Arnol’d Conjecture
Semon Rezchikov

Abstract




1
Introduction

Let
(M^{2n},\omega)
be a compact symplectic manifold. The letter
J
will always denote some compatible almost-complex structure. We will fix a time-dependent Hamiltonian
H=H_{t}
,
t\in[0,1]
, which generates a nondegenerate Hamiltonian diffeomorphism
\phi_{H}
.
In this document, we prove the following

Theorem 1

(Integral Arnold Conjecture)
.

The number of fixed points
|Fix(H)|
of a nondegenerate Hamiltonian diffeomorphism
\phi_{H}
is bounded below by the minimal rank of a cochain complex of free
\mathbb{Z}
modules with cohomology isomorphic to
H^{*}(M,\mathbb{Z})
.

The method is to set up a compatible system of global charts for Hamiltonian Floer Homology via an adaptation of
[
AMS21
]
, and then to apply the recent results involving FOP perturbations
[
BX22
]
to extract differentials for the Floer complex with integer values. This is a stronger result than the result established in
[
AB21
]
; the latter only proves the Arnol’d conjecture for all primes
p
, and so in particular fails to capture the interaction of
p
-torsion information for distinct primes simultaneously.

The general idea of using perturbations similar to the form used in this paper is due to McLean
[
McL21
]
. The technical contributions of this paper are a convenient two-form which makes it easy to make sense of McLean stabilization in the context of Hamiltonian Floer Homology (Section
3.1
), as well as a language in which to express how to compatibly choose perturbation data such that the various global charts can be
compatibly smoothed
and such that the resulting Kuranishi sections can be
inductively perturbed
. The author intends to use these charts for a somewhat more sophisticated result in an upcomping paper; this paper is written first because it is an easier result, in order to keep documents short, and to keep separate ideas into separate papers.

Much of the discussion is centered around around about the notion of a
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold
and a
derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold
. Regular Morse-theoretic moduli spaces (including those involved in more complex operations beyond the differential) are naturally smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds, and in the general case, we expect that the (compatible) global charts constructed in this paper should naturally give the moduli spaces underlying Floer-theoretic operations with multiple inputs and outputs the structure of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifolds. A
\langle k\rangle
-manifold is a type of manifold with corners to which one can apply the
doubling
trick – the natural generalization to manifolds with corners of the standard realization of a manifold-with-boundary as a fundamental domain for a
\mathbb{Z}/2
action on a closed manifold. Passing to the double allows us to avoid much of the general complexity of working with manifolds or orbifolds with corners, and in particular to smooth the moduli spaces using a standard application of equivariant smoothing theory on the doubled spaces. Compatibility is achieved by a generalization of uniqueness of stable equivariant smoothing up to concordance, together with taking collars, which is also a particularly nice operation on
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds. We thus hope that the combinatorial language and group-theoretic constructions of this paper will be helpful to others working in pseudoholomorphic curve theory.

Remark 1
.

The author was informed shorly before making this paper public that the forthcoming independent paper of Bai-Xu has also achieved a proof of this conjecture, with
analogous but distinct techniques. We hope that the geometric and combinatorial constructions of our paper can provide a shortcut to using these techniques for those interested in flexibly applying these tools to various problems in Floer theory.

Acknowledgements.

We thank Egor Shelukhin for his encouragement and commentary when the author was trying to understand
[
AMS21
]
. We also thank Mohammed Abouzaid for answering the author’s questions about
[
AMS21
]
. Finally, we thank Shayoun Bai and
Guangbo Xu for communication regarding their paper.

2
Basic Notions

2.1
Floer homology conventions

We write

S^{1}=[0,1]/(0\sim 1);Z=\mathbb{R}_{s}\times S^{1}_{t};D^{2}=\{z\in\mathbb{C};|z|\leq 1\};\partial D^{2}\simeq S^{1}\text{ via }z=e^{2\pi it}.

(1)

We have
i\partial/\partial_{s}=\partial/\partial_{t}
.

The Hamiltonian vector field
X_{H}
associated to a Hamiltonian function
H:M\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies
\omega(X_{H},v)=-dH(v)
. This convention reproduces the flow
X_{H}=p(\partial/\partial q)
of the standard free-particle Hamiltonian if we choose
H:\mathbb{R}^{2}_{p,q}\to\mathbb{R}
to be
\frac{1}{2}p^{2}
with
\omega=dpdq
. If
H
depends on an auxiliary time variable then we continue to write
X_{H}
for the associated time-dependent Hamiltonian vector field.

A capping of a free loop
x:S^{1}\to M
is a map
\tilde{x}:D^{2}\to M
which restricts to
x
on
S^{1}=\partial D^{2}
. Two cappings are equivalent if the functional

\bar{x}\mapsto\int_{\bar{x}}\omega

agrees on the cappings.

Remark 2
.

There is another variant of this equivalence relation, where one also requires that the functional

\bar{x}\mapsto\int_{\bar{x}}\tilde{c}_{1}(M)

where
\tilde{c}_{1}(M)
is any differential form representing the real first Chern class
c_{1}^{\mathbb{R}}(M)\in H^{2}(M,\mathbb{R})
agrees on the cappings. This allows one to define integrally graded Floer groups, which we will avoid in this paper.

Let
LM
be the free loop space of
M
, and let
\widetilde{LM}
be its Novikov cover. Elements of the latter are elements
x\in LM
equipped with equivalence classes
\bar{x}
of cappings of the loops
x
. The fiber over a loop
x
is naturally a torsor over the group

\Pi=\frac{\pi_{2}(M)}{\ker[\omega]}

(2)

via which acts on the fiber via connect sum
(A,\bar{x})\mapsto\bar{x}\#A.

We write
N
for minimal Chern number of
M
, that is, the positive generator of the subgroup
c_{1}(\Pi)\subset\mathbb{Z}
.

We define
\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}
to be completion of the integral group ring
\mathbb{Z}[\Pi]
with respect to the valuation defined by
\omega
.

We define the
universal Novikov ring

\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}
to be the set

\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}=\{\sum_{i}c_{i}T^{r_{i}}|c_{i}\in\mathbb{Z},r_{i}\in\mathbb{R},\#\{c_{i}\neq 0|r_{i}<R\}<\infty\text{ for all }R\in\mathbb{R}\}.

(3)

We write
\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z},0}
to consist of sums
\sum_{i}c_{i}T^{r_{i}}
with
r_{i}\geq 0
for all
i
, and
\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z},+}
to consist of such sums with
r_{i}>0
for all
i
.

We will write
Fix(H)=\{x:S^{1}\to M;x^{\prime}=X_{H},[x]=[pt]\in\pi_{0}(LM)\}
, and will write
\widetilde{Fix(H)}
for the set of equivalence classes of cappings of elements of
Fix(H)
.

Given
\bar{x}_{\pm}\in\widetilde{Fix}(H)
, we write
\mathcal{M}(\bar{x}_{-},\bar{x}_{+})
for the set of Floer trajectories

\{u:Z\to M:\partial_{s}u+J(\partial_{t}u-X_{H})=0,\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}u(s,\cdot)=x_{\pm}(\cdot),\bar{x}_{-}=\tilde{x}_{+}\#u.\}

(4)

Here the last line denotes the concatenation action of cylinders on cappings. The Floer compactifications of these spaces are denoted by
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})
; elements of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})
are sequences of Floer trajectories with pseudoholomorphic bubble trees attached.

For any given Floer trajectory, as
s
increases, the family of loops
u(s,\cdot)
traces out
[
salamon-zehnder
]
a negative gradient trajectory of the action functional
\mathcal{A}:\widetilde{LM}\to\mathbb{R}
given by

\mathcal{A}_{H}(x)=\int_{0}^{1}H(t,x(t))\,dt-\int_{\bar{x}}\omega.

(5)

The Floer cochain groups
CF^{k}(H,J;\mathbb{Z})
consist, as abelian groups, of infinite sums
\sum_{i}a_{i}\bar{x_{i}}
with
a_{i}\in\mathbb{F}
, all
\bar{x}_{i}
of Conley-Zehnder index
CZ(\tilde{x}_{i})=k\;(\mod 2N)
, and such that for any
C>0
, the set
\{i|a_{i}\neq 0,\omega(\bar{x}_{i})<C\}
is finite for all
C\in\mathbb{R}
. Our conventions on Conley-Zehnder indices are such that if we take a constant capping of a fixed point the Hamiltonian flow of a
C^{2}
-small morse function, its Conley-Zehnder index is equal to its index as critical point (modulo
2N)
. To compare with the conventins of
[
SZ92
]
, we have that
CZ(\tilde{x})=\mu_{\tau}(\tilde{x})+n
(for
\mu_{\tau}
as in
[
SZ92
]
). Whenever
\#Fix(\phi_{H})<\infty
, these are finitely generated free modules over
\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}
of dimension equal to the number of elements of
Fix(\phi_{H})
admitting a cap of Conley-Zehnder index equal to
k
modulo
2N
; a basis can be obtained by fixing an equivalence class of such a cap for each such periodic orbit. The task of this paper is to define a differential on this complex which is equivariant with respect to the operation of
\Pi
on these groups, and show that its quasi-isomorphism type agrees with the
2N
-periodized Morse cochain complex of
M
with coefficients in
\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

2.2
Constructions on manifolds with corners

In this section we will discuss the notion of a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold, which is a particularly convenient type of manifold with corners. The global Kuranishi charts we will produce for Floer trajectories will be
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds, and the extra structure will record the decomposition of the underlying Floer domain into a sequence of
1
-level Floer domains.

2.2.1
Manifolds with corners.

A stratified topological space
X
is a filtration on a space
X
by closed subsets

X=X(d)\supset X(d-1)\supset\ldots\supset X(0)\supset X(-1)=\emptyset
. The
open stratum
associated to
k=0,\ldots,d
is
X(k)\setminus X(k-1)=\mathring{X}(k)
.

More generally, we can consider a topological space
X
stratified by a poset
\mathcal{P}
. The data of such a stratification is an assignment, for each
p\in\mathcal{P}
, of a closed subset
X(p)\subset X
, such that if
p\leq q
then
X(p)\subset X(q)
. If
\mathcal{P}
is a finite poset, then we can define the open stratum associated to
p
to be

\mathring{X}(p)=X(p)\setminus\left(\bigcup_{q<p}X(q)\right).

This notion specializes to the previous one when we take
\mathcal{P}=\{0<1<\ldots<d\}.
Given
S\in\mathcal{P}
, we will refer to the maximal length of a chain of strictly increasing elements of
\mathcal{P}
starting with
S
as the
codimension of the stratum

X(S)
.

Write
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
for the closed positive orthant in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. This is space stratified by
0<\ldots<d
with
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}(i)
the subset with at least
d-i
coordinates equal to zero. A topological manifold of dimension
d+\ell
with codimension-
d
corners is a second-countable Hausdorff space
X
stratified by
0<\ldots<d
such that for every
x\in X
, there is an open neighborhood
X\supset V\ni x
and a homeomorphism called a
chart

\psi:\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\supset U\to V\subset X

(6)

such that
\psi(U\cap\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}(i)\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell})\subset X(i)
, and such that
\mathring{X}(d)\neq\emptyset
. A topological manifold with codimension-
d
corners is countable disjoint union of the above objects where
\ell
is allowed to vary. We will drop “codimension-
d
” if we do not need to specify
d
, although
d
is always finite. We note that topological manifolds are topological manifolds with codimension
d
corners for all
d\geq 0
.

A smooth manifold with codimension
d
corners is a topological manifold with corners equipped with a maximal atlas of charts to
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
with smooth transition functions between such charts.

2.2.2
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds

Let
[k]=\{1,\ldots,k\}
be the set with
k
elements. (We set
[0]=\emptyset
.) We can think of elements of
2^{[k]}
(note that
2^{\emptyset}
is a set of a single element) as subsets
S\subset[k]
; alternatively, we can think of them corresponding to partitions

P\in\mathcal{P}_{k+1}
of the ordered sequence
1,\ldots,k+1
. The bijection is as follows: if
[n]\setminus S=\{s^{\prime}_{1}<\ldots<s^{\prime}_{j}\}\subset[n]
, then the corresponding partition
P(S)
is
\{\{1,\ldots,s^{\prime}_{1}\},\{s^{\prime}_{1}+1,\ldots,s^{\prime}_{2}\},\ldots,\{s^{\prime}_{j}+1,\ldots,k+1\}\}
. We write
h(S)=(s^{\prime}_{1},s^{\prime}_{2}-s_{1},\ldots,n+1-s^{\prime}_{j})
for the sequence of lengths of elements of
P(S)
, which of course determines the partition
P(S)
itself. To clarify any ambiguity in the notation, we have that if
S=[k]
then
P(S)=\{\{1,\ldots,n+1\}\}
, and
h(S)=(k+1)
. We will write
S(h)
for the subset
S\subset[k]
associated to the sequence of lengths
h=(h_{1},\ldots,h_{j+1})
of a partition
P\in\mathcal{P}_{k+1}
. We will also write
|S|
for the cardinality of a subset
S\subset[k]
, and
|h|=|S(h)|+1
for the length of a sequence
h=h(S)
.

Now
2^{[k]}
is a poset; we will say that
S\leq T
if the partition
P(S)
refines the partition
P(T)
, or equivalently that
S\subset T
.

For each
S\in 2^{[k]}
, we have a closed subset
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}(S)\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}
consisting of those vectors for which the coordinates with indices in
[k]\setminus S
are forced to be zero. Thus, the partition
\{\{1,\ldots,k+1\}\}=P([k])
corresponds to
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}
, while the partition
\{\{1\},\ldots,\{n+1\}\}=P(\emptyset)
corresponds to
\{0\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
.

More generally, given a finite set
R
, we have the space
\mathbb{R}^{R}_{+}
, stratified by
2^{R}
with
\mathbb{R}^{R}_{+}(T)
, for
T\subset R
, given by the subset with coordinates in
R\setminus T
required to be zero.

Let
X
be a space stratified by
2^{[k]}
. For
x\in X
we write
S(x)=\min\{S\in 2^{[k]}\,|xinX(S)\}
, and we define
\text{sdim }X=|S(x)|
. Given
S\in 2^{[k]}
write
[k]\setminus S=\{r^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,r^{\prime}_{\ell}\}
. Then there is a unique isomorphism of posets

r_{S}:\{T\subset[k]\;|\>T\supset S\}\to 2^{[k\setminus|S|]}\text{ such that }r_{S}(S\cup\{s^{\prime}_{j}\})=\{j\}.

(7)

A
n
-dimensional
\langle k\rangle
-manifold chart
(centered at
x\in X
) is a homeomorphism

\psi:\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-|k\setminus S|}\supset U\to V\subset X

(8)

from an open subset
U\ni(0,0)
to an open subset
V
such that

•

For
y\in V
, we have that
S(y)\geq S(x)
, and moreover

•

For
[k]\supset T\supset S
, have that
\psi^{-1}(X(T))\subset\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}(r_{S}(T))\times\mathbb{R}^{n-|k\setminus S|}
; and finally

•

we say that the chart
\psi
is centered at
x
if
\psi(0,0)=x
.

A
topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
of dimension
n
is a second-countable Hausdorff space
X
stratified by
2^{[k]}
such that for every
x\in X
, there exists an
n
-dimensional
\langle k\rangle
-manifold chart centered at
x
, and such that
\mathring{X}([k])\neq\emptyset
. A
smooth

\langle k\rangle
-manifold is a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold equipped with a maximal subset (an
atlas
)of
n
-dimensional
\langle k\rangle
-charts such that transition maps between charts are smooth.

We will drop the dimension
n
when it is implied, and declare a countable disjoint union of topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds of varying dimension to be a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold. The quantity
k
can sometimes be a generic parameter; thus we may say that
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{1},\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\ldots
are all
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds. When
k
is a specific quantity, this should be clear from the context.

Remark 3
.

A
\langle k\rangle
-manifold is, with this definition, naturally a
\langle k+1\rangle
-manifold in many diferent ways, corresponding to the inclusions of posets
2^{[k]}\subset 2^{[k+1]}

induced from the
k+1
distinct inclusions of ordered sets
[k]\subset[k+1]
. This construction forces certain strata, e.g. the deepest stratum
X(\emptyset)
, to be empty, which is allowed. A
\langle k+1\rangle
-manifold, however, is never a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold.

Let
G
be a group. A topological
G
-
\langle k\rangle
-manifold is a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold with an action of
G
on the underlying space such that the action of
G
preserves each stratum. A smooth
G
-
\langle k\rangle
-manifold is a topological
G
-
\langle k\rangle
-manifold with underlying
\langle k\rangle
-manifold equipped with a smooth structure, such that the action of
G
on the corresponding manifold with corners is smooth. In particular, taking
k=0
, we recover the notions of topological and smooth
G
-manifolds.

We will write
G_{x}
for the stabilizer of
x\in X
of the action of
G
on a
G
-space
X
. We may also write
Stab(x)
when the group
G
is clear from the context.

Transversality on
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds.

Let
V
be a vector bundle over a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
X
, which just means a smooth vector over the underlying manifold with corners, and let
s
be a section of
V
. We say that
s
is
transverse
(along
K\subset X
) if, writing
s_{V}:U\to\mathbb{R}^{\dim V}
for the expression defining
s
in some chart
X\supset V\xrightarrow{\psi^{-1}}U\subset\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-|[k]\setminus S|}
and some
S\in 2^{[k]}
, we have that
ds_{U_{i}}
is surjective on the image of
K\cap s^{-1}(0)\cap V_{i}
in
U_{i}
for a collection of charts
\psi:U_{i}\simeq V_{i}
with codomains
V_{i}
covering
K
. Now, every substratum
X(S)
is naturally a
\langle k^{\prime}\rangle
-manifold for some
k^{\prime}\leq k
, and we can ask for
s|_{X(S)}
to be transverse along
K\cap X(S)
. It is possible for
s|_{X(S)}
not to be transverse along
K\cap X(S)
, even when
s
is transverse on
s^{-1}(0)
in
X
. We say that
s
is
strongly transverse
(along
K
) when
s|_{X(S)}
is strongly transverse (along
K\cap\cap X(S)
) for all
S\in 2^{[k]}
. Note that if
s|_{X(S)}
is strongly transverse, then
s
is strongly transverse on an open neighborhood of
X(S)
in
X
.

Submersions and immersions of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds

A submersion of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds is a stratum-preserving smooth map
f:M\to N
between
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds
M
and
N
for which the differential is surjective everywhere. More generally:

Definition 1
.

Given a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
M
of dimension
m
and a
\langle k^{\prime}\rangle
-manifold
N
of dimension
n
with
k^{\prime}\leq k
, a submersion from
M
to
N
is:

•

Let
\mathcal{P}\subset 2^{[k]}
,
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\subset 2^{[k^{\prime}]}
be the sub-posets of nonempty strata of
M
and
N
, respecitvely. We fix a subset
S_{f}\subset[k]
and a map of sets
\tilde{f}:[k]\setminus S_{f}\to[k^{\prime}]
such the formula
S\mapsto[k^{\prime}]\setminus\tilde{f}([k]\setminus(S\cup S_{f}))
defines a map of posets
\bar{f}:\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
with
|[k]\setminus(S\cup S_{f})|=|[k^{\prime}]\setminus\bar{f}(S)|
for all
S\in\mathcal{P}
; and furthermore,

•

we have a smooth map
f:M\to N
which is locally modelled on the map

\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k+|S|}=\mathbb{R}^{([k]\setminus S)\cap S_{f}}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus(S\cup S_{f})}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k+|S|}\to\mathbb{R}^{[k^{\prime}]\setminus\bar{f}(S)}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-k^{\prime}+\bar{f}(S)}

(9)

which is a restriction of the linear map
\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}

which is zero on
\mathbb{R}^{([k]\setminus S)\cap S_{f}}_{+}
(the “fiber corner directions”), and is otherwise the sum of a surjective map
\mathbb{R}^{n-k+|S|}\to\mathbb{R}^{m-k^{\prime}+\bar{f}(S)}
and an isomorphism
\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus(S\cup S_{f})}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{[k^{\prime}]\setminus\bar{f}(S)}_{+}
induced by applying
f
to the coordinate labels. The fibers of such a map are manifestly
\langle S_{f}\rangle
-manifolds.

Remark 4
.

We will only use the case where
S_{f}=\emptyset
and the case where
S_{f}=\{2,\ldots,k\}
and
k^{\prime}=1
in this paper, for which the definition simplifies drastically.

An immersion of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds from an
m
-dimension
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
M
to an
n
-dimensional
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
N
is a stratum-preserving smooth map from
M
to
N
locally modelled on the inclusions
\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
for
m<n
.

We now describe two natural operations on
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds.

2.2.3
The double of a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold

Just for this paragraph, let
M
be a topological
\langle 1\rangle
-manifold, i.e. a topological manifold with boundary. The
double

\mathcal{D}(M)
is the space
M\cup_{\partial M}M
; this is a topological manifold equipped with an inclusion
M\to\mathcal{D}
which is a homeomorpism onto its image.

Now let
M
be a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold. The group
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
acts on
\mathbb{R}^{k}
by flipping signs, and each
S\in 2^{[k]}
defines a subgroup
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{S}=(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{[k]\setminus S}
which preserves the subset
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}(S)\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
under this action, and does not preserve any
\mathbb{R}^{k}_{+}(T)
for
T\supsetneq S
. The double
\mathcal{D}(M)
of
M
is the union

\mathcal{D}(M):=\bigsqcup_{g\in(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}}gM/\sim

(10)

with the equivalence relation being that for each
S\in 2^{[k]}
, we identify

gx\sim g^{\prime}x
for
x\in M(S)
and
g,g^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{S}
. We have a canonical inclusion
\iota_{\mathcal{D}}:M\to\mathcal{D}
and a canonical action of
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
on
\mathcal{D}(M)
. The operation of doubling is a functor: given a map
f:M\to N
of topological
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds, there is a unique extension of
f
to a map of topological
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-spaces
\mathcal{D}f:\mathcal{D}(M)\to\mathcal{D}(N)
. By applying this functor to charts, one immediately concludes

Lemma 1
.

The space
\mathcal{D}(M)
is a topological
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-manifold. If
M
was a
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold then there is an action of
G
on
\mathcal{D}(M)
induced by acting separately on each piece of the union in (
10
) which makes
\mathcal{D}(M)
into a topological
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-manifold.
∎

2.2.4
Collaring of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds

Let us again assume for just this paragraph that
M
is a topological manifold with boundary. Then
Coll(M)=\partial M\times[0,1]\cup M/((x,1)\sim x)
is again a topological manifold with boundary, which is homeomorphic to
M
. Moreover, if
M
is smooth then
Coll(M)
is also canonically smooth and diffeomorphic to
M
.

A generalization of this construction exists for
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds
M
. As a space, we have

Coll(M)=\bigcup_{S\in 2^{[k]}}M(S)\times[0,1]^{[k]\setminus S}/\sim

(11)

with the equivalence relation is generated by the relations that if
(x,t)\in M(S)\times[0,1]^{[k]\setminus S}
and
(y,u)\in M(T)\times[0,1]^{[k\setminus T]}
, then
(x,t)~(y,u)
if
S<T
and
x=y
, and if
t|_{([n]\setminus S)\setminus([n]\setminus T)}=1
. One can use the charts on a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold to immediately check that

Lemma 2
.

The space
Coll(M)
is a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold with
Coll(M)(S)=M(S)\times 0
. If
M
was a topological
G
-
\langle k\rangle
-manifold then the
G
action on
Coll(M)
descending from the
G
-action on each component of the union in (
11
) via
g\cdot(x,t)=(gx,t)
, is continuous, making
Coll(M)
into a topological
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold.
∎

Given a map
f:M\to N
of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds, we get an associated map
Coll(f):Coll(M)\to Coll(N)
of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds, defined via

Coll(f)|_{M(S)\times[0,1]^{[k]\setminus S}}=f|_{M(S)}\times\text{id}.

(12)

We have the following useful

Lemma 3
.

If
M
is a smooth
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold, then
Coll(M)\setminus M
admits a canonical smooth structure and a smooth embedding of
G
-
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds
Coll(M)\setminus M\to M
that is canonical up to isotopy of embeddings. In particular,
\mathcal{D}(M)
is a smooth
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-manifold.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Proposition 4.1 and Theorem 4.2 of
[
AM11
]
. Indeed, the boundary faces of a
G
-
\langle k\rangle
manifold have a natural partition into
k

G
-invariant collective boundary hypersurfaces satisfying
[
AM11
, Equation 4.2]
. The doubling construction in that paper agrees with our description. The smooth embedding of
Coll(M)\setminus M
into
M
is exactly the data of the
G
-invariant product structure of Proposition 4.1 of
[
AM11
]
.
∎

As a corollary of the above result we see that if
f:M\to N
is a submersion of smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds then
\mathcal{D}(f):\mathcal{D}(M)\to\mathcal{D}(N)
is also a smooth submersion. Note however that in general,
Coll(f)
is not necessarily a smooth map when
f
was smooth.

2.2.5
Smoothing of
G
-
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds

Much of the technical content of this paper will involve the use of equivariant
G
-smoothing theory
[
Las79
]
in order to compatibly smooth topological
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds underlying the Kuranishi charts we produce. Before delving into smoothing theory, we note the following lemma, which will allow us to apply smoothing theory to the setting of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds.

Lemma 4
.

Let
M
be a topological
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold. Suppose that
\mathcal{D}(M)
is equipped with a smooth structure as a
(G\times\mathbb{Z}/2^{k})
-manifold. There is a unique smooth structure on
M
such that the inclusion
M\to\mathcal{D}(M)
when viewed through smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold charts on
M
and smooth charts on
\mathcal{D}(M)
.

Proof.

We must specify which topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold charts on
M
are smooth.

For any
x\in M\subset\mathcal{D}(M)
we can choose a chart
\psi^{-1}:V\to U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where
n=\dim M
, and such that
V
is fixed by the action of
(G\times\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{x}
,
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a
(G\times\mathbb{Z}/2^{k})_{x}
-representation, the map
\psi
is equivariant,
\psi^{-1}(x)=0
, and
U
is bounded. For any
S\in 2^{[k]}
, we have that
x\in M(S)
if and only if
(G\times\mathbb{Z}/2^{k})_{x}=G_{x}\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{S}\subset G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
for some subgroup
G_{x}\subset G
. Each of the
\mathbb{Z}/2
factors
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{S,j}\subset(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{S}
fixes a codimension
1
submanifold which we denote
\mathcal{D}(M)([k]\setminus\{j\})
, and the fixed loci all intersect transversely. From this we conclude that as a
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{S}
-representation, the codomain of
\psi
can be written (non-canonically) as
\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k+|S|}
where the second factor is a trivial
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{S}
representation and the first factor is the standard representation of
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{[k]\setminus S}
by flipping signs. Moreover, by composing with the action of
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
on
\mathcal{D}(M)
we can assume that
\psi^{-1}(V\cap M)\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}^{[k]\setminus S}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k+|S|}
.
We declare that the restrictions of the inverses of such charts to
U\cap\mathbb{R}_{+}^{[k]\setminus S}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-k+|S|}
, which have image
M\cap V
, are smooth charts on
M
. Now, the transition maps between any pair of such charts on
M
are smooth, since they are the compositions of coordinate permutations and smooth maps arising from transition functions of charts on
\mathcal{D}(M)
. This gives
M
the structure of a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold. To see that
G
action with respect to this smooth structure is smooth, we use the same construction to build smooth charts on the
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
G\times M
from the smooth charts on
G\times\mathcal{D}(M)
to in order to verify that the map
G\times M\to M
is smooth.
∎

In particular, by Lemma
3
and Lemma
4
we see that an equivariant smoothing of a
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold
X
is
equivalent
to an equivariant smoothing of its double.

2.2.6
Products, Induction, Restriction

Finally, we describe three natural operations on
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds which will be used to relate different such manifolds thorougout this work.

Product.

Given a
G_{1}-\langle k_{1}\rangle
-manifold
M_{1}
and a
G_{2}-\langle k_{2}\rangle
-manifold
M_{2}
, the space
M_{1}\times M_{2}
admits the structure of a
(G_{1}\times G_{2})-\langle k_{1}+k_{2}\rangle
-manifold by using the product action and the product charts. If
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
were smooth, then
M_{1}\times M_{2}
is manifestly smooth as well.

Induction.

We will assume now that
G
is a compact Lie group. Given a closed Lie subgroup
H\subset G
and an
H-\langle k\rangle
-manifold
M
, we have an associated
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold with underlying stratified space

Ind_{H}^{G}M=G\times_{H}M,(Ind_{H}^{G}M)(S)=G\times_{H}M(S)\text{ for }S\in 2^{[k]}.

(13)

This is a smooth
G-\langle k\rangle
manifold if
M
was smooth. We say that any
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold that is homeomorphic/diffeomorphic to
Ind_{H}^{G}M
as a
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold is an
induction
(to
G
along
H\subset G
) of
M
.

Restriction.

Given a
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold
N
, we will say that an
H-\langle k\rangle
-manifold
M
is a
restriction
of
M
if
M
is an induction of
N
(along an inclusion
H\subset G
. A criterion for checking that
N
is a restriction of
M
is

Lemma 5
.

With notation above, if there exists an immersion of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds
N\to M
such that any point of
M
can be translated to a point in the image of
N
via
G
, and such that the subgroup
H_{n}\subset G
sending
n\in N
into
N
is independent of
n
, then
N
is a restriction of
M
.

Proof.

Write
H_{n}=H
for this subgroup of
G
, which must also be the subgroup of
G
preserving the image of
N
;
H
is thus a closed Lie subgroup. We have a smooth
G
-equivariant map
G\times_{H}N\to M
given by
(g,n)\mapsto gn
. The condition in the lemma makes this map surjective. This map is injective since if
g_{1}n_{1}=g_{2}n_{2}
, then
n_{1}=g_{1}^{-1}g_{2}n_{2}
, and thus
g_{1}^{-1}g_{2}\in H
and so
(g_{1},n_{1})\sim(g_{1}g_{1}^{-1}g_{2},n_{2})=(g_{2},n_{2})
.
∎

Moreover, induction is manifestly functional: given an
H
-equivariant map between
H
-spaces
f:M_{1}\to M_{2}
, we get an associated map between
G
-spaces
Ind_{H}^{G}:Ind_{H}^{G}M_{1}\to Ind_{H}^{G}M_{2}
. In particular, an equivariant section of a vector bundle induces to an equivariant section on the induced vector bundle.

Lemma 6
.

Let
V
be an
G
-representation,
H
a closed subgroup of
G
, and
N
an
H-\langle k\rangle
-manifold. Then, viewing
V
as a
G
-space via the restriction, we have that
Ind_{H}^{G}(N\times V)
is canonically isomorphic as a
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold to
(Ind_{H}^{G})\times V
.

Proof.

One checks that the canonical map
G\times_{H}(N\times V)\to(G\times_{H}N)\times V
, which on equivalence classes is given by
(g,n,v)\mapsto(g,n,v)
, is a bijection.
∎

Generalized induction.

Given a submanifold
V\subset G
containing a subgroup
H
and preserved by
H
, we have a functorial operation from
H-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds to
H-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds given by
X\mapsto Ind_{H}^{G,V}X:=V\times_{H}X
. We call this procedure
generalized induction
.

Smooth structures.

Lemma 7
.

Let
M
be an induction of
N
. There is a bijection between smooth structures on
M
and smooth structures on
N
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3
and Lemma
4
we can assume that
M
and
N
are manifolds.
We can assume without loss of generality that
M=Ind_{H}^{G}N
. Given a smooth structure on
N
, it is clear that
Ind_{H}^{G}N
inherits a canonical smooth structure. We argue that this map on smooth structures has an inverse. Note that for
x\in N
, the stabilizer of the image of
x
in
Ind_{H}^{G}N
is just the stabilizer of
x
in
N
, which we denote by
H_{x}
. The slice theorem for smooth group actions says that an open neighborhood of
G\cdot x
is difeomorphic to the total space of a
H_{x}
-equivariant disk bundle over
G\cdot x\simeq G/H_{x}
; the disk is a disk inside the normal in
T_{x}Ind_{H}^{G}N
to the submanifold
G\cdot x
. But an open neighborhood of
H\cdot x\subset N\subset Ind_{H}^{G}N
is then just the restriction of this principal bundle to
H/H_{x}\subset G/H_{x}
, and so in particular
N\subset Ind_{H}^{G}N
is automatically a smooth submanifold.
∎

Lemma 8
.

Let
E
be a topological
G
-vector bundle over a topological
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold
M
. A choice of smooth structure on
M
induces a choice of smooth structure on
E
that is canonical up to contractible choice.

Proof.

This follows by applying the doubling construction to
E
and
M
, using the corresponding bijection between isomorphism classes of topological and smooth equivariant bundles over a smooth
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-manifold
[
Was69
]
, and restricting the resulting transition functions.
∎

2.3
Kuranishi charts and derived orbifolds with corners.

Definition 2
.

let
X
be a space stratified by
2^{[k]}
. A
topological Kuranishi chart
is a quadruple
\mathcal{K}=(G,T,V,\sigma)
, where
G
is a compact Lie group,
T
is a topological
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold where
G
has finite stabilizers,
V
is a
G
-vector bundle over
T
, and
\sigma
is a
G
-equivariant section of
V
. We say that
\mathcal{K}
is a Kuranishi chart
on X
when a
footprint homeomorphism

\phi:X\simeq\sigma^{-1}(0)/G
is specified, where
\phi
should be a map of stratified spaces.

We say that a topological Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}
is smooth when the thickening
T
is a smooth
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold and
V
is a smooth vector bundle over
T
. A smooth Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}
is
regular
among a subset
C\subset\sigma^{-1}(0)/G
when
\sigma
is strongly transverse along the preimage of
C
in
T
. Such a chart is
regular
whenever it is regular on
\sigma^{-1}(0)/G
.

Given a smooth Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}=(G,T,V,\sigma)
, if the thickening
T
is a
\langle 0\rangle
-manifold then we get an associated
derived orbifold chart

([T/G],[V/G],[\sigma/G])

[
BX22
, Definition 1.3]
. In the setting of Floer homology we must introduce an appropriate generalization of the language of derived orbifolds to include the case where the thickening can be a convenient kind of manifold with corners.

Let
X
be a Hausdorff and second-countable topological space stratified by
\{0<\ldots<k\}
.

Definition 3
.

An (effective)
n
-dimensional orbifold chart with codimension
\ell
-corners
is a triple
C=(U,\psi,\Gamma)
where

•

U\subset\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}
is an open neighborhood of
0
,

•

\Gamma
acts effectively and linearly on
\mathbb{R}^{n}\supset\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}
, with the action a direct sum of a nontrivial action on
\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}
with the trivial action on
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
;

•

U
is
\Gamma
-invariant with respect to this action;

•

the
footprint
map
\psi:U\to X
is a
\Gamma
-invariant continuous map such that the induced map
\bar{\psi}:U/\Gamma\to X
is a homeomorphism onto an open subset
V\subset X
, and

\bar{\psi}^{-1}(X(i))=U\cap((\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+})(i)\times\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}).

(14)

Given
C=(U,\psi,\Gamma)
and
C^{\prime}=(U^{\prime},\psi^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime})
a pair of
n
-dimensional orbifold charts with codimension
\ell
and
\ell^{\prime}
corners, respectively, a chart embedding is a smooth open embedding
\iota:U\to U^{\prime}
such that
\psi^{\prime}\circ\iota=\psi
. We will drop “with codimension
\ell
” when some
\ell
is implicit. (To avoid linguistic confusion, note that that the inclusion
\mathbb{R}_{+}\subset\mathbb{R}
is
not
an open embedding, but the inclusion
(1,2)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}

is
an open embedding. Also note that smooth open embeddings will send points in codimension
i
strata to points in codimension
i
strata necessarily.)
This automatically defines an equivariant injection of groups
\iota:\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
.

WIth these definitions, we can proceed as usual. A chart is centered at
x\in X
if
0\in U
and
x=\bar{\psi}(0)
; two charts are compatible if there are charts centered at every point in the intersections of their footprints which each admit a chart embedding into both charts. An orbifold atlas with codimension
k
corners is a collection of orbifold charts on
X
with corners of codimension at most
k
which are mutually compatible, and a pair of orbifold atlases with codimension
k
corners are equivalent if they admit a common refinement. Thus
X
as above with an equivalence class of such atlases is an effective orbifold with codimension
k
-corners.

Continuous functions
f:X\to\mathbb{R}
are considered smooth when their pullbacks to all charts as in Definition
3
are smooth; orbifold vector bundles (orbibundles) and their sections are defined identically to
[
BX22
, Definition 3.3]
.

There is a natural refinement of an effective orbifold with codimension
k
corners to the notion of an effective
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold. We use notation as in Section
2.2.2
.

Definition 4
.

Let
X
be a space stratified by
2^{[k]}
. We can view
X
as a space stratified by
\{0<\ldots<k\}
. A
\langle k\rangle
-chart

(U,\Gamma,\psi)
where
\psi
is a map

\psi:\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-|k\setminus S|}\supset U\to V\subset X

(15)

such that,
v, and we say that an orbifold chart with codimension
\ell
corners
C=
on
X
is a if, writing
\bar{\psi}(U)=V
, we have

•

Viewing the domain of
\psi
as
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}
(via, say, the order-preserving bijection
[k]\setminus S\to[\ell]
) we have that
(U,\Gamma,\psi)
is an
n
-dimensional orbifold chart with codimension
\ell
corners. Writing
\bar{\psi}:U/\Gamma\to X
as in Definition
3
, it also holds that

•

for
y\in V
, we have that
S(y)\geq S(x)
, and moreover

•

For
[k]\supset T\supset S
, have that
\bar{\psi}^{-1}(X(T))\subset\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}_{+}(r_{S}(T))\times\mathbb{R}^{n-|k\setminus S|}
.

An embedding of
\langle k\rangle
-charts is just an embedding of the underlying orbifold charts with corners, and thus the notion of compatiblity of
\langle k\rangle
-charts continues to make sense. We have

Definition 5
.

An
(effective)
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold
is a space
X
stratified by
2^{[k]}
which is equipped with an orbifold
\langle k\rangle
-atlas, i.e. a maximal collection of compatible
\langle k\rangle
-charts. We will denote a
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold with underlying space
X
by
X
when the orbifold
\langle k\rangle
-atlas is clear from the context.

A smooth function, orbibundle, smooth section, etc. on a
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold are the same as those on the underlying orbifold with corners.

The formula for doubling a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold makes sense for
any
space stratified by
2^{[k]}
, and by functoriality of doubling, the double
\mathcal{D}(X)
of the underlying space of an effective
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold is covered by charts
\mathcal{D}(\psi):\mathcal{D}(U)\to\mathcal{D}(X)
: if
C=(U,\Gamma,\psi)
was a
\langle k\rangle
-chart with codimension
\ell
-corners, then
U
is an
\langle\ell\rangle
-manifold, and
\mathcal{D}(\psi)
is
\Gamma\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{\ell}
-equivariant. We call any such orbifold chart on
\mathcal{D}(X)
produced by this procedure
doubled chart
. Since the double of an open embedding of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds is a smooth open embedding (by Lemma
3
the doubled charts are mutually compatible, and so
\mathcal{D}(X)
the underlying space of an effective orbifold, also denoted
\mathcal{D}(X)
, by declaring the doubled charts to be smooth. Moreover, by composing the doubled charts with the quotient map
\mathcal{D}(X)\to X
, we get new charts
(\mathcal{D}(U),\Gamma\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{S(\psi(0))},\mathcal{D}(\psi))
on
X
(where
\mathcal{D}(U)\subset\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-|k\setminus S|}
) which are also mutually compatible. These make make
X
into the underlying space of another effective orbifold, which we will denote by
\mathcal{D}(X)/(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
.

Producing
\langle k\rangle
-orbifolds from global quotients.

Let
T
be a smooth
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold with corners where the
G
-action has finite stabilizers with the stabilizer of a generic point being empty.

Lemma 9
.

The space
T/G
is equipped with a canonical structure of an effective
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold, which we denote
[T/G]
.

Proof.

We only explain the construction of the charts. Choose
x\in T\subset\mathcal{D}(T)
, and use the slice theorem for the latter manifold acted on by
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
. Write
S=S(x)
. We have that
Stab(x):=(G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k})_{x}=G_{x}\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)_{S}^{k}\subset G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
. The slice theorem then shows that
x
has a
G
-invariant neighborhood
V
which is
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)_{S}^{k}
-equivariantly isomorphic to
G\times_{G_{x}}D
, where the latter is a disk in the normal to
V=T_{x}G\cdot x
; here
(\mathbb{Z}/2)_{S}^{k}
acts on
D
via its action on the normal space. In particular there is a
Stab(x)
-invariant map
\psi^{-1}
from a neighborhood
V
of
x
to
U\subset Lie(G)\times\mathcal{R}
, where
Lie(G)
is the lie algebra of
G
thought of as a trivial
G_{x}\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)_{S}^{k}
-representation, while
\mathcal{R}
is a nontrivial such representation. As in Lemma
4
we can write
\mathcal{R}
as
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}
where the second factor is a trivial
(\mathbb{Z}/2)_{S}^{k}\simeq(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{\ell}
representation but a nontrivial
G_{x}
-representation, while in the first factor the
\mathbb{Z}/2
factors of
(\mathbb{Z}/2)_{S}^{k}
act by flipping sings while
G_{x}
acts trivially. Composing such
\psi
with an appropriate permutation of the last
\ell
coordinates to produce
\psi^{\prime}
, we see that
(G_{x},(\psi)^{-1}(D)\cap\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell},\psi^{\prime})
is a
\langle k\rangle
-chart on
T/G
. We declare such charts to be smooth.
∎

From the above construction of charts, we conclude

Lemma 10
.

Let
T^{\prime}
be the induction of
T
along
G\subset G^{\prime}
. Then
[T^{\prime}/G^{\prime}]
and
[T/G]
define canonically isomorphic orbifolds.
∎

If
V
is a
G
-vector bundle over
T
, then there is an associated orbibundle denoted
[V/G]
. Moreover, by applying the induction functor to charts, we see that the the induction
V^{\prime}=Ind_{G}^{G^{\prime}}V
to a vector bundle over
T^{\prime}
defines the an isomorphic vector bundle
[V^{\prime}/G^{\prime}]
over
[T^{\prime}/G]\simeq[T/G]
. Finally, the construction of charts above shows (by restricting
s
to
1\times D\cap\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}\subset G\times_{G_{x}}D
) that given a
G
-equivariant section
s:T\to V
, there is an induced section
s:[T/G]\to[V/G]
with underlying map
s/G:T/G\to V/G
induced by the quotient construction on spaces, which is smooth if
s
was smooth.

Derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifolds and global Kuranishi charts.

Given a smooth Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}=(G,T,V,\sigma)
, we can form a triple
\mathcal{K}//G=([T/G],[V/G],[\sigma/G])
, where
[T/G]
is an effective
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold,
[V/G]
is the orbibundle over
[T/G]
corresponding to
V
, and
[\sigma/G]
is the continuous section
[T/G]\to[V/G]
induced from
\sigma
. We call such a triple a
derived orbifold chart
, and we say that
\mathcal{K}//G
is the derived orbifold chart
associated
to
\mathcal{K}
. We write
\text{vdim }(T,V,\sigma)=\dim V-\dim T
for the expected dimension of
\sigma^{-1}(0)
. We say that a pair of derived orbifold charts
(\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{1},\sigma_{1})
and
(\mathcal{T}_{2},\mathcal{V}_{2},\sigma_{2})
are
equivalent
when there is a diffeomorphism
\Phi:U_{1}\to U_{2}
between open neighborhoods
U_{i}\supset\sigma_{i}^{-1}(0)
along with an isomorphism of orbibundles
\bar{\Phi}:\mathcal{V}_{1}\simeq\Phi^{*}\mathcal{V}_{2}
such that

\sigma_{2}\circ\Phi=\bar{\Phi}\circ\sigma_{1}
. There is an obvious notion of the product of a pair of derived orbifold charts.

Lemma 11
.

Let
\mathcal{K}_{1}=(H,T,V,\sigma)
and let
\mathcal{K}_{2}=Ind_{H}^{G}\mathcal{K}_{1}
. Then
\mathcal{K}_{2}//G
is equivalent to
\mathcal{K}_{1}//H
. Similarly, given
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}=(H^{\prime},T^{\prime},V^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
, we have that

\mathcal{K}\times\mathcal{K}^{\prime}//(H\times H^{\prime})
is equivalent to
\mathcal{K}_{1}//H\times\mathcal{K}_{2}//H^{\prime}
.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Lemma
10
and the subsequent discussion about induction of vector bundles and smooth sections of orbibundles induced from equivariant sections.
∎

A
diffeomorphism
of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifolds
F:(\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{1},\sigma_{1})\to(\mathcal{T}_{2},\mathcal{V}_{2},\sigma_{2})
is a pair of diffeomorphisms
(f,g)
, with
f:\mathcal{T}_{1}\to\mathcal{T}_{2}
and
g:\mathcal{V}_{1}\to\mathcal{V}_{2}
, such that
\pi_{2}g=f\pi_{1}
,
g\iota_{1}=\iota_{2}f
, and
g\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}g
, where
\pi_{i}
is the projection from
\mathcal{V}_{i}
to
\mathcal{T}_{i}
, while
\iota_{i}
is the inclusion of the zero section.

Normally complex structures

Fix a
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold
X
equipped an orbibundle
V
and a distinguished compact subset
K\subset X
. We can view
X
as the as a smooth orbifold
\mathcal{D}(X)/(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
via the doubling construction;
V
then becomes an orbibundle
\mathcal{D}(V)/(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
over this orbifold.

We say that a straightened metric on
X
is a straightened metric on
\mathcal{D}(X)/(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
; given a straightened metric on
X
, a straightened connection on
\mathcal{V}
is a straightened connection on
\mathcal{D}{V}/(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
with respect to this metric; and a straightened structure on
(X,V)
is a straightened structure on
(\mathcal{D}(X)/(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k},\mathcal{D}(V)/(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k})
near
K
.
In particular
, a straightened metric on
X
, resp. a straightened connection on
\mathcal{V}
, is a metric on
X
, resp. a connection on
\mathcal{V}
, satisfying a certain condition. We will say that a straightened structure on
(X,V)

extends
the straightened metric on
X
.

The
[
BX22
, Lemma 3.15]
establishes that every
(X,V)
has a straightned structure near a compact set
K\subset X
. One immediately sees that there is another tautological method for constructing straightened structures from global charts:

Lemma 12
.

Let
N
be a
G
-
\langle k\rangle
manifold and suppose that we have chosen a straightened structure on
[N/G]
. Let
\tilde{V}
be a
G
-representation. Then the trivial connection on the bundle
\tilde{V}\times N
over
N
induces a straightened structure on
([N/G],[(\tilde{V}\times N])
.
∎

As for straightened structures on orbifolds, given a straightened structure on
X
which extends to a straightened structure on
(X,V)
and to a straightened structure on
(X,W)
we have an associated straightened structure on
(X,V\oplus W)
.

We can use a straightened structure on
(X,V)
define a
normal complex structure
on
V
and
X
. As in the theory of orbifolds, every
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold chart
(U,\Gamma,\psi)
extends to a
bundle chart

(U,E,\Gamma,\hat{\psi})
. Here,
E=E_{0}\times U
is a trivial
\Gamma
-equivariant vector bundle; writing
\pi_{V}:V\to X
and
\pi_{E}:E\to U
for the projections, we have that
\hat{\psi}
is
\Gamma
-equivariant-map
E\to V
such that
\pi_{V}\hat{\psi}=\psi\pi_{E}
. In particular, in each doubled bundle chart
(U,E,\Gamma,\hat{\psi})
on
(\mathcal{D}(X)/(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k},\mathcal{D}(V)/(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k})
, the straightened structure defines a metric
g_{U}
on
U
and a connection
\nabla_{U}
on
E
such that

•

For each
G\subset\Gamma
, we have the exponential map
N_{\epsilon}U^{G}\to U
from the
\epsilon
-disk bundle of the normal bundle of
U^{G}
, such in a neighborhood of
U^{G}
, the metric
g
agrees with the pushforward under the exponential map of the bundle metric on
NU^{G}
induced by using the Levi-Civita connection and the restriction of the metric to
NU^{G}
under the canonical equivariant orthogonal splitting
TU|_{U^{G}}\simeq TU^{G}\oplus NU^{G}
into trivial and nontrivial
G
-representations; and

•

We have a connection
\nabla^{E}
on
E
induced from the straightened structure. Identifying this neighborhood of
U^{G}
with
N_{\epsilon}U^{G}
, writing
\rho:(\pi_{E}|_{U^{G}})^{*}E\to E|_{N_{\epsilon}U^{G}}
for the vector bundle isomorphism induced by parallel transport along normal geodesics, we have that that the pullback of
\nabla^{E}
along
\rho
agrees with the pullback of
\nabla^{E}|_{U^{G}}
along
(\pi_{E}|_{U^{G}})^{*}
.

Now, in every doubled bundle chart
(U,E,\Gamma,\hat{\psi})
, we have that
\Gamma\simeq\Gamma^{\prime}\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{S}
for some
S\in 2^{[k]}
and some finite group
\Gamma^{\prime}
. This doubled chart is the double of the
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold bundle chart
(V_{+},E_{+},\hat{\psi}|_{E_{+}})
with
E_{+}=E|_{U^{\prime}}
and
U_{+}=U\cap\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}
,
\ell=k-|S|
, and
\Gamma^{\prime}
acting trivially on the last
\ell
coordinates and
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{S}
flipping their signs. Let us consider
G\subset\Gamma
for the form
G=G_{+}\times 1
and see what data the straightened structure on
\mathcal{D}(X)/(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
induces on
(V_{+},E_{+},\hat{\psi}|_{E_{+}})
. Since
U_{+}^{G_{+}}\simeq U\cap(\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell})^{G}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}
, we have that
NU_{+}^{G_{+}}=NU^{G}|_{U_{+}^{G_{+}}}
, which the straightened structure on the doubled chart equips with a metric. Moreover, by the
(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}_{S}
-equivariance of the metric, each boundary hypersurface of
U_{+}
is totally geodesic in
U
, so the restriction of the exponential map to
N_{\epsilon}U_{+}^{G_{+}}
has image in
U_{+}
. Thus the pushforward of the metric by the exponential map agrees with the restriction of the metric to
U_{+}
near
U_{+}^{G_{+}}
. Similarly, the connection
\nabla^{E}
restricts to a connection
\nabla^{E_{+}}
, and since normal geodesics stay in
U_{+}
, we have that the pullback of this metric along
\rho
restricted to
(\pi_{E_{+}|_{U_{+}^{G_{+}}}})^{*}E_{+}
agrees with the pullback of
\nabla^{E_{+}}
along
(\pi_{E_{+}|_{U_{+}^{G_{+}}}})^{*}
.

We now drop the pluses from our notation and work with the
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold instead of its double. In each orbifold bundle
\langle k\rangle
-chart, we have identifications of
N_{\epsilon}U^{G}
with a neighborhood of
U^{G}
, and over this neighborhood the canonical splitting of
E|_{U^{G}}\simeq\mathring{E}^{G}\oplus\check{E}^{G}
into isotrivial and nonisotrivial
G
-representations extends to a
G
-equivariant splitting of
E|_{N_{\epsilon}U^{G}}

which we will denote using the same notation
. As in
[
BX22
]
, a normal almost complex structure on
X
is an almost
H
-equivariant complex structure
I_{H}:NU_{H}\to NU_{H}
for each
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold chart
(\Gamma,U,\psi)
and every subgroup
H\subset\Gamma
such that

•

for every
y\in U
and every pair of subgroups
H\subset G\subset\Gamma_{y}
(where we write
\Gamma_{y}
for the stabilizer of
y
in
\Gamma
),
(I_{G})_{y}
restricts to
(I_{H})_{y}
under the decomposition

N_{y}U^{G}\simeq(T_{y}U^{H}\cap N_{y}U^{G})\oplus N_{y}U^{H},

(16)

and such that

•

For any chart embedding
\iota:V\to U
between charts
(V,\Pi,\psi_{V})
and
(U,\Gamma,\psi_{U})
, one has that the induced map
N_{y}V^{\Pi_{y}}\simeq N_{\iota}(y)U^{\Gamma_{\iota(y)}}
is complex linear for each
y\in U
.

Similarly a normal almost complex structure on an orbibundle
V\to X
is, for every bundle chart
(U,E,\Gamma,\hat{\psi})
and every subgroup
G\subset\Gamma
, the data of a complex structure
J_{G}
on
\check{E}^{G}\to U^{G}
for each bundle chart
(U,E,\Gamma,\hat{\psi})
and each subgroup
G\subset\Gamma
, such that

•

for every
y\in U
and every pair of subgroups
H\subset G\subset\Gamma_{y}
,
(J_{G})_{y}
restricts to
(J_{H})_{y}
under the decomposition

\check{E}^{G}_{y}\simeq(\check{E}^{G}_{y})^{H}\oplus\check{E}^{H}_{y},

(17)

and such that

•

Under any equivariant bundle chart embedding
\hat{\iota}:F\to E
from
(V,F,\Pi,\hat{\psi}_{V})
to
(U,E,\Gamma,\hat{\psi}_{U})
covering
\iota:V\to U
, we have that the induced map
\hat{\iota}:\check{F}^{\Pi_{y}}\to\check{E}^{\Gamma_{\iota(y)}}
is complex linear for each
y\in V
.

Given a diffeomorphism of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifolds
F:(X_{1},V_{1})\to(X_{2},V_{2})
,
F=(f,g)
, we can ask
F
to preserve certain structures, namely:

•

If
(X_{1},V_{1})
and
(X_{2},V_{2})
have straightened structures, we can ask for
F
to be a
straightened diffeomorphism
, namely that
f
is an isometry with respect to the straightened metrics, while
g^{*}\nabla^{V_{2}}=\nabla^{V_{1}}
where
\nabla^{V_{i}}
is the straightened connection on
V_{i}
. (Note that in general the pullback
g^{*}\nabla^{V_{2}}
is only an Ehresmann connection on
V_{1}
rather than a connection on this vector bundle, so this is a rather stringent condition;)

•

If
F
is a straightened diffeomorphism and
(X_{i},V_{i})
have fixed normal complex structures, we can ask for
F
to be a
normally complex diffeomorphism
, namely that
f
is normally complex and
g
is normally complex. These, mean, respectively:

•

We say that
f
is normally complex if for every
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold chart
(U_{1},\Gamma,\psi_{1})
on
X_{1}
, we have a
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold chart
(U_{2},\Gamma,\psi)
with
\tilde{f}:U_{1}\to U_{2}
a
\Gamma
-equivariant map defining
f|_{U_{1}/\Gamma}
,
then since
f
is an isometry, we can identify
\tilde{f}
with the restriction of linear bundle map
\tilde{f}^{\prime}:NU_{1}^{\Gamma}\to NU_{2}^{\Gamma}
, and
we require
that
\tilde{f^{\prime}}
is complex linear,

•

We say that
g
is normally complex if for bundle charts
(U_{1},E_{1},\Gamma,\hat{\psi}_{1})
and
(U_{2},E_{2},\Gamma,\hat{\psi}_{2})
covering the
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts above, with
\tilde{g}:E\to E^{\prime}
a
\Gamma
-equivariant bundle map
g
(which is a fiberwise diffeomorphism)
we require
that with respect to the decompositions
E_{i}|_{U_{i}^{\Gamma}}\simeq\mathring{E}_{i}\oplus\check{E}_{i}
, we can write
\tilde{g}(\mathring{e}_{1},\check{e}_{2})=(\mathring{g}(\mathring{e}_{1}),\check{g}(\check{e}_{2}))
, with
\check{g}
an
I_{H}
-biholomorpic map.

Normally complex sections

Given two complex representations of a finite group
\Gamma
, let
V,W
, let
Poly_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)

be
\Gamma
-equivariant polynomial maps from
V
to
W
of degree at most
d
, and let
ev:V\times Poly_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)\to W
be the evaluation map. Given two
\Gamma
-equivariant complex vector bundles
V,W
over a space
U
with a trivial
\Gamma
-action, let
Poly_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
be complex vector bundle with fiber
Poly_{d}^{\Gamma}(V_{x},W_{x})
at
x\in U
, and let
ev:V\oplus Poly_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
be the fiberwise evaluation map. The inverse images of
0
under
ev
are denoted by
Z^{\Gamma}_{d}(V,W)
in each case, and they each admit
canonical Whitney stratifications
according to
[
BX22
]
.

Given a bundle
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold chart
(U,E,\Gamma,\hat{\psi})
, a smooth
\Gamma
-equivariant section
s:U\to E
, and a point
x\in U
, we write
s
in a neighborhood of
x
as

s=(\mathring{s}_{x},\check{s}_{x}):N_{\epsilon}U^{G_{x}}\to\mathring{E}^{G_{x}}\oplus\check{E}^{G_{x}}\simeq E|_{N_{\epsilon}U^{G_{x}}}

(18)

and call a normally complex lift of
s
of degree at most
d
near
x
is a
G_{x}
-equivariant smooth bundle map over
U^{G_{x}}

\begin{gathered}\mathfrak{s}_{x}:N_{\epsilon}U^{G_{x}}\to Poly_{d}^{G_{x}}(NU^{G_{x}},\check{E}^{G_{x}})\\
\text{ inducing }\text{graph}(\mathfrak{s}_{x}):=(id,\mathfrak{s}_{x}):N_{\epsilon}U^{G_{x}}\to NU^{G_{x}}\oplus Poly_{d}^{G_{x}}(NU^{G_{x}},\check{E}^{G_{x}})\end{gathered}

(19)

such that

\check{s}_{x}=ev\circ\text{graph}(\mathfrak{s}_{x}).

(20)

We say that
s
is a normally complex section, or an FOP section, if it has a normally complex lift near every
x\in U
. We say that a smooth section
s:X\to V
is an FOP section if its pullback to every
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold bundle chart is an FOP section.

Remark 5
.

If
s
admits a normally complex lift at
0\in U
then it admits a normally complex lift for all
x
in a neighborhood of
0
, via the decompositions (
16
,
17
).

An FOP section
s:X\to V
is
strongly transverse
at
p\in X
if there is a
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold bundle chart
(U,E,\Gamma,\hat{\psi})
such that, writing

\mathfrak{s}:N_{\epsilon}U^{\Gamma}\to Poly^{\Gamma}_{d}(NU^{\Gamma},\check{E}^{\Gamma})

(21)

for the normally concrete lift of
s_{V}:U\to E
near
0
, we have that the section

\begin{gathered}\tilde{S}:N_{\epsilon}U^{\Gamma}\to\mathring{E}^{\Gamma}\oplus NU^{\Gamma}\oplus Poly^{\Gamma}_{d}(NU^{\Gamma},\check{E}^{\Gamma})\\
\tilde{S}(x)=(\mathring{s}_{0}(x),x,\mathfrak{s}(x))\end{gathered}

(22)

is transverse to
\{0\}\oplus Z^{\Gamma}_{d}(NU^{\Gamma},\check{E}^{\Gamma})
with respect to the canonical Whitney stratification at the point
0\in U
.

Note that for any
S\in 2^{[k]}
, the subset
X(S)
is a
\langle|S|\rangle
-orbifold. The orbibundle
V\to X
restricts to an orbibundle
V(S)\to X(S)
, and straightened structures and normally complex structures on
(X,V)
naturally restrict to the same on
(X,S),V(S)
. By restricting normally complex lifts we see that the restriction of an FOP section
s
to
X(S)
is FOP.

Definition 6
.

An FOP section
s:X\to V
is
strongly
\langle k\rangle
-transverse
if
s|_{X(S)}
is strongly transverse for each
s\in 2^{[k]}.

We can now prove the fundamental extension lemma:

Lemma 13
.

Suppose
V\to X
is an orbibundle over a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold, with
(X,V)
equipped with a straightened structure and with normally complex structures. Let
s:X\to V
be a continuous section with
s^{-1}(0)
compact, and such that such that
s|_{X([k]\setminus\{j\})}
is smooth, FOP, and strongly transverse for each
j=0,\ldots,k
. Let
D\subset X
be a precompact open neighborhood of
s^{-1}(0)
. Then there exists a section
s^{\prime}:X\to V
which is FOP near
\overline{D}
, which agrees with
s
on
X([k]\setminus\{j\})
for each
j
, and which is arbitrarily
C^{0}
-close to
s
on
D
.

Proof.

Write
\partial X=\cup_{j=0}^{k}X([k]\setminus\{j\}
for the boundary of the
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold.
It suffices to find any FOP extension of
s|_{\partial X}
to
D
. Indeed, the proof of
[
BX22
, Lemma 6.2]
goes through verbatim to our setting, so given such an extension
s^{\prime}
it is necessarily strongly
\langle k\rangle
-transverse in a neighborhood of
\partial X\cap(s^{\prime})^{-1}(0)
. Away from this neighborhood of the boundary we are in the the setting of orbifolds, and we can apply
[
BX22
, Theorem 6.3]
.

Let
(U,E,\Gamma,\hat{\psi})
be a
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold bundle chart at
x\in X
. We will show how to extend
s
from
\partial U
to an FOP section on
U
. Now, we can construct smooth partitions of unity subordinate to any collection of
\langle k\rangle
-charts on
X
by doubling the orbifold and applying the existence of partitions of unity for orbifolds
[
ChenRuan
, Lemma 4.2.1]
. Moreover, FOP sections are a module over smooth functions (this is an elementary generalization of the elementary
[
BX22
, Lemma 3.29]
to our setting), so this extension theorem for a single chart suffices. Assume that that
|S(x)|=\ell
, so
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
, and think of
U
as an
\langle\ell\rangle
-manifold via
U(T)=\mathbb{R}^{n-k}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}(T)
for
T\in 2^{[\ell]}
. Shrink
U
such that the (
\Gamma
-equivariant!) projections

\pi_{T}:U\to U(T),T\in 2^{[\ell]},\text{ defined by setting the coordinates of $\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}$ not in $T$ to zero,}

(23)

is well defined.
On these charts, one can extend
s|_{\partial U}
to an FOP section on
U
by applying the the following lemma to
\mathring{s}_{0}
and to
\mathfrak{s}_{0}
separately:

Lemma 14
.

Let
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}
be a neighborhood of zero, with
\Gamma
a finite group acting linearly on
\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}
and on a vector space
V
, and acting trivially on
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}
. Write
\partial U=\cup_{j=0}^{\ell}U([\ell]\setminus\{j\}
, and let
s:\partial U\to E_{0}
be a
\Gamma
-equivariant continuous map such that
s|_{U([\ell]\setminus\{j\}}
is smooth for each
j
. Suppose that the projection maps (
23
) are well defined. Then
s
admits a canonical smooth equivariant extension to
U
.

Proof.

Write
s^{T}=s|_{U(T)}
. If we had that
s^{T}=0
for each
T\in 2^{[\ell]}
with
|T|\leq\ell-2
then

s=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}s_{[\ell]\setminus\{j\}}\circ\pi_{[\ell]\setminus\{j\}}

(24)

would be an extension of the desired form. We inductively modify
s
until it is of this form. For the initial step of the induction, set
s_{0}=s-s^{\emptyset}\circ\pi_{\emptyset}
; the second term is a smooth function on
U
. For each
j=1,\ldots,\ell
, we assume that
s_{j-1}^{T}=0
for
T\in 2^{[\ell]}
,
|T|=j-1
. We set

s_{j}=s_{j-1}-\sum_{T\in 2^{[\ell]},|T|=j}s^{T}_{j-1}\circ\pi_{T}.

(25)

Each of the latter is a smooth function on
U
and and now we can continue with the induction.

Thus combining the formulae above we will have written a smooth
\Gamma
-equivariant extension of
s
.
∎

∎

Definition 7
.

Let
V\to X
be an orbibundle over a
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold such that
(X,V)
are equipped with normal complex structures. We say that
(X,V)
is
oriented
if for every
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold bundle chart
(U,E,\Gamma,\hat{\psi})
we have an orientation of
U
and an orientation of the bundle
E
, such
\Gamma
acts on each by
orientation-preserving maps
, and such that for any embedding of
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold bundle charts from
(V,F,\Pi,\hat{\psi}_{V})
to
(U,E,\Gamma,\hat{\psi}_{U})
, the pushforwards of the orientations agree with the restrictions of the orientations.

Finally, we have

Lemma 15
.

Suppose that
s:X\to V
is strongly
\langle k\rangle
-transverse with
(X,V)
oriented. Then for each
S\in 2^{[k]}
for which
\dim X(S)=\dim V
,
s^{-1}(0)
is in the interior of
X(S)
and is a finite collection of points equipped with signs (i.e. an oriented zero-dimensional manifold) lying away from the isotropy locus, while for each
S
for which
\dim X(S)=\dim V+1
,
s^{-1}(0)
is a manifold with boundary on strata
X(T)
,
T\leq S
,
|T|+1=|S|
, lying away from the isotropy locus, with the boundary orientation of the manifold inducing the signs on the points of
\partial s^{-1}(0)\cap X(S)
coming from the transversality of the restriction of
s
to these strata.

Proof.

All statements except the compatibility of orientations follow from applying the dimension computations of
[
BX22
, Prop 6.6]
to the restriction of
s
to the interior of each stratum of
X
. The statement about boundary orientations holds because away from the isotropy locus, strong transversality for
\langle k\rangle
-orbifolds reduces to strong transversality for
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds, and the oriented orbibundle restricts to an oriented vector bundle, where this statement is standard.
∎

3
Global Kuranishi charts for Hamiltonian Floer Homology

Fix a compact symplectic manifold
(M^{2n},\omega)
with a compatible complex structure
J
, and a Hamiltonian diffeomorphism
\phi=\phi^{1}_{H}
. Let
g
be the associated metric.

3.1
An integral
2
-form for every Floer trajectory

Given a closed
2
-form
\Omega
, we define

\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}:\widetilde{Fix}(\phi)\to\mathbb{R}
to be
\int_{x}Hdt-\int_{\tilde{x}}\Omega
. Write
Im(Fix(\phi))
for the union of the images of all the maps
x:S^{1}\to M
,
x\in Fix(\phi)
.

Action Integrals

We say that a closed
2
-form
\Omega

tames

J
if the quantity
g_{\Omega}(v,w)=\Omega(v,Jw)-\Omega(Jv,w)
defines a metric on
M
. We will write
\|v\|_{\Omega}^{2}=g_{\Omega}(v,v)
. By construction we have
\|Jv\|_{\Omega}=\|v\|_{\Omega}
.

If
\Omega
is symplectic, we will write
X_{H}^{\Omega}
for the Hamiltonian vector field of
H
with respect to
\Omega
. Suppose
u
is a Floer trajectory solving

\partial_{s}u+J(\partial_{t}u-X_{H}^{\omega})=0

(26)

with asymptotics
\bar{x}_{\pm}
. We will compute

\int\|\partial_{s}u\|^{2}_{\Omega}=\frac{1}{2}\int\|\partial_{s}u\|^{2}_{\Omega}+\|J(\partial_{t}-X_{H}^{\omega})\|_{\Omega}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\int-\langle g_{\Omega}(\partial_{s}u,J\partial_{t}u)+g(\partial_{s}u,JX^{\omega}_{H}).

(27)

Now we have that

-g_{\Omega}(\partial_{s}u,J\partial_{t}u)=\Omega(\partial_{s}u,\partial_{t}u)+\Omega(J\partial_{s}u,J\partial_{t}u)=2\Omega(\partial_{s}u,\partial_{t}u)+\Omega(X^{\omega}_{H},\partial_{s}u)+\Omega(J\partial_{s}u,JX^{\omega}_{H}).

(28)

On the other hand,

g_{\Omega}(\partial_{s}u,JX^{\omega}_{H})=-\Omega(\partial_{s}u,X^{\omega}_{H})-\Omega(J\partial_{s}u,JX^{\omega}_{H}).

(29)

Thus the integral in (
27
) is equal to

\int u^{*}\Omega+\Omega(X^{\omega}_{H},\partial_{s}u)\;dsdt=\int u^{*}\Omega-\partial_{s}H(u(s,t))dsdt+\Omega(X^{\Omega}_{H}-X^{\omega}_{H},\partial_{s}u)\;dsdt.

(30)

In particular, if
\Omega=\omega
then we see that

\int\|\partial_{s}u\|_{\omega}^{2}=\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\bar{x}_{-})-\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\bar{x}_{+}).

(31)

confirming that the Floer equation is the downwards gradient flow of
\mathcal{A}_{\omega}
.

More generally, given a broken bubbled Floer trajectory
u:\Sigma\to M
, wrote
\Omega^{F}_{u}
for the
2
-form on
\Sigma
which which is
u|_{\Sigma_{\alpha}}^{*}\Omega
on each bubble component
\Sigma_{\alpha}
, and is
u|_{\Sigma_{i}}^{*}\Omega-\partial_{s}H(u(s,t))\,ds\,dt
on each cylindrical component. Then

E_{\Omega}(u)=\int_{\Sigma}\Omega^{F}_{\Omega}+\sum_{j=1}^{r_{u}}\int_{\Sigma_{j}}\Omega(X^{\Omega}_{X^{k}}-X^{\omega}_{H},\partial_{s}u)\,ds\,dt

(32)

where
E_{\Omega}(u)
is the geomeric Floer energy of
u
with respect to
g_{\omega}
, and
\Sigma_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,r_{u}
runs over the cylindrical components of
\Sigma
.

Convenient integral forms.

For
x\in Fix(\phi)
, let
\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{x}=\{\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{x}):\tilde{x}\text{ is capping of }x\}
. Write
Pairs_{\Omega}=\{\tilde{x},\tilde{y}:\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(x)-\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(y)>0\}
. Write
Pairs=\{(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq\emptyset\}
. It is a basic consequence of Gromov compactness for Floer trajectories that

M=\min\{\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\tilde{x})-\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\tilde{y})|(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs\}

(33)

exists and is positive.

Lemma 16
.

There exists a neighborhood
U\subset M
of
Im(Fix(\phi))
such that any closed
2
-form
\Omega
on
M
is cohomologous to a
2
-form
\tilde{\Omega}
restricting to zero on
U
and satisfying
\int_{\bar{x}}\Omega-\tilde{\Omega}=0
for all
x\in\widetilde{Fix}(\phi)
.

Proof.

We first note that there is a neighborhood
U_{0}\subset M
of
Im(Fix(\phi))
such that
H^{2}(U_{0})=0
. Indeed, this follows from the definition of Cech cohomology, together with the fact that
H^{2}(Im(Fix(\phi))=0
for dimension reasons. Thus,
\Omega|_{U_{0}}=d\lambda_{0}
for some
\lambda_{0}\in\Omega^{1}(U_{0})
. We partition the elements of
Fix(\phi)
into subsets
F_{i}
with identical images; for each
x\in F_{i},\int_{x}\lambda_{0}=\lambda(i)
only depends on
i
. If
\lambda_{i}\neq 0
then the image of
x\in F_{i}
is not constant, we can find an embedded interval
I_{i}
inside the image of
x
disjoint from the images of
y\in F_{j}
for
j\neq i
. Thus we can find a closed
1
-form
\lambda_{i}
on
U_{0}
such that
\int_{x}\lambda_{i}=\int_{x}\lambda_{0}
, while
\int_{y}\lambda_{i}=0
for
y\in F_{j}
,
j\neq i
. Set
\lambda^{\prime}_{0}=\lambda_{0}-\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}
; then
d\lambda^{\prime}_{0}=d\lambda_{0}=\Omega|_{U_{0}}
while
\int_{x}\lambda^{\prime}_{0}=0
for each
x\in Fix(\phi)
. Choose a smooth function
f
which is zero outside of
U_{0}
and
1
on an open
U
with
\overline{U}\subset U_{0}
such that
Im(Fix(\phi))\subset U
. Write
\lambda
for the
1
-form equal to
0
outside
U_{0}
and equal to
f\lambda^{\prime}_{0}
on
U_{0}
. Then

\tilde{\Omega}=\Omega-d\lambda
satisfies the desired properties, since
\int_{\bar{x}}\Omega-\tilde{\Omega}=\int_{x}\lambda=0
.
∎

We fix this neighborhood
U
.

Lemma 17
.

Let
x:S^{1}\to M
be a map. Then there exists an
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that if
\int_{S^{1}}\|x^{\prime}-X_{H}\|^{2}_{\omega}<\epsilon_{0}
then
Imx\subset U
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Lemma 5.1 of
[
VO95
]
.
∎

Lemma 18
.

There exists a rational symplectic form
\Omega
taming
J
, and a closed
2
-form
\tilde{\Omega}
cohomologous to
\Omega
and restricting to zero in an open neighborhood of the image of each
x\in Fix(\phi)
, such that
Pairs\subset Pairs_{\Omega}=Pairs_{\tilde{\Omega}}
, and

M^{\prime}=\inf\{\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{x})-\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{y})|(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs\}\geq M/4.

(34)

Proof.

Given
\Omega
, we can construct
\tilde{\Omega}
by applying Lemma
16
, since the conditions on
\tilde{\Omega}
imply that
\int_{\Sigma}u^{*}\Omega=\int_{\Sigma}u^{*}\tilde{\Omega}
for
u:\Sigma\to M
any broken bubbled Floer trajectory.

Define for
\epsilon_{1}>0
the set

S^{\epsilon_{1}}_{\omega}=\left\{\Omega\in\Omega^{2}(M)|\Omega\text{ symplectic },|\Omega(X^{\Omega}_{H}-X^{\omega}_{H},v)|<\epsilon_{1}\|v\|_{\omega},\|v\|_{\Omega}\geq\frac{1}{2}\|v\|_{\omega}\text{ for }v\in TM,\Omega|_{U}=\omega\right\}.

(35)

We have a map
S^{\epsilon_{1}}_{\omega}\to H^{2}(M,\mathbb{R})
given by taking cohomology classes, and this map is surjective onto a neighborhood of
[\omega]
for every
\epsilon_{1}>0
. This follows from the fact that we can choose a set of closed
2
-forms whose cohomology classes span
H^{2}(M,\mathbb{R})
, apply Lemma
16
produce cohomologous
2
-forms which are zero on
U
, and add sufficiently small multiples of these
2
-forms to
\omega
to produce elements of
S^{\epsilon_{1}}_{\omega}
.

We will show that for
\Omega\in S^{\epsilon_{1}}_{\omega}
and
\epsilon_{1}
sufficiently small, there is a constant
C
such that

\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\tilde{x})-\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\tilde{y})\leq C(\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{x})-\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{y})).

(36)

whenever
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs
.

To prove this, it suffices to prove this inequality whenever there exists a
u\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
where the domain of
u
has only one cylindrical component.

In that case, by (
32
) we have the inequality

\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\tilde{x})-\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\tilde{y})=E_{\omega}(u)\leq 2E_{\Omega}(u)=2(\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{x}_{-})-\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{x}_{+}))+2\int_{\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}}\Omega(X^{\Omega}_{H}-X^{\omega}_{H},\partial_{s}u)\,dsdt

(37)

where
\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}
is the cylindrical component of the domain of
u
. We can write
E_{\omega}(u)=E^{sphere}_{\omega}(u)+E^{cyl}_{\omega}(u)
and

E_{\Omega}(u)=E^{sphere}_{\omega}(u)+E^{cyl}_{\Omega}(u)
as the sums of energies of the sphere components and the cylindrical components.

We write
L=\{s\in\mathbb{R}|\int_{S^{1}}\|\partial_{s}u(s,t)\|^{2}_{\omega}dt>\epsilon_{0}\}
and note that
|L|
, the length of
L
, satisfies
|L|\leq E^{cyl}_{\omega}(u)/\epsilon_{0}
. By Cauchy Schwartz and the defining condition for
S^{\epsilon_{1}}_{\omega}
we thus have that

\int_{\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}}|\Omega(X^{\Omega}_{H}-X^{\omega}_{H},\partial_{s}u)|\,dsdt\leq\frac{\epsilon_{1}\sqrt{E^{cyl}_{\omega}(u)}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}}\left(\int_{L}\int_{S^{1}}\|\partial_{s}u\|_{\omega}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq 2\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}}E^{cyl}_{\omega}(u)

(38)

Pick
\epsilon_{1}=\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}/8
. Moving the last term of (
37
) to the left hand side and applying the inequality derived above, we see that

\left(1-4\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}}\right)E^{cyl}_{\omega}(u)+E^{sph}_{\omega}(u)\leq 2(\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{x})-\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{y})).

(39)

But the left hand side is greater than
\frac{1}{2}(E^{cyl}_{\omega}(u)+E^{sph}_{\omega}(u)
, so we can take
C=4
in
36
.

Thus any
\Omega\in S^{\epsilon_{1}}_{\omega}
representing a rational cohomology class suffices.

∎

For
\Omega
as in the lemma above, we have that
\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{x}\in r_{x}+1/m\mathbb{Z}
for some
r_{x}\in\mathbb{R}
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Choose
0<\epsilon_{x}<min(1/m,M^{\prime})
such that

\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{x}+\epsilon_{x}\subset\mathbb{Q}
, and choose
N_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
N_{1}(\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{x}+\epsilon_{x})\subset\mathbb{Z}
.

\text{For the rest of the paper, fix }\Omega_{0}=N_{1}\tilde{\Omega}.

(40)

\bar{\mathcal{A}}:\tilde{Fix}(\phi)\to\mathbb{Z},\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{x})=\int_{\tilde{x}}N_{1}\tilde{\Omega}-\int_{x}N(H+\epsilon_{x})dt.

(41)

Then
\{\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{x})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{y})|(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs\}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. The proof of the previous lemma also shows:

Lemma 19
.

There are real constants
C
and
D
such that

\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\tilde{x})-\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\tilde{y})\leq C(\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{x})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{y}))+D.

(42)

In particular, the space of of broken bubbled Floer trajectories

{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(H,d)=\{u\in{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}),(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs\,|\,\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{x})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{(}y))\leq K\}

(43)

is compact for every
K>0
.
∎

Choose a smooth bump function
h:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
,
h_{Op(0)}=1,h_{Op(1)}=0
,
h(x)+h(1-x)=1
. Choose
\epsilon_{2}>0
such that (when measured using, say,
g
) the exponential map from the
\epsilon
-ball in
T_{x(t)}M
to
M
is injective for each
t\in S^{1}
and each
x\in Fix(\phi)
, and such that for each fixed
t
the images of these balls do not overlap. Define
\bar{H}:M\times S^{1}\to\mathbb{R}
to be equal to
N_{1}H(x,t)
in the complement of
\cup_{x,t}\exp(D_{\epsilon_{2}}T_{x(t)}M\times\{t\}
, and define

\bar{H}(y,t)=h(1-d(y,x(t))/\epsilon_{2})N_{1}H(y,t)+h(d(y,x(t))/\epsilon_{2})N_{1}(H(x(t))+\epsilon_{x})\text{ for }y\in\exp(D_{\epsilon_{2}}T_{x(t)}M).

(44)

Lemma 20
.

The function
\bar{H}
is smooth, and if, for every smooth map

u:\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}\to M,\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}u(s,t)=x_{\pm}(t)\text{(uniformly)},u\#\bar{x}_{-}=\bar{x}_{+}

(45)

we define the
2
-form

\Omega_{u}\in\Omega^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}),\Omega_{u}(s,t)=u^{*}\Omega_{0}(s,t)-\partial_{s}\bar{H}\;dsdt

(46)

then
\Omega_{u}
is zero near
s=\pm\infty
and
\int\Omega_{u}\in\mathbb{Z}
agrees with
\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{x})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{y})
, and takes positive values on
u\in\mathcal{M}(\bar{x}_{-},\bar{x}_{+})
.

Summarizing, we have defined the quantities
(\Omega_{0},\bar{\mathcal{A}},\Omega_{u})
. These are needed to make sense of McLean stabilization of Floer trajectories. The quantity
\bar{\mathcal{A}}
is the
integralized action
. To define these quantities, we had to choose data
(\tilde{\Omega},N_{1},\bar{H})
, which we will call
integralization data
.

3.2
Moduli Spaces for McLean Stabilizations

In this section we describe the Floer-theoretic analog of the moduli spaces of stable maps to projective space in some detail, as well as an alternative way for thinking about elements of the universal curves over these moduli spaces.

3.2.1
Moduli of decorated stable maps to projective space

Write
\mathcal{M}_{0,k}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
for the space of connected stable maps
u:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}
of degree
d
, where
\Sigma
is a nodal domain with
k
marked points. Any such map is contained in a
d
-dimensional linear subspace of
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}
. Let
\mathcal{D}_{k}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell};d)
be the Zariski-open subset consisting of those curves whose image is not contained in any
(d-1)
-dimensional linear subspace.

Given an element
u:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}
in
\mathcal{M}_{0,2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
, call the marked points
p_{-},p_{+}
. There is a unique natural number
r_{u}
and sequence of components
S^{2}_{1},\ldots,S^{2}_{r_{u}}
of
\Sigma
, with
S^{2}_{j}
joined to
S^{2}_{j+1}
along the nodal point
p_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,r_{u}-1
, such that
p_{-}=p_{0}
lies on
S^{2}_{1}
and
p_{+}=p_{r_{u}}
lies on
S^{2}_{r_{u}}
.

We define
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
to be the set of choices
(u,\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r_{u}-1})
of element of
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
together with a choice, for each
j=1,\ldots,r_{u}-1
, of a minimal geodesic
\gamma_{j}
from
p_{j-1}
to
p_{j}
on
S^{2}_{j}
. The choice of minimal geodesic on each component fixes a preferred class of biholomorphisms

\psi_{u,j}:\to\times S^{1}\to S^{2}_{j}\setminus\{p_{-},p_{+}\}

(47)

taking
\mathbb{R}\times\{0\}
to the geodesic, with any two biholomorphisms related by an
\mathbb{R}
-translation. We write
\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
for the universal curve over
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
.

Real oriented blow-up of
\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}
.

The
real oriented blowup
of a smooth submanifold
Y
of a smooth manifold
X
is the union

Bl_{Y}(X)=X\setminus Y\bigsqcup SN_{Y}X,\text{ where }SN_{Y}X\text{ is the unit sphere bundle of the normal bundle of }Y\text{ in }X.

(48)

This carries a canonical smooth structure
[
KM07
]
.

The stratified space
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
has a natural complexification
\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}
. Let
\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}
denote
\mathbb{C}^{n}\supset\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
with the stratification indexed by
2^{[n]}
, with
\mathbb{C}^{n}(S)
equal to the Zariski closure of
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}(S)
. Each
\mathbb{C}^{n}(S)
is a linear subspace of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
, and is given by an intersection of coordinate hyperplanes of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
. Write
H_{1},\ldots,H_{n}
for the coordinate hyperplanes of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
.

We explain how to generalize this notion to construct the real oriented blow-up of
\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}
along its stratification, which will be a smooth manifold of real dimension
2n
with codimension
n
corners. This model case will allow us to construct real-oriented blow-ups of
\mathcal{M}_{0,2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d_{1})
, which will endow
\mathcal{F}(d;d_{1})
with the structure of a smooth manifold with corners.

We define
\Bl\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(S^{1})^{n}
. There is a diffeomorphism
(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\setminus\partial\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\times(S^{1})^{n}\to\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}H_{i}
with inverse given by

(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})\mapsto((|z_{1}|,\ldots,|z_{n}|),(\arg z_{1},\ldots,\arg z_{n}))
. Write
\pi:\Bl\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\to\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}
for the unique continuous (in fact, smooth) extension of this diffeomorphism. We have that
\Bl\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}
is stratified by the poset
2^{[n]}
with stratum associated to
S
given by
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}(S)\times(S^{1})^{n}
. The map
\pi
takes strata surjectively to strata, and exhibits open strata of
\Bl\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}
as trivial torus bundles over open strata of
\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}
.

Let
\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}
denote the space
\mathbb{C}^{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}
with stratification by
2^{[n]}
given by
(\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m})(S)=\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}(S)\times\mathbb{C}^{m}
. The real oriented blow-up
\Bl\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}
is simply
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(S^{1})^{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}
, which admits a smooth map
\pi
to
\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}
given by the product of the map
\pi
in the paragraph above with the identity map.

The fundamental property of this construction is

Lemma 21
.

Given a diffeomorphism
\phi:\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}\supset U\to V\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}
which preservers the stratification, there is a canonical induced diffeomorphism
\tilde{\phi}:\pi^{-1}(U)\to\pi^{-1}(V)
such that
\phi\circ\pi=\pi\circ\tilde{\phi}
.

Proof.

The differential of
\phi
preserves the tangent bundle to each stratum
\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}(S)
thus induces a map
N\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}(S)\simeq N\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}(S)
covering
\phi
which is a linear isomorphism on each fiber. Because it preserves the strata containing
\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}(S)
this map is block diagonal under the decomposition

N\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}(S)=\bigoplus_{j\notin S}N_{\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}(S\cup\{j\})}\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}(S)

(49)

where each of the latter bundles is canonically a trivial
\mathbb{C}
-bundle. But a real-linear isomorphism
\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}
induces a smooth diffeomorphism
\mathbb{C}\setminus 0/\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}\setminus 0/\mathbb{R}
where the
\mathbb{R}
-action is by scaling. As fibers over the open strata are canonically the unit circle bundles of these normal bundles, this defines the lift
\tilde{\phi}
. To see that
\tilde{\phi}
is a diffeomorpism, we note that we can write
\tilde{\phi}
as the unique continuous extension of

\phi^{m}(w,\vec{z})=((|\phi_{1}(w,\vec{z})|,\ldots,|\phi_{n}(w,\vec{z})|),\arg\phi_{1}(w,\vec{z}),\ldots,\arg\phi_{n}(w,\vec{z})),\text{ where }\vec{z}=(|z_{1}|e^{\arg z_{1}},\ldots,|z_{n}|e^{\arg z_{n}}),w\in\mathbb{C}^{m}

(50)
