where
\phi_{j}
are are the compositions of
\phi
with projection to the
j
-th complex coordinate in the
\mathbb{C}^{n}_{+}
factor, while
\phi^{m}
is the composition of
\phi
with projection to the first
m
coordinates. Thus
\tilde{\phi}
pulls smooth functions back to smooth functions and thus is smooth. The inverse of
\tilde{\phi}
is of the same form.
∎

Real oriented blow-up of a manifold with stratification modeled on
\mathbb{C}^{k}_{+}
.

Let
X
be a smooth manifold stratified by
2^{[k]}
. We say that this stratification is modeled on
\mathbb{C}^{k}_{+}
if
X
is
2(m+k)
-dimensional, and if there exists a subset
Charts(X,\mathbb{C}^{k}_{+})
of smooth charts on
X
such that for each chart
\phi:X\supset U\to V\subset\mathbb{R}^{2(m+k)}=\mathbb{C}^{k}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}
,
\phi\in Charts(X,\mathbb{C}^{k}_{+})
, we have that
\phi^{-1}(\mathbb{C}^{k}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}(S))=X(S)\cap U
.

Given such a smooth manifold
X
with stratification modeled on
\mathbb{C}^{k}_{+}
, we can define a new smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
\Bl X
by covering
M
by a collection of charts
\phi:U_{\phi}\to V_{\phi}
,
\phi\in Charts(X,\mathbb{C}^{k}_{+})
and letting
\Bl X
the union of
\pi^{-1}(V_{\phi})
with the equivalence relation that for each
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
a pair of charts in the collection, we identify
\pi^{-1}\phi_{1}(U_{\phi_{1}}\cap U_{\phi_{2}})
with
\pi^{-1}\phi_{1}(U_{\phi_{1}}\cap U_{\phi_{2}})
using the lifts
\widetilde{\phi_{1}\circ\phi_{2}^{-1}}
of
\phi_{1}\circ\phi_{2}^{-1}
produced by Lemma
21
. This manifold is manifestly independent of the choice of charts up to canonical diffeomorphism.

Any stratum-preserving diffeomorphism
f:X\to X
thus lifts to a unique diffeomorphism
\bar{f}:\Bl X\to\Bl X
such that
\pi\bar{f}=f\pi
. Given a smooth
G
-action on
X
which preseves the strata, this lifting procedure gives an induced action
G\times\Bl X\to\Bl X
which makes
\Bl X
into a smooth
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold.

Manifold with corner structure of McLean-stabilized Floer moduli spaces.

We now use the construction of the previous paragraph to give
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
the structure of a smooth
\langle d-1\rangle
-manifold.

There are evaluation maps
ev_{\pm}:\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)\to{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}
given by
(u,\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r_{u}-1})\mapsto u(p_{\pm})
. For
S\in 2^{[d-1]}
, we define

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{S}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)=\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},h_{1})\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}}\times\ldots\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}}\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},h_{d-1-|S|}),\;(h_{1},\ldots,h_{d-1-|S|})=h(S),

(51)

with each fiber product taken over
ev_{+}
on the left and
ev_{-}
on the right. There is a codimension
0
open submanifold

\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S)\subset\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{S}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)

(52)

consisting of tuples of maps
(u_{1},\ldots,u_{d-1-|S|})
such that the dimension of the minimal subspace containing all of their images is
d
; by gluing these stable maps along their marked points we can think of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S)\subset\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
, and we will identify
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S)
with the image of this injection. There is a map
F:\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)\to\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
induced by forgetting the choices of geodesics, and it sends

\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S)
to
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S)
which is a codimension zero open submanifold in

\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S)\subset\mathcal{D}_{2,S}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)\simeq\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},h_{1})\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}}\times\ldots\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}}\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},h_{d-1-|S|}).

(53)

We think of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S)
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S)
as defining stratifications on the corresponding spaces.

We have the following

Lemma 22
.

The stratification of
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
defined by the sets above makes this manifold into a manifold with stratification modeled on
\mathbb{C}^{d-1}_{+}
.

Proof.

This is essentially a standard fact in Gromov-Witten theory. First, assume
\ell=d
. Then given
(u:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d})\in\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
, let
v:\Sigma^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
be the corresponding stable map to
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
with no marked points. We can choose
d+3
hypersurfaces
H_{1},\ldots,H_{d-1}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
such that every nonconstant component of
\Sigma
is transverse to each of the
H_{j}
, such that there exists a choice
x_{j}\in v^{-1}(H_{j})
such that
(\Sigma^{\prime},x_{1},\ldots,x_{d+3})
is a stable curve, and such that
v(x_{j})\notin H_{k}
for
k\neq j
. Choose small neighborhoods
W_{j}
of
x_{j}
which do not overlap and do not intersect
H_{k}
for
k\neq j
. The set of stable maps
V\subset{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,0}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
which are transverse to all
H_{j}
and intersect
H_{j}
in
V_{j}
once is an open set diffeomorphic to its image
V_{marked}\subset{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,d+3}
send a curve
u:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
to
(\Sigma,v^{-1}(H_{1}),\ldots,v^{-1}(H_{d+3}))
. The inverse image of this open set
U
in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
is thus diffeomorphic to its image
U_{marked}
in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,d+5}
under the diffeomorphism
(u,p_{-},p_{+})\mapsto(\Sigma,p_{-},p_{+},v^{-1}(H_{1}),\ldots,v^{-1}(H_{d+3})
. Given
\Sigma^{\prime}\in{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,k}
its neighborhood contains an intersection of hyperplanes
T_{1},\ldots,T_{e}
, one for each node of of
\Sigma^{\prime}
, which is modeled on
\mathbb{C}^{e}_{+}

[
MS12
, Theorem D.4.7]
. The image of our stratification is simply the stratification corresponding to the hyperplanes associated to the nodes on the path between
p_{-}
and
p_{+}
. There are at most
d-1
such nodes, and since we are proving a local statement this establishes the lemma for
\ell=d
.

To prove the lemma for
\ell>d
, can use the fact that there is a smooth stratum-preserving diffeomorphism (see the next paragraph)

U(\ell+1)\times_{(U(d+1)\times U(\ell-d))/U(1)}\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)\to\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d).

(54)

and thus the stratification of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
is also modeled on
\mathbb{C}^{d-1}_{+}
.
∎

Let
\Theta_{-}
denote the circle bundle over
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
with fiber over
u:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}
given by the unit circle of
Tp_{-}\Sigma
. The smooth structure on
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
is defined by the following

Lemma 23
.

Write
\pi:\Bl\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)\to\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
for the canonical projection. There is a bijection
\mathcal{B}:\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)\to\pi^{*}\Theta_{-}
intertwining
F:\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)\to\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
and
\pi\circ\pi_{\pi^{*}\Theta_{-}}:\pi^{*}\Theta_{-}\to\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
. The space
\pi^{*}\Theta_{-}
is a smooth
\langle d-1\rangle
-manifold with the strata given by
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S))
as
S
runs over
2^{[d-1]}
.

Proof.

We think of a point in the fiber of
\pi^{*}\Theta_{-}
as specifying the tangent vector to the geodesic on
S^{2}_{1}
at
p_{-}
; as such, it determines this geodesic, and this defines
\pi
on the interior of the top strata of each of the spaces. The fiber of
\pi
over a curve with
r_{u}
components between the component containing
p_{-}
and the component containing
p_{+}
is
(S^{1})^{r_{u}-1}
as in Lemma
22
, and we interpret these circles as measuring the angle between geodesics. One verifies that this bijection extends from the interior of the top-dimensional strata by analyzing local models for the universal curve over neighborhoods of points on the stratification of
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
, which, as in Lemma
22
, can be extracted from
[
MS12
, Theorem D.4.7]
.
∎

By using the smooth charts from
\mathbb{C}^{k}_{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{\ell}
on
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
induced by the choices of hypersurfaces in the proof of Lemma
22
, we can also see that the pullback
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
of the universal curve
\mathcal{C}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
over
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
to
\Bl\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
is also a smooth
\langle d-1\rangle
-manifold, with the induced map to
\Bl\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
a smooth map. The universal curve
\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
is thus in natural bijection with the pullback of
\pi^{*}\Theta_{1}
to
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
, and thus is a smooth
\langle d-1\rangle
-manifold with a smooth map to
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
. We have proven:

Proposition 1
.

\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
and
\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
are smooth
\langle d-1\rangle
-manifolds and the projection map from the latter to the former is a submersion.
∎

3.2.2
Unitary group actions

There is a natural
U(\ell+1)
action on
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
given by the linear action on homogeneous coordinates; it factors through the projective unitary group
PU(\ell+1)
, which acts effectively. This induces an action of
PU(\ell+1)
on
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
by postcomposition. This action is smooth on
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
, and induces a smooth action on
\Theta_{-}
. Thus, it lifts canonically to a smooth action
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
, making the latter into a smooth
PU(\ell+1)-\langle d-1\rangle
-manifold.

It will be convenient for later sections to note that we can give an alternative formula for
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S)
in terms of
induction and restriction
, without ever taking fiber products over evaluation maps.

Fix
p\in{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}
. We have subspaces
\mathcal{F}_{\pm}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)=ev^{-1}_{\pm}(p)\subset\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
. The subgroup
Stab(p)\subset PU(\ell+1)
which fixes
p
is isomorphic to
U(\ell)\subset PU(\ell+1)
, and the smooth surjective map
PU(\ell+1)\times\mathcal{F}_{\pm}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)\to\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
descends to a map

PU(\ell+1)\times_{U(\ell)}\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d;p,\pm)\simeq\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d).

(55)

The geometry immediately implies that this is a diffeomorphism Lemma
5
, showing that the spaces
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d;p,\pm)
are restrictions of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
.

Since every linearly nondegenerate curve of degree
d
in
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}
is contained inside a unique
d
-dimensional subspace, we have canonical maps

\begin{gathered}U(\ell+1)\times_{(U(d+1)\times U(\ell-d))/U(1)}\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)\to\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d),\\
U(\ell+1)\times_{U(d)\times U(\ell-d)}\mathcal{F}_{\pm}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)\to\mathcal{F}_{\pm}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d).\end{gathered}

(56)

which are also diffeomorphisms by
5
that show that
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
is an induction of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
and similarly for
\mathcal{F}_{\pm}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)

Now, write
d=d_{1}+d_{2}
. Choose a copy of
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
and choose
p\in{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}}
. The stabilizer of
(p,{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}})
is in
PU(d+1)
is a copy of
U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})\subset PU(d+1)
. Define the subset

\begin{gathered}\mathcal{F}^{tr}_{S(d_{1},d_{2})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)\subset\mathcal{F}_{S(d_{1},d_{2})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)\\
\mathcal{F}^{tr}_{S(d_{1},d_{2})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)=\left\{u:\Sigma_{1}\cup_{p_{1}}\Sigma_{2}\to{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d};\;u|_{\Sigma_{1}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}},\deg u|_{\Sigma_{1}}=d_{1},u(p_{1})=p\right\}/\{\text{equiv.}\},\end{gathered}

(57)

where
\Sigma_{1}\cup_{p_{1}}\Sigma_{2}
denotes a nodal curve decomposed into two nodal curves
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
joined along a nodal point
p_{1}
. This subset is a slice for the
PU(d+1)
-action on
\mathcal{F}_{S(d_{1},d_{2})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
, and so
\mathcal{F}^{tr}_{S(d_{1},d_{2})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
induced along
U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})\subset PU(d+1)
is
\mathcal{F}_{S(d_{1},d_{2})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
.

Finally, we have an inclusion

\mathcal{F}_{+}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}},d_{1})\times\mathcal{F}_{-}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}_{d_{2}},d_{2})\subset\mathcal{F}^{tr}_{S(d_{1},d_{2})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)

(58)

as the subspace of
u
where
u|_{\Sigma_{2}}
lies in the complementary subspace
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{2}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
which is spanned by
p
and
d_{2}
additional vectors which are all orthogonal to the vectors projecting to
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}}
. Let
V^{tr}_{d,d_{1}}\subset PGL_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})
be the set of matrices which act isometrically on
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
and send the complementary subspace
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{2}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
isometrically onto another copy of
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{2}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
intersecting
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}}
exactly at
p
.

One sees immediately that there is a
U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})
-equivariant diffeomorphism

V_{d,d_{1}}^{tr}\times_{(U(d)\times U(\ell-d)}\mathcal{F}_{+}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}},d_{1})\times\mathcal{F}_{-}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}_{d_{2}},d_{2})\to\mathcal{F}^{tr}_{S(d_{1},d_{2})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d).

(59)

(sending
(g,u_{1},u_{2})
to the gluing of
gu_{1}
with
gu_{2}
along
p
)
making its codomain a
generalized induction
if its domain.

By iterating the constructions of the above subsection above, we conclude:

Lemma 24
.

The
U(\ell-1)
-
\langle d-1\rangle
-manifold
\mathcal{F}_{S}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
can be obtained from the
U(h_{i})-\langle h_{i}-1\rangle
-manifolds
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{h_{i}},h_{i})
, with
(h_{1},\ldots,h_{d-1-|S|})=h(S)
, by taking products, (generalized) induction, and restriction.
∎

3.2.3
Framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectories.

We recall
[
AMS21
, Definition 6.9]
based on
[
AMS21
, Lemma 6.8]
.

Definition 8
.

Let
\Sigma
be a genus zero nodal curve, and let
\Omega\in\Omega^{2}(\Sigma)
be a closed
2
-form representing an integral cohomology class. There is a unique Hermitian line bundle
L_{\Omega}
with curvature
-2\pi i\Omega
and Hermitian inner product
\langle\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\rangle_{\Omega}
, up to isomorphism.

Definition 9
.

A framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectory (for the Hamiltonian
H
) is a tuple
(u,\Sigma,F)
, where

•

\Sigma
, the
domain
of the trajectory, is a genus zero nodal curve with two marked points
p_{-},p_{+}
, equipped with geodesic segments from
p_{j-1}
to
p_{j}

S^{2}_{J}
, with
S^{2}_{1},\ldots,S^{2}_{j},\ldots,S^{2}_{r_{u}}
the sequence of components from
p_{-}=p_{0}
to
p_{+}=p_{r_{u}}
,

•

The map
u:\Sigma\setminus\{p_{0},\ldots,p_{r_{u}}\}\to M
is a smooth function such that the integral
2
-form
\Omega_{u}\in\Omega^{2}(\Sigma)
defined subsequently has a strictly positive integral on every unstable component. The form
\Omega_{u}
is defined to be
u|_{S^{2}_{\pi}}^{*}\Omega_{0}
(see
40
) on each component
S^{2}_{\pi}
of
\Sigma
that is not one of the
S^{2}_{j}
, and on each
S^{2}_{j}
equal to the extension by zero of the form denoted
\Omega_{u}
in ((
123
)) under any preferred biholomorphism
S^{2}_{j}\setminus\{p_{j-1},p_{j}\}\simeq\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}
.

•

Additionally, the map is required converge uniformly to closed Hamiltonian orbits
(x_{-}=x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{r_{u}}=x_{+})
with
x_{i}\in Fix(H)
the limiting condition the ends corresponding to the
p_{i}
under the biholomorphisms described above; we write
\lim_{z\to p_{i}}u(z)=x_{i}
.

•

Moreover, writing
u^{*}g
for the pull-back of the metric on
M
by
u
, and
dV_{u^{*}g}
for the associated volume form (which will in general not be defined on the
p_{i}
), we have the volume of
\Sigma
with respect to
dV_{u^{*}g}
is finite.

•

The datum
F=(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d})
is a
\mathbb{C}
-basis for
H^{0}(L_{\Omega_{u}})
.

Two framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectories
(u_{1},\Sigma_{1},F_{1})
and
(u_{2},\Sigma_{2},F_{2})
are
equivalent
when there is biholomorphsm
f:\Sigma_{1}\to\Sigma_{2}
intertwining the marked points, geodesic segments, the map
u
, and such that
f_{*}F_{1}=cF_{2}
for some nonzero complex number
c
.

We note that due to the finiteness of the volume, there is a nongenerate Hermitian inner product on
H^{0}(L_{\Omega_{u}})
given by

H(f,g)=\int_{\Sigma}\langle f,g\rangle_{\Omega}dV_{u^{*}g}.

(60)

Thus, to any basis
F
, we have an associated Hermitian matrix
H(F)_{ij}=H(f_{i},f_{j})
.
There is an action of
U(d+1)
on bases
F
and thus on framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectories; this action does
not
involve the form
H
above. Given an element
U\in U(d+1)
, we have that
H((UF))=UH(F)U^{-1}
.

The matrix
H(F)
has positive eigenvalues, so
\log H(F)
is a well-defined Hermitian matrix. The equivalence class. Write
i\mathfrak{u}_{n}
for the set of
n\times n
Hermitian matrices; then the equivalence class of
H(F)\in i\mathfrak{u}_{d+1}/\mathbb{R}I
only depends on the equivalence class of the framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectory.

Any framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectory
(u,\Sigma,F)
gives a well-defined map

u_{F}:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}}
given by

u_{F}(x)=[f_{0}(x),\ldots,f_{d}(x)],\text{ under any $\mathbb{C}$-isomorphism }L_{x}\simeq\mathbb{C}.

(61)

Thus, we have a map
\phi_{F}:\Sigma\to\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d};d)
identifying
\Sigma
with a fiber of the universal curve over
u_{F}
. Moreover, the
U(d+1)
-action on bases
F
makes
((u,\Sigma,F)\mapsto u_{F}
into a
U(d+1)
-equivariant map.

Conversely, to stable map
u_{F}:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{P}}^{d}
such that the degree of each component agrees with the degree of a fixed closed integral
2
-form
\Omega
on that component, we can associate a basis
F
of
H^{0}(L_{\Omega})
that is well defined up to scaling every element of
F
by a nonzero complex number. Such a scaling action changes the matrix
H(u,\Sigma,F)
by scaling the matrix by a positive real number, and we write the corresponding element of
i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}:=i\mathfrak{u}_{d+1}/\mathbb{R}id
as
H(u,\Sigma,u_{F})
.

Remark 6
.

It is fundamental that
H(u,\Sigma,u_{F})
actually depends on
u
, and
cannot
be recovered from the point
u_{F}\in\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
.

The set of equivalence classes of framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectories can thus be viewed as a subset of the space of choices of an integer
d
, an element
u_{F}\in\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
, and a smooth map from the fiber of the universal curve
u:\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)_{u_{F}}\setminus\{p_{j}\}_{j=0}^{r_{u}}\to M
satisfying several conditions. We use this identification to equip this set with the topology induced from the Hausdorff topology on the closures of the graphs of these maps in
\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)\times M
. The different choices of total degree
d
give different components of the space of maps. In particular, the map from the degree
d
classes of framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectories to
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
are continuous and equivariant with respect to the natural
PU(d+1)
action on this component.

3.3
Perturbation data

In this section we explain the kind of data we need to specify to build a candidate Kuranishi chart using the McLean stabilization technique.

We say that a differential form on a nodal curve is a differential form on its normalization. We write
\Omega^{0,1}(\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)_{u},C^{\infty}(TM))
for the
(0,1)
-forms on the fiber of
\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
over
u\in\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
which are valued in vector fields on
M
. We write
\tilde{\Omega}^{0,1}
for the (
finite dimensional
) vector bundle of fiber-wise differential forms on
\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
over
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
which are defined away from the nodes and the marked points; this is a vector bundle over
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(d;d_{1})=\mathcal{C}(d;d_{1})\setminus\{\text{nodes, points}\}
. An element of
\tilde{\Omega}^{0,1}(\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)_{u},C^{\infty}(TM))
defines a section of
\tilde{\Omega}^{0,1}|_{u}\otimes TM
over
\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)_{u}\setminus\{\text{nodes, points}\}\times M
.

Definition 10
.

Let
S
be a subset of a product
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1})\times\ldots\times\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{r}},d_{r})
which is preserved under the action of
G\subset U(\ell_{1}+1)\times\ldots\times U(\ell_{r}+1)
.
An
perturbation datum
on
S
is a
G
-equivariant vector-bundle
V
on
S
together with a smooth map of
G
-equivariant vector bundles over
(\tilde{\mathcal{C}}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1})\times\ldots\times\tilde{\mathcal{C}}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{r}},d_{r}))|_{S}\times M
of the form

\lambda:\tilde{\Pi}^{*}V\to\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}\tilde{\Omega}^{0,1}_{j}\otimes TM

(62)

where

\tilde{\Pi}:(\tilde{\mathcal{C}}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1})\times\ldots\times\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{r}},d_{r}))|_{S}\times M\to S

(63)

is the projection to all but the last factor composed with the projection to
S
,
\tilde{\Omega}^{0,1}_{j}
is the fiber-wise differential forms over
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{j}},d_{j})
, and such that there is an open neighborhood
U
of the nodal locus and the set of marked points for which the linear maps
\lambda_{z,m}
are identically zero whenever
z\in U
.

Equivalently, a perturbation datum is a collection of linear maps

\lambda_{u}:V_{u}\to\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}\Omega^{0,1}(\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{j}},d_{j})_{u_{j}},C^{\infty}(TM)),(u_{1},\ldots,u_{r})\in S

(64)

which are equivariant with respect to the
G
action on all data, satisfy a natural smoothness condition, and are zero in open neighborhoods of the nodes and the marked points.

An isomorphism of perturbation data
\Lambda_{1}=(V_{1},\lambda_{1})\to\Lambda_{2}=(V_{2},\lambda_{2})
is an isomorphism map of
G
-bundles
\phi:V_{1}\to V_{2}
such that
\lambda_{2}\circ\Pi^{*}\phi=\lambda_{1}
.

The
direct sum
of two perturbation data
\Lambda_{i}=(V_{i},\lambda_{i}),i=1,2
over
S
is a perturbation datum over
S
given by
\Lambda_{1}\oplus\Lambda_{2}=(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})
, with

\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}:\Pi^{*}V_{1}\oplus\Pi^{*}V_{2}\to\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}(\tilde{\Omega}_{j}^{0,1}\otimes TM,\;(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})(v_{1},v_{2})=\lambda_{1}(v_{1})+\lambda_{2}(v_{2}).

(65)

The
product
of two perturbation data
(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
over
(S_{1},S_{2})
is the perturbation datum over
S_{1}\times S_{2}
given by
\Lambda_{1}\times\Lambda_{2}=(V_{1}\times V_{2},\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})
with

(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})_{((u_{1},\ldots,u_{r_{1}}),(u^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,u^{\prime}_{r_{2}}))}(v_{1},v_{2})=(\lambda_{1}(u_{1}),\lambda_{2}(u_{2})).

(66)

Given a
G
-equivariant vector bundle
V
on
S
, we have an associated
zero perturbation datum

\Lambda^{V}_{0}=(V,0)
. A
stabilization
of a perturbation datum
\Lambda
is any perturbation datum given by
\Lambda\oplus\Lambda^{V}_{0}
for some
G
-equivariant vector bundle
V
. We say that a perturbation datum
\Lambda=(V,\lambda)
is
convenient
if
G_{x}
acts faithfully on
V_{x}
for each
x\in S
, and we say that a stabilization is
convenient
if it is obtained by stabilizing by a zero perturbation datum which is convenient. Finally, we say that a perturbation datum is
elementary
if
V\simeq\bar{V}\times S
as a an equivariant vector bundle, where
\bar{V}
is a
G
-representation and the
G
-action on
\bar{V}\times S
is the diagonal action.

We have the following

Proposition 2

(Lashof
[
Las79
]
, Prop. 1.1)
.

Let
V
be a
G
-equivariant vector bundle over a
G
-space
S
with finite covering dimension. Then there exists a
G
-vector bundle
W
over
X
such that
V\oplus W\simeq\bar{V}\times S
for
\bar{V}
a
G
-representation.

In particular, every perturbation datum can be stabilized to an elementary perturbation datum, and the stabilization can be chosen to be convenient.
Finally, given
S^{\prime}\supset S
,
S\subset\mathcal{F}(d;d_{1})
, any perturbation datum
\Lambda_{S^{\prime}}=(V,\lambda)
over
S^{\prime}
can be restricted to a perturbation datum
\Lambda_{S}
over
S
by restricting
V
to
S
and
\Lambda
to
\Pi^{-1}(S)
. Conversely, if
\Lambda_{S}
is the restiction of
\Lambda_{S^{\prime}}
we call
\Lambda_{S^{\prime}}
is an extension of
\Lambda_{S}
. Since extending smooth functions from submanifolds of manifolds with corners is straightforward, we have that if
S=\mathcal{F}(d;d_{1})(T)
for
T\in 2^{[d]}
, then an elementary perturbation datum over
S
can always be extended to
S^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}(d;d_{1})(T^{\prime})
for
T\leq T^{\prime}\in 2^{[d]}
. Moreover, if the elementary perturbation datum over
S
restricted to a zero datum on all strata strictly contained in
S
, then the extension to
S^{\prime}
can be chosen to restrict to the zero datum on all strata not strictly containing
S
; we will call such an extension
minimal
.

Remark 7
.

The notion of perturbation datum described above is a natural generalization of the notion of a perturbation datum in
[
AMS21
, Definition 6.11]
. The idea to use perturbation data as in Definition
10
with
V
fixed as the trivial bundle, i.e. the data of a
U(n)
-equivariant map

\lambda:\mathbb{R}^{k}\to\{\xi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)\times M,\Omega^{0,1}_{\mathcal{C}(d;d)/\mathcal{F}(d;d)}\otimes TM)\,|\,\xi=0\text{ near nodes }\}

(67)

was explained to the author in general outline by Mark McLean
[
McL21
]
, although the idea to use the existence of forms
\Omega_{u}
as in Section 2 to make sense of McLean Stabilization for Hamiltonian Floer trajectories, and the many compatibility conditions needed to set up Floer homology, were not present in this interaction, had to be worked out by the author.

Induction and restriction of perturbation data

If we have a perturbation datum
\Lambda=(V,\lambda)
over
S
which is preserved by
H
, and
S^{\prime}
is a (generalized) induction of
S
along
H\subset G\supset V
, then
\Pi^{-1}(S^{\prime})
must be the (generalized) induction of
\Pi^{-1}(S)
. Thus, applying the generalized induction construction to the vector bundle
V
and the map
\lambda
defines a new perturbation datum
Ind_{H}^{G,V}\Lambda
, which is
G
-equivariant when
V=G
in which case we drop
V
from the notation, and is otherwise
H
-equivariant.

In particular, by utilizing Lemma
24
, we conclude

Lemma 25
.

Given perturbation data
\Lambda_{S_{i}}=(V_{S_{i}},\lambda_{S_{i}})
,
S_{i}\subset[d_{i}-1]
,
i=1,\ldots,r
on each of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{i}},d_{i})(S_{i})
, we have a canonically associated perturbation datum
Ind(\Lambda_{S_{1}},\ldots,\Lambda_{S_{r}})=(V_{\vec{S}},\lambda_{\vec{S}})
,
\vec{S}=(S_{1},\ldots,S_{r})
on
\mathcal{F}_{S}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
, where

S=S(S_{1},\ldots,S_{r})=S_{1}\cup\{d_{1}\}\cup S_{2}+d_{1}\cup\{d_{1}+d_{2}\}\cup\ldots\cup S_{r}+\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}d_{i}.

(68)

Here,

V_{\vec{S}}
is the restriction to
\mathcal{F}_{\vec{S}}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
of the bundle on

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\vec{S}}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)=\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d_{1})(S_{1})\times\ldots\times\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d_{r})(S_{r})

(69)

with fiber over

(u_{1},\ldots,u_{r})
given by

\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}(Ind_{PU(d_{i}+1)}^{PU(d+1)}V_{S_{i}})_{u_{i}}

(70)

and

\lambda_{\vec{d}}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{r})=\sum_{i=1}^{r}(Ind_{(U(d_{i}+1)}^{U(d+1)}\lambda_{S_{i}})(v_{i})

(71)

where we extend the terms of the sum by zero away from the codomain of
Ind_{(U(d_{i}+1)}^{U(d+1)}\lambda_{S_{i}}
.

From this above formula, it is clear that

Ind(Ind(\Lambda_{d_{1}^{1}}\ldots,\Lambda_{d_{1}^{r_{1}}}),Ind(\Lambda_{d^{2}_{1}},\ldots,\Lambda_{d_{2}^{r_{2}}}),\ldots)=Ind(\Lambda_{d_{1}^{1}},\ldots,\Lambda_{d_{1}^{r_{1}}},\Lambda_{d_{2}^{1}},\ldots).

(72)

Where
S_{j}=S(d_{j}^{1},\ldots,d_{j}^{r_{j}})
and we write
\Lambda_{d}
for a perturbation datum defined on all of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
.
Finally, this datum can be produced from the
\Lambda_{S_{i}}
by taking products, restrictions, and (generalized) inductions.

Definition 11
.

The data of a
compatible choice of peturbation data
(up to degree
D
) is a choice, for each positive integer
d\leq D
, of a perturbation datum
\Lambda_{d}
on
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
, which, for every
S\in 2^{[d-1]}
, restricts to a convenient stabilization of the perturbation datum
Ind_{h(S)}(\Lambda_{h_{1}},\ldots,\Lambda_{h_{d-1-|S|}})
on
\mathcal{F}_{S(d_{1},\ldots,d_{r})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
, where
(h_{1},\ldots,h_{d-1-|S|})=h(S)
. When
D
is not specified, then this refers to a choice of
\Lambda_{d}
for all
d>0
such that the above conditoins are satisfied for all
D
.

Remark 8
.

This could be weakened significantly to require that the restriction of
\Lambda_{d}
restricts to
\mathcal{F}_{S(d_{1},\ldots,d_{r})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
admits
Ind_{h(S)}(h_{1},\ldots,h_{d-1-|S|})
as direct summand, at the cost of additional argument later on.

3.4
Global Kuranishi Charts

For each positive integer
d
, write
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(H,d)
for the set of bubbled Floer trajectories
u:\Sigma\to M
in
\bigsqcup_{\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+}}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})
such that
\int_{\Sigma}\Omega_{u}=d
. By Lemma
19
, this closed subspace is compact. We will write
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\leq d)=\cup_{d^{\prime}\leq d}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(d^{\prime})
.

Definition 12
.

Let
\Lambda_{d}=(V_{d},\lambda_{d})
be a perturbation datum on
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d};d)
. The Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda_{d}}=(U(d+1),T_{\Lambda_{d}}\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda_{d}}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1},\sigma_{\Lambda_{d}}\oplus\sigma_{H})
associated to
\Lambda_{d}
has
thickening

T_{\Lambda_{d}}
with elements consisting of equivalence classes of pairs of

•

a framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectory
(u,\Sigma,F)
,

•

An element
\eta\in(V_{d})_{u_{F}}

such that
\deg u_{F}|_{S^{2}_{\alpha}}=\int_{S^{2}_{\alpha}}\Omega_{u}
for every component
S^{2}_{\alpha}
of
\Sigma
, and such that we have

\begin{gathered}\bar{\partial}u_{\alpha}+\lambda_{i}(\eta)\circ di_{F}=0\text{ for }u_{\alpha}=u|_{S^{2}_{\alpha}},S^{2}_{\alpha}\alpha\neq S^{2}_{1},\ldots,S^{2}_{r_{u}},\\
(d\tilde{u}_{j}+dt\otimes X_{H})^{0,1}_{J}+\lambda_{i}(\eta)\circ di_{F}\circ d\psi_{u,j}=0\text{ for }\tilde{u}_{j}=u|_{S^{2}_{j}}\circ\psi_{u,j}\end{gathered}

(73)

Here as usual
S^{2}_{\alpha}
is a component of
\Sigma
.

We write
V_{\Lambda_{d}}
for the vector bundle over
T_{\Lambda_{d}}
with fiber over
((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)
given by
(V_{d})_{u_{F}}
, and
\sigma_{\Lambda_{d}}
for the section of
\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda_{d}}
given by
((u,\Sigma,F),\lambda)\mapsto\lambda
, and
\sigma_{H}
for the section of the trivial bundle with fiber
i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
given by
((u,\Sigma,F),\lambda)\mapsto H(u,\Sigma,u_{F})
.

We also define the Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda_{d},\epsilon}=(PU(d+1),T_{\lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon},\mathcal{V}_{d}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1},\sigma_{\Lambda_{d}}\oplus\sigma_{H})
where
T_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}\subset T_{\Lambda_{d}}
consists of those
((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)
for which
\|\eta\|<\epsilon
. We will take
\epsilon
to be a sufficiently small positive number depending on
d
.

The space
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{d},\epsilon}
carries a (continous)
PU(d+1)
-action induced from the diagonal action on pre-Floer trajectories and the elements in the fibers of the perturbation datum. The map
\pi
to
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
is equivariant.

For
S\in 2^{[d-1]}
, we write
T_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}(S)
for the set of pairs
((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)\in T_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}
with
u_{F}\in\mathbb{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)(S)
, and we write
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}(S)=(PU(d+1),T_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}(S),\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda_{d}}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1},\sigma_{\Lambda_{d}}\oplus\sigma_{H})
(where we have restricted the vector bundles and the sections to
T_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}(S)
).

Ampleness of
L_{\Omega_{u}}
on all unstable components, smallness of
\eta
, and Gromov compactness implies as in
[
AMS21
]
,

Lemma 26
.

The
PU(d+1)
action on
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}
has finite stabilizers. ∎

Remark 9
.

In
[
AMS21
]
it is claimed that the
U(d+1)
action has finite stabilizers, which is clearly incorrect as the center
S^{1}
of the unitary group fixes projective space. That is the reason for the switch to
PU(d+1)
throughout this paper; with this modification the argument of
[
AMS21
]
is correct.

Lemma 27
.

The induced topology on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(a)
agrees with the usual Gromov topology.

Proof.

Recall
[
Par16
, p. 96]
that a sequence of Floer trajectories
u_{i}
Gromov converges to
u_{\infty}
if we can find a set of divisors transverse to the images of all maps sufficiently far in the sequence such that the graphs of the maps
u^{\prime}_{i}
obtained from
u_{i}
by stabilizing their domains with divisors converge in the Hausdorff [ on the closures of their graphs in
\overline{\mathcal{C}}^{\$}_{0,r+2}/\Gamma\times M
, where
r
is the number of added stabilizing points,
\Gamma
is the group permuting the stabilizing points arising from the same divisor, and
\overline{\mathcal{C}}^{\$}_{0,r+2}
is the modification of the universal curve over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}(0,r+2)
obtained by replacing each of the marked points on the path between the two original marked points by a circle (which is possible since the curves being considered are now stable). Choosing bases
F_{i}
to extend
u_{i}
to framed bubbled Floer trajectories gives a sequence of stable maps from the domains
\Sigma
to
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
with
r+2
marked points. These latter spaces of stable maps are compact, so a subsequence of these
F_{i}
converges, and thus the tuples
((u_{i},\Sigma_{i},F_{i}),0)
converge in the induced topology. Conversely if such a sequence converges in the induced topology, then adding stabilizing divisors gives stable maps to
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
with
r+2
marked points which still converge, and thus (by forgetting the map to
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
) the graphs of the
u_{i}
in
\overline{\mathcal{C}}^{\$}_{0,r+2}\times M
Hausdorff-converge as well.
∎

Choose
k>1
,
p>2
.
Given an element
((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)\in T_{\Lambda_{d}}
, the linearization of the defining equation (
73
) is the Fredholm operator

\begin{gathered}D_{u,F,\eta,\Lambda_{d}}:\bigoplus_{S^{2}_{\alpha}\subset\Sigma}W^{k,p}(u_{\alpha}^{*}TM)\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r_{u}}W^{k,p}(\tilde{u}_{j}^{*}TM)\oplus(V_{d})_{u_{F}}\to\bigoplus_{S^{2}_{\alpha}\subset\Sigma}W^{k-1,p}(u_{\alpha}^{*}TM)\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r_{u}}W^{k-1,p}(\tilde{u}_{j}^{*}TM)\\
D_{u,F,\Lambda_{d}}(\ldots,\xi_{\alpha},\ldots,\xi_{1},\ldots\xi_{r_{u}},\eta^{\prime})=(\ldots,D\bar{\partial}u_{\alpha}+\lambda_{i}(\eta^{\prime})\circ di_{F},\ldots,D_{\tilde{u}_{j}}\tilde{u}_{j}+\lambda_{i}(\eta^{\prime})\circ di_{F}\circ d\psi_{u,j}).\end{gathered}

(74)

Here we use notation as in (
73
),
\Sigma_{\alpha}\subset\Sigma
runs over components that are not marked by a geodesic, and
D\bar{\partial}
and
D_{u}
are the linearization of the Cauchy-Riemann and Floer equations respectively, which can be found in e.g.
[
MS12
]
. By elliptic regularity the surjectivity of such an an operator is independent of
(k,p)
. Moreover, if
D_{u,F,\Lambda_{d}}
is surjective then so is
D_{u,UFU^{-1},\Lambda_{d}}
for any
U\in U(d+1)
.

Lemma 28
.

We can choose a sequence of compatible perturbation data
\Lambda_{1},\ldots\Lambda_{d},\ldots
as in Definition
11
, and such that the operators defined (
74
) are surjective for each
(u:\Sigma\to M)\in{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(d)
and each framing
F
of
(u,\Sigma)
.

Proof.

We will construct the perturbation data
\Lambda_{d}
by induction on
d
.

For each
d
, we will choose an elementary perturbation datum
\Lambda^{o}_{d}
on
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
which will restrict to a zero datum on every proper substratum of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
. The perturbation datum
\Lambda_{d}
will then take the form

\Lambda_{d}=\bigoplus_{S\in 2^{[d-1]}}\Lambda^{o}_{d,S}

(75)

where
\Lambda^{o}_{d,[d-1]}=\Lambda^{o}_{d}
, and
\Lambda^{o}_{d,S}
is a
minimal
extension of a convenient stabilization of
Ind(\Lambda^{o}_{h_{1},[h_{1}-1]},\ldots,\Lambda^{o}_{h_{r},[h_{r}-1]})
for
(h_{j})=h(S)
.

The compatibility condition on
\Lambda_{d}
is satisfied if the above ansatz (
75
) holds, as well as the condition below: for all
T\supset S
, writing
h(T)=(d_{1}\ldots,d_{r})
and
(d_{1}^{1},\ldots,d_{1}^{a_{1}},d_{2}^{1},\ldots,d_{2}^{a_{2}},\ldots,d_{r}^{a_{r}})
, where
\sum_{\ell=1}^{a_{j}}d_{j}^{\ell}=d_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots r
, we have that
\Lambda^{0}_{d,S}
restricted to
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)(T)
is a convenient stabilization of

Ind(\Lambda^{o}_{d_{1},S(d_{1}^{1},\ldots,d_{1}^{a_{1}})},\ldots,\Lambda^{o}_{d_{r},S(d_{r}^{1},\ldots,d_{r}^{a_{r}})}).

(76)

(We only need to consider
T\supset S
by the minimality condition; the restriction of
\Lambda^{o}_{d,S}
to all other strata is a zero datum.)

Assume that we have constructed compatible perturbation data
\Lambda_{1},\ldots,\Lambda_{d-1}
satisfying the ansatz (
75
), and thus the condition (
76
). Let’s write
\Lambda^{o}_{d,S}=(V^{o}_{d,S},\lambda^{o}_{d,S})
. This latter condition almost specifies
\Lambda^{o}_{d,S}
for
S\neq[d-1]
; specifically, it is required to be a convenient stabilization of a perturbation datum that is already defined on each codimension
1
boundary stratum of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
. Let us first understand how to find a compatible extension of the underlying vector bundles of all of these perturbation data specified on the boundary of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
to the interior. By the inductive hypothesis, we can apply (
76
) to each of the
\Lambda^{o}_{d_{j},S^{\prime}}
appearing in (
76
) for the restriction of
\Lambda^{o}_{d,S}
to each codimesion
1
stratum
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)(T)
; associativity of induction then implies that there exist vector bundles
V^{o}_{d,S,T}
over
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)(T)
for
T\supset S
,
T\neq[d-1]
, such that there is a
PU(d+1)
-equivariant inclusion
\iota_{d,S,T^{\prime},T}:V^{o}_{d,S,T}\to V^{o}_{d,S,T^{\prime}}
for
T^{\prime}\supset T
,
|T^{\prime}\setminus T|=1
; such that composing a sequence of these inclusions along pair of paths in the poset
\{T^{\prime}\in 2^{[d-1]}:T\leq T^{\prime}\leq[d-1]\}
gives the same map; and moreover that
V^{o}_{d,S,S}=Ind(\Lambda^{o}_{h_{1},[h_{1}-1]},\ldots,\Lambda^{o}_{h_{r},[h_{r}-1]})
where
(h_{j})=h(S)
. The first step is to find a compatible extension of all of these bundles:

Lemma 29
.

We can find a faithful representation
R(\sigma_{d,S})
such that there are
PU(d+1)
-equivariant bundle inclusions
\iota_{d,S,T}:V^{o}_{d,S,T}\to R(\sigma_{d,S})\times\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)(T)
such that

\iota_{d,S,T^{\prime}}\iota_{d,S,T^{\prime},T}=\iota_{d,S,T}
.

Proof.

This follows repeated application of
2
along inclusions in the poset
\{T^{\prime}\in 2^{[d-1]}:T\leq T^{\prime}\leq[d-1]\}
, together with the fact that if we take the direct sum of two maps of bundles, then if one of them is a bundle inclusion then so is the direct sum.
∎

Now, (
76
) specifies
\lambda^{o}_{d,S}
on each
\tilde{\Pi}^{*}V^{o}_{d,S,T}
, with the property that it is zero on the complement of
\tilde{\Pi}^{*}V^{o}_{d,S,T}
in
\tilde{\Pi}^{*}V^{o}_{d,S,T^{\prime}}|_{\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)(T)}
for
[d-1]\supset T^{\prime}\supset T\supset S
,
|T^{\prime}\setminus T|=1
. By extending
\lambda^{o}_{d,S}
by zero to the complements of
\tilde{\Pi}^{*}V^{o}_{d,S,T}
over all
T
, we have defines
\lambda^{o}_{d,S}:\tilde{\Pi}^{*}(R(\sigma_{d,S})\times\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d))|_{\partial\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)}
. It then remains to extend this smooth function from the boundary of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
to the interior to produce
\Lambda^{o}_{d,S}
for all
S\neq[d-1]
satisfying the conditions claimed.

By ansatz
(
75
)
and the inductive hypothesis, we have that
D_{u,F,\bar{\Lambda}_{d}}
is surjective for each
(u,\Sigma,F)
with
\pi(\phi_{F}(\Sigma))\in\partial\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
. By the gluing theorem for linear Cauchy-Riemann operators, this operator is thus surjective for all
(u,\Sigma,F)
with
\pi(\phi_{F}(\Sigma))\in U
for
U
some open neighborhood of the boundary. Let
U^{\prime}
be the set of bubbled Floer trajectories
(u:\Sigma\to M)\in{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(d)
which extend to such a framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectory, and let
K
be the complement of
U
in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(d)
. Then
K
is compact. Given any
(u,\Sigma,F)
and any element
\theta\in\Omega^{0,1}(\Sigma)
supported away from the marked points and with
\Sigma
having only one component, there exists a smooth
(0,1)
-form
\bar{\theta}
on the universal curve over
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
supported away from all marked points and nodes and restricting to zero on a neighborhood over
\partial{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
, which restricts to
\theta
on the fiber associated to
(u,\Sigma,F)
. Taking the
PU(d+1)
-orbit of such a
(0,1)
-form defines a perturbation datum
\Lambda_{\theta}
with a trivial underlying vector bundle (which has fiber the span of the orbit of
\bar{\theta}
). For each
(u:\Sigma\to M)\in K
, choose an
F
such that
(u,\Sigma,F)
is a framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectory, and choose a finite collection of
1
-forms
\theta_{i}^{u}
on
\Omega^{0,1}(\Sigma)
, which surject onto the cokernel of
D_{u}
and are supported away from the marked points of
\Sigma
. Applying the previous construction gives a perturbation datum
\Lambda_{u}
such that
\mathcal{D}_{u,F,\Lambda_{u}}
is surjective, and by continuity
\mathcal{D}_{u^{\prime},F^{\prime},\Lambda_{u}}
is surjective for
(u^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime},F^{\prime})
arising by framing a Floer trajectory sufficiently near
u
. By compactness of
K
we can take
\Lambda^{o}_{F}
to be the direct sum of
\Lambda_{u,F}
over a finite collection of
u\in K
.
∎

By compactness of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(d)
, by taking a sufficiently small
\epsilon
, we can arrange for
D_{u,F,\Lambda_{d}}
to be surjective for all
(u,F,\eta)\in T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}

Proposition 3
.

The spaces
T_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}
are topological
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds, and the projection
\Pi
to
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
a fiberwise smooth
C^{1}_{loc}

PU(d+1)
-equivariant topological submersion.

Proof.

This follows analogously to
[
AMS21
, Section 6.8]
from the gluing results of
[
Par16
]
.

First, the fibers of
\Pi:T_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}\to\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
are naturally smooth manifolds of the expected dimension, as we can identify them with thickened moduli spaces of Floer trajectories with a fixed domain.

Next, as in Lemma
22
, we can identify a neighborhood
V
of
\Pi((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)\in\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
with an open subset
j:U\to\widetilde{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,d+5}}
of a circle bundle of the real-oriented blowup of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,d+5}
along its natural stratification; call this identification
\mu_{H}:V\to U
. Let
K\subset\Pi^{-1}(\Pi((u,\Sigma,F),\eta))
be a compact neighborhood of
((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)
in the fiber. We claim, as in
[
AMS21
, Theorem 6.1]
, that, shrinking
K
and
V
as necessary, that there is a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}=\Pi^{-1}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}_{0,d+5}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{H}\circ\Pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

(77)

which is a homeomorphism onto its image. Moreover, for each
a\in U
, the restriction
g|_{\{a\}\times K}\to\Pi^{-1}(\mu_{H}^{-1}(a))
takes place in
\Pi^{-1}(\mu_{H}^{-1}(a))
and is a diffeomorphism onto its image.

Indeed, this is a special case of appendix C of
[
Par16
]
where
n=0
, i.e. we are working with a single Hamiltonian rather than a continuation map. The map
g
is defined by
[
Par16
,
\nopp
C.10.3]
and
[
Par16
,
\nopp
C.10.4]
; the commutativity of the diagram follows from
[
Par16
,
\nopp
C.10.1]
. The horizontal arrow is a homeomorphism onto its image because of Proposition
[
Par16
,
\nopp
C.10.11]
, Lemmas
[
Par16
,
\nopp
C.10.3]
and
[
Par16
,
\nopp
C.10.4]
, all combined with Lemma
[
Par16
,
\nopp
B.12.1]
.
The restriction of
g
to
\{a\}\times K
is obtained by Newton-Picard iteration (
[
Par16
,
\nopp
C.9.5]
and
[
Par16
,
\nopp
C.9.6]
) with initial conditions smoothly depending on
K
for each
a\in U
; hence this map is smooth for each fixed
a
.

We have proven that
\Pi
has a fiberwise smooth structure. The fact that it has a
C^{1}_{loc}
structure follows as in
[
AMS21
, Corollary 6.29]
but with
[
Par16
,
\nopp
B.11.1]
replaced with
[
Par16
,
\nopp
C.11.1]
.
∎

Definition 13
.

A pair of global Kuranishi charts
\mathcal{K}_{1}=(G,T_{1},V_{1},\sigma_{1})
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}=(G,T_{2},V_{2},\sigma_{2})
are
equivalent
if there are
G
-equivariant diffeomorphisms
f:T_{1}\to T_{2}
and a bundle isomorphism
g:V_{1}\to V_{2}
such that
\pi_{2}\circ g=f\circ\pi_{1}
, where
\pi_{i}:V_{i}\to T_{i}
are the vector bundle projections, and
g\circ\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}\circ f
.

Proposition 4
.

The Kuranishi charts
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}(S)
can be obtained by induction, restriction, products, taking open subsets, and equivalences from the Kuranishi charts

\mathcal{L}_{\ell}
,
\ell<d
.

Proof.

It is clear that since
\Lambda_{d}
restricts to a stabilization of
\Lambda_{\vec{S}}=Ind(\Lambda_{S_{1}},\ldots,\Lambda_{S_{r}})
, the Kuranishi chart
K^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
is a stabilization of

K(S_{1},\ldots,S_{r})^{\epsilon}=(U(d+1),T(\vec{S}),\mathcal{V}(\vec{S})\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1},\sigma_{\Lambda_{\vec{S}}}\oplus\sigma_{H})

(78)

where

T(S_{1},\ldots,S_{r})=\{((u,\Sigma,F),\eta))\in T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}|u_{F}\in\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)(S),\eta\in(V_{\vec{S}})_{u_{F}}\},S\text{ as in }\eqref{eq:S-composition}.

(79)

and
\mathcal{V}(\vec{S})_{((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)}=(V_{\vec{S}})_{u_{F}}
.

More generally, taking products, inductions/restrictions, and stabilizations of perturbation data corresponds to taking products, inductions/restrictions, and stabilizations of associated Kuranishi charts.

Thus, we wish to apply Lemma
24
. The crucial point is to show that the generalized inductions (
59
) correspond to
stabilizations
of the corresponding Kuranishi charts. This follows from an explicit computation given below. Given this claim, the lemma follows from associativity of induction (
72
).

Fix
d_{1}+d_{2}=d
. Write
\Lambda_{a}=\Lambda_{d_{1}}
and
\Lambda_{b}=\Lambda_{d_{2}}
; write
\Lambda_{ab}=Ind(\Lambda_{a},\Lambda_{b})
. We fix embeddings
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{2}}
as the subset with the last
d_{2}
coordinates zero, and
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{2}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}
as the subset with the first
d_{1}
coordinates zero. They intersect in
p=[0,\ldots,0,1,0,\ldots,0]
where the
1
is in the
d_{1}+1
-st coordinate. Consider the following Kuranishi charts:

\begin{gathered}\mathcal{K}_{1}=(U(d_{1}),T_{1}^{\epsilon},\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda_{a}}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1}+1},\sigma_{\Lambda_{1}}\oplus\sigma_{H})\\
T_{1}^{\epsilon}=\{((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)|u_{F}\in\mathcal{F}_{+}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}},d_{1}),\eta\in(V_{\Lambda_{b}})_{u_{F}},\|\eta\|<\epsilon,(\ref{eq:perturbed-floer-equation})\text{ holds }\};\end{gathered}

(80)

\begin{gathered}K_{2}^{\epsilon}=(U(d_{2}),T_{2}^{\epsilon},\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda_{b}}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{2}+1},\sigma_{\Lambda_{2}}\oplus\sigma_{H}\}\\
T^{\epsilon}_{2}=\{((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)|u_{F}\in\mathcal{F}_{-}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{2}},d_{2}),\eta\in(V_{\Lambda_{b}})_{u_{F}},\|\eta\|<\epsilon,(\ref{eq:perturbed-floer-equation})\text{ holds }\};\end{gathered}

(81)

\begin{gathered}K_{3}^{\epsilon,\epsilon}=\{(U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2}),T^{\epsilon,\epsilon}_{3},\mathcal{V}_{ab}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1},\sigma_{\Lambda_{ab}}\oplus\sigma_{H}\}\\
T^{\epsilon,\epsilon}_{3}=\{((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)|u_{F}\in\mathcal{F}^{tr}_{S(d_{1},d_{2})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d),\eta=(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\in(V_{ab})_{u_{F}},\|\eta_{1}\|<\epsilon,\eta_{2}<\epsilon,(\ref{eq:perturbed-floer-equation})\text{ holds }\};\end{gathered}

(82)

\begin{gathered}K^{\epsilon}_{3}=(U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2}),T^{\epsilon,\epsilon}_{3},\mathcal{V}_{ab}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1})\\
T^{\epsilon}_{3}=\{((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)\in T^{\epsilon,\epsilon}_{3}|\|\eta\|<\epsilon\}.\end{gathered}

(83)

Here, we can write
\eta=(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\in(V_{ab})_{u_{F}}
in (
82
) due to (
70
). We have an inclusion

\begin{gathered}T_{1}^{\epsilon}\times T_{2}^{\epsilon}\to T_{3}^{\epsilon,\epsilon}\\
(g,((u_{1},\Sigma_{1},F_{1}),\eta_{1}),((u_{2},\Sigma_{2},F_{2}),\eta_{2})\mapsto((u_{1}\#\circ u_{2},\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2},F_{1}\#F_{2}),\eta_{1}+\eta_{2})\end{gathered}

(84)

where the domain
\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2}
is obtained by gluing
p_{+}\in\Sigma_{1}
with
p_{-}\in\Sigma_{2}
, the map
u_{1}\#u_{2}
is the corresponding concatenation of maps, and
F_{1}\#F_{2}
is obtained as follows. Write
F_{1}=(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d_{1}})
and
F_{2}=(f^{\prime}_{d_{1}},\ldots,f^{\prime}_{d_{2}})
. Then we have evaluation maps
ev_{1}:H^{0}(L_{u_{1}})\to(L_{u_{1}})_{p}
and
ev_{2}:H^{0}(L_{u_{2}})\to(L_{u_{2}})_{p})
and we must have that
f_{0},\ldots f_{d_{1}-1}\in\ker
and
f^{\prime}_{d_{1}+1},\ldots,f^{\prime}_{d_{2}}\in\ker ev_{2}
. Then
F_{0}\#F_{1}
is obtained by globally rescaling
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
each so that
f_{d_{1}}
and
f^{\prime}_{d_{1}}
such that
ev_{1}(f_{d_{1}})=ev_{2}(f^{\prime}_{d_{1}})
. This defines a basis of
L_{u_{1}\#u_{2}}
which is well defined up to scaling by
\mathbb{C}^{*}
, and which we denote by
(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d-1},\bar{f}_{d_{1}},f^{\prime}_{d_{1}+1},\ldots,f^{\prime}_{d})
.

The claim is that
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon,\epsilon}_{3}
is equivalent to the stabilization of
K_{1}^{\epsilon}\times K_{2}^{\epsilon}
by the vector bundle
i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}^{d_{1},d_{2}}
, which is a trivial bundle with fiber the set of
d_{1}+1\times d_{1}+1
Hermitian matrices on which only the top right
d_{1}\times d_{2}
and bottom left
d_{2}\times d_{1}
block are allowed to be nonzero; this vector space is naturally a
U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})
-representation. Clearly
K^{\epsilon}_{3}
is just the restriction of
K^{\epsilon,\epsilon}_{3}
to an open subset, and an induction of
K^{\epsilon}_{d}
along
U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})\subset PU(d+1)
gives the Kuranishi chart associated to
Ind(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
.

To see this, we first explain the map
i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1}+1}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{2}+1}\to i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}|_{T_{1}^{\epsilon}\times T_{2}^{\epsilon}}
covering the map (
84
) and sending
\sigma_{H}\oplus\sigma_{H}
to
\sigma_{H}
. This map is the map which sends the equivalence class of
\log H_{1}
, where
H_{1}
is an inner product on
H^{0}(L_{u_{1}})
up to scale expressed in the basis
(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d_{1}})
, and the equivalence class of
\log H_{2}
, where
H_{2}
is in turn an inner product on
H^{0}(L_{u_{2}})
expressed in the basis
(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d_{1}})
, to the equivalence class of
\log H
, where
H
is an inner product on
H^{0}(L_{u_{1}\#u_{2}})
expressed in the base
F_{1}\#F_{2}
determined by the condition that it restricts to
H_{1}
on
F_{1}
, to
H_{2}
on
F_{2}
, and such that we have chosen
(H_{1},H_{2})
in their equivalence classes such that
H(\bar{f}_{d},\bar{f}_{d})=H_{1}(f_{d},f_{d})=H_{2}(f^{\prime}_{d},f^{\prime}_{d})
and that
f_{0},\ldots,f_{d-1}
are orthogonal to
f^{\prime}_{d_{1}},\ldots,f^{\prime}_{d}
.

In fact, writing
(a_{i,j})_{i,j=0}^{d_{1}}\in i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1}+1}
and
(b_{i,j})_{i,j=d_{1}}^{d_{2}}\in i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{2}+1}
, the equivalence class of
\log H
is represented by
(A_{i,j})_{i,j=0}^{d_{2}}
given by

A_{i,j}=\begin{cases}a_{i,j}&\text{if }0\leq i,j\leq d_{1}\\
b_{i,j}&\text{if }d_{1}\leq i,j\leq d_{2},i\neq j\\
b_{i,i}+a_{d_{1},d_{1}}-b_{d_{1},d_{1}}&\text{if }d_{1}\leq i,j\leq d_{2},i=j\\
0&\text{else}.\end{cases}

(85)

In particular we see that this is a linear bundle map that is equivariant with respect to the
U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})
action.

Now, given a matrix
M\in i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}^{d_{1},d_{2}}
as well an inner product
H
as above, there is a
unique
element
g_{H}(M)\in V^{tr}_{d,d_{1}}\in PU(d+1)
such that the inner product expressed of the basis
F_{1}\#_{g_{H}(M)}F_{2}(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d-1},g_{H}(M)\bar{f}_{d_{1}},\ldots,g_{H}(M)f^{\prime}_{d})
is
H+M
. This defines a diffeomorphism

\begin{gathered}i\mathfrak{pu}^{d_{1},d_{2}}_{d+1}\times T^{\epsilon}_{1}\times T^{\epsilon}_{2}\to T^{\epsilon,\epsilon}_{3}\simeq V^{tr}_{d,d_{1}}\times_{U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})}T^{\epsilon}_{1}\times T^{\epsilon}_{2}\\
(M,((u_{1},\Sigma_{1},F_{1}),\eta_{1}),((u_{2},\Sigma_{2},F_{2}),\eta_{2}))\mapsto((u_{1}\#u_{2},\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2},F_{1}\#_{g_{H}(M)}F_{2}),(\eta_{1}+g_{H}(M)\eta_{2})\end{gathered}

(86)

where
H=\sigma_{H}(u_{1}\#u_{2},\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2},F_{1}\#F_{2})
. Covering this map we have a diffeomorphism

\begin{gathered}i\mathfrak{pu}^{d_{1},d_{2}}_{d+1}\times i\mathfrak{pu}^{d_{1},d_{2}}_{d+1}\mathcal{V}_{a}\times i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1}+1}\times\mathcal{V}_{b}\times i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{2}+1}\to\mathcal{V}_{ab}\times i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}\\
(M,N,((u_{1},\Sigma_{1},F_{1}),\eta_{1}),\eta^{\prime}_{1},H_{1},((u_{2},\Sigma_{2},F_{2}),\eta_{2}),\eta^{\prime}_{2},H_{2})\mapsto\\
((u_{1}\#u_{2},\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2},F_{1}\#g_{H}(M)F_{2}),\eta_{1}+g_{H}(M)\eta_{2}),\eta_{1}^{\prime}+g_{H}(M)\eta_{2}^{\prime},H^{\prime}+N)\end{gathered}

(87)

where
H^{\prime}=H(H_{1},H_{2})
.

This proves that
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon,\epsilon}_{3}
is equivalent to a stabilization of
\mathcal{K}_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\mathcal{K}^{2}_{\epsilon}
.

The final relation that needs to be shown is that
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{3}
is induced to
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda_{d}}
under the inclusion
U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})\subset PU(d+1)
. The only nontrivial point is to see that the induction acts trivially on the
i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
factor, which follows from Lemma
6
.

∎

4
Compatibly smoothing global charts

In this section, we show that we can stabilize the system of Kuranishi charts described in Section
3.4
such that after the stabilization, the new system of Kuranishi charts admits compatible smooth structures. The basic challenge is to extend equivariant stable smoothing theory the setting of of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds.

4.1
Brief review of microbundles

Definition 14
.

A microbundle of rank
n
over a topological space
X
is a diagram
\{X\xrightarrow{s}E\xrightarrow{p}X\}
where
E
is a topological space, and
p
and
s
are continuous maps (called the
projection
and the
section
, respectively) such that
p\circ s=id_{X}
, and moreover for each
x\in X
, there are neighborhoods
x\in U\subset X
and
s(x)\in V\subset E
, as well as a homeomorphism
h:U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to V
with
p\circ h=pr_{U}
, and
h|_{U\times\{0\}}=s
. The space
E
is the
total space
of the microbundle. When the context is clear, the we will abbreviate the microbundle as
E
.

A morphism of microbundles is an equivalence class of continuous map between the total spaces which commute with the projections and the sections, respectively. Two such maps are equivalent if they agree in a neighborhood of the image of the section.

Given a vector bundle
\pi:V\to X
, the
associated microbundle
is
\{X\xrightarrow{0}V\xrightarrow{\pi}X
, which we will also denote by
V
. Given a microbundle
E
, a vector bundle
V
together with an isomorphism of the associated microbundle
V\to E
is called a
vector bundle lift
of
E
.

Given a pair of microbundles
E_{1},E_{2}
over
X
, the direct sum
E_{1}\oplus E_{2}
is the microbundle

X\xrightarrow{s_{1}\times s_{2}\circ\Delta}(p_{1}\times p_{2})^{-1}(\Delta(X))\xrightarrow{p_{1}\times p_{2}}X.

(88)

where the total space is a subspace of
E_{1}\times E_{2}
.

Given a map of spaces
f:X\to Y
and a microbundle
E
over
Y
, the pullback microbundle
f^{*}E
is given by

X\xrightarrow{x\mapsto(s(f(x),x)}f^{*}E=\{(e,x)\in E\times X|p(e)=f(x)\}\xrightarrow{(e,x)\mapsto x}X.

(89)

These notions admit equivariant versions. Here,
G
is a compact Lie group.

Definition 15
.

Let
X
be a
G
-space. A
G
-microbundle on
X
is a microbundle
X\xrightarrow{s}E\xrightarrow{p}X
where
E
is a
G
-space and such that
s
and
p
are
G
-equivariant maps. A morphism between microbundles
E_{1},E_{2}
over
X
is a morphism of microbundles such that the map between total spaces is
G
-equivariant. The direct sum (
88
) of two
G
-microbundles is a
G
-microbundle via restricting the diagonal action of
G
on
E_{1}\times E_{2}
to
E_{1}\oplus E_{2}
.

The associated microbundle of a
G
-vector bundle over a
G
-space is a
G
-microbundle; correspondingly, a
G
-vector bundle lift of a
G
-microbundle
E
is a
G
-vector bundle which has an associated
G
-microbundle equipped with an isomorphism to
E
.

Definition 16
.

Let
X
be a topological manifold. The
tangent microbundle

T_{\mu}X
is the microbundle
X\xrightarrow{\Delta}X\times X\xrightarrow{p_{1}}X
, where
\Delta
is the diagonal map and
p_{1}
is projection to the first factor. If
G
is a topological
G
-manifold
T_{\mu}X
is a
G
-microbundle via the diagonal action on
X\times X
.

If
X
is a smooth manifold, then the exponential map gives a canonical vector bundle lift
TX
of
T_{\mu}X
. If
X
is further equipped with a smooth
G
-action, then, by choosing a
G
-equivariant Riemannian metric and utilizing the
G
-equivariance of the exponential map, this vector bundle lift becomes a
G
-vector bundle lift. These lifts are canonical up to contractible choice, since the space of (
G
-equivariant) Riemannian metrics on
X
is contractible. In particular, any pair of lifts is isotopic in the following sense:

Definition 17
.

Let
X
be a space,
E\to X
be a microbundle, and
V\to X
be a vector bundle. An isotopy of vector bundle lifts
\phi_{0}:V_{0}\to E
,
\phi_{1}:V_{1}\to E
is a vector bundle lift
\phi:V\to\pi^{*}E
, where
\pi:M\times[0,1]\to M
is the projection, such that we have isomorphisms
V|_{M\times\{i\}}\simeq V_{i}
which compose with
\phi|_{M\times\{i\}}
to give
\phi_{i}
, for
i=0,1
.

The basic theorem of equivariant stable smoothing theory is

Proposition 5

(Lashof
[
Las79
]
)
.

Let
M
be a topological manifold equipped with a continuous action of
G
. Assume that

1.

There are finitely many orbit types, and

2.

The microbundle
T_{\mu}M
admits a
G
-vector bundle lift
E
.

Then there is a
G
-representation
V
or which the product
V\times M
admits a
G
-equivariant smooth structure. Any two such smooth structures are
stably slice concordant
in the following sense. Given any two such smoothings of
V_{0}\times M
and
V_{1}\times M
associated to isotopic vector bundle lifts
E_{0}\to T_{\mu}M
, and
E_{1}\to T_{\mu}M
, there is a vector space
V^{\prime}\subset V_{0}\oplus V_{1}
and a smooth structure on
V^{\prime}\times M\times[0,1]
such that
the smooth structure on
V^{\prime}\times M\times\{j\}
is induced from that on
V_{j}\times M
for
j=0,1
, and such that the projection to
[0,1]
is a submersion.

We will unpack this result and generalize it to the setting of topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds.

4.2
Smoothing of
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds

We first define the tangent microbundle of a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold.

Definition 18
.

Given a topological
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold
M
, we define its tangent microbundle
T_{\mu}M
to the be the pullback of
T_{\mu}\mathcal{D}(M)
under the canonical inclusion
M\to\mathcal{D}(M)
.

Definition 19
.

Let
M
,
B
be topological
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds and let
\pi:M\to B
be a map of
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds. Let
p\in M
and let
b=\pi(p)\in B
. A
product neighborhood
of
p
is a homeomorphism
\iota:W\to W\cap\pi^{-1}(b)\times\pi(W)
, where
W
is a neighborhood of
p
satisfying

•

\pi\circ\iota^{-1}
is the projection to
\pi(W)
, and

•

\pi(W)
is an open subset of
B
, and

•

\iota|_{W\cap\pi^{-1}(b)}:W\cap\pi^{-1}(b)\to\{b\}
is the identity map, and

•

We have that
\iota(W\cap M(S))=W\cap\pi^{-1}(b)\times\pi(W)\cap B(S)
for each
S\in 2^{[k]}
.

We say that
\pi
is a
topological submersion
if every point in
M
admits a product neighborhood. In that case, the fibers of
\pi
are manifestly
topological manifolds
, and the
vertical tangent microbundle

T^{vt}_{\mu}(\pi)
of
\pi
is the microbundle

M\xrightarrow{\Delta}M\times_{B}M\xrightarrow{\pi\times\pi}M

(90)

where
M\times_{B}M
is the fiber product of
\pi
with itself,
\Delta
is the diagonal, and
\pi\times\pi
is the canonical map out of the fiber product. This is manifestly a
G
-equivariant microbundle under the diagonal action of
G
on the total space.

By looking at doubled charts and using the functoriality of doubling, one sees that

Lemma 30
.

Let
\pi:M\to B
be a topological submersion. Then the natural map of topological manifolds
\mathcal{D}(\pi):\mathcal{D}(M)\to\mathcal{D}(B)
is a topological submersion.

There is a natural map
\tau\mathcal{D}(pi):T_{\mu}\mathcal{D}(M)\to T_{\mu}\mathcal{D}(B)
defined in
[
AMS21
, Eq. 4.11, Corollary 4.26]
, and the restriction of this map to
M
defines a map
\tau:T_{\mu}M\to T_{\mu}B
.

Proposition 6
.

There is a map
P:T_{\mu}M\to T^{vt}_{\mu}M
such that
P\oplus\tau:T_{\mu}M\to T^{vt}_{\mu}M\oplus\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B
is an isomorphism.

The rest of this subsection is dedicated to proving this proposition. The arguments are a direct adaptation of
[
AMS21
, Section 4.4]
.

Each
g\in G
sends a product neighborhood
\iota
of
p
to a new product neighborhood
g_{*}\iota
of
g\cdot p
given by the formula

g_{*}i:g(W)\to g(W\cap\pi^{-1}(b))\times g(\pi(W)),g_{*}(\iota)(w)=g\iota(g^{-1}\cdot w).

(91)

Definition 20
.

A
G_{p}
-invariant product neighborhood is a product neighborhood of
p\in M
which is
G_{p}
-invariant under the action above. We say that
G
is a
G
-equivariant topological submersion
if every point
p\in M
admits a
G_{p}
invariant product neighborhood.

Definition 21
.

We say that
G
acts on
\mathbb{R}^{n}

linearly relative to
v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
if the the action becomes linear after translating
v
to
0
.

A
G
-action on a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold is
locally linear
if for each point
p
in this manifold there is a chart around
p
such that
G_{p}
acts linearly in this chart relative to the image of
p
; such a chart is called a
linear chart
. We say that
\pi
is
fiberwise locally linear
if it is a topological submersion, and if for each
b\in B
, the group
G_{b}
is locally linear on the manifold
\pi^{-1}(b)
.

By using the existence of collars
G
-invariant collars of
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds (Lemma
3
), we have

Lemma 31
.

Given a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
B
, the
G
-action on
B
is
strongly linear
in the sense that for each
p\in B(S)
, there exists a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold chart around
p
from
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-\ell}
such that
G
acts
trivially
on the coordinates of
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}_{+}
.

In particular, the lemma above implies that the action of
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
is linear on the double is linear.

Proof of Proposition
6
.

Apply apply
[
AMS21
, Prop. 4.25, Corollary 4.26]
to the topological submersion
\mathcal{D}(M)\to\mathcal{D}(B)
to produce a natural morphism of microbundles
P:T_{\mu}\mathcal{D}(M)\to T^{vt}_{\mathcal{D}(\pi)}\mathcal{D}(B)
such that
P\oplus\tau\mathcal{D}(\pi):T_{\mu}\mathcal{D}(M)\to T^{vt}_{\mathcal{D}(\pi)}\mathcal{D}(B)\oplus\mathcal{D}(\pi)^{*}T_{\mu}\mathcal{D}(B)
is an isomorphism. Restrict these maps to to
M
along the inclusion into
\mathcal{D}(M)
.
∎

∎

Fiberwise
C^{1}_{loc}
structures and smoothing
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds

We assume that we have topological submersion of topological
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifolds
\pi:M\to B
such that the fibers of
\pi
(which are topological manifolds) are each equipped with smooth structures. The definition
[
AMS21
, Definition 4.27]
of a pair of
C^{1}_{loc}
-compatible product neighborhoods of
\pi
adapts verbatim to this setting, as do the subsequent notions of a
fiberwise-smooth
C^{1}_{loc}

G
-bundle
, and
vertical tangent bundle

T^{vt}M
of a
C^{1}_{loc}

G
-bundle
\pi
. Moreover, the proof of
[
AMS21
, Lemma 4.29]
adapts verbatim to this setting, establishing that for
\pi
which are fiberwise-smooth
C^{1}_{loc}

G
-bundles, there is a
G
-equivariant lift of the vertical tangent microbundle to the vertical tangent bundle.

Proposition 7
.

Let
\pi:M\to B
be a
G
-equivariant topological submersion of
\langle k\rangle
-manifolds, where
B
is in fact smooth. Moreover, suppose
\pi
has a fiberwise smooth
C^{1}_{loc}
structure. Then there is a
G
-representation
V
such that
M\times V
has the structure of a smooth
\langle k\rangle
-manifold.

Proof.

The map
\mathcal{D}(\pi):\mathcal{D}(M)\to\mathcal{D}(B)
is a
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-equivariant topological submersion of topological manifolds, and it is immediate to verify that te
C^{1}_{loc}
-compatible product neighborhoods for
\pi
induce corresponding
C^{1}_{loc}
-compatible product neighborhoods for
\mathcal{D}(\pi)
. By Proposition
6
we have an isomorphism of microbundles
T_{\mu}\mathcal{D}(M)\to T^{vt}_{\mathcal{D}(\pi})\oplus\mathcal{D}(\pi)^{*}T_{\mu}B
. We have lifts of both of these latter microbundles to vector bundles by
[
AMS21
, Lemma 4.29]
and Proposition
1
. We conclude by applying Theorem
5
.
∎

By combining Propositions
7
and
3
with Lemma
8
, we have

Lemma 32
.

There exists an elementary stabilization
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d},1}
of the Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
by a faithful complex
PU(d+1)
representation such that
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d},1}
admits a smooth structure.

4.3
Normally complex structures on Kuranishi charts

We now explain how to put normally complex structures on the derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifolds associated to the smoothed Kuranishi charts produced by Lemma
32
, as well as the compatility conditions between them.

4.3.1
Normally complex structures on global Kuranishi charts

Given a vector bundle
V
over a
G
-space
N
, we say that
V
is
G
-trivial when for each
x\in N
,
Stab(x)\subset G
acts trivially on
V_{x}
. Moreover, when
N
is smooth and
G
, a compact Lie group, acts with finite stabizers, there is a subvector bundle
\mathfrak{g}\times N\subset TN
with
(\mathfrak{g}\times N)_{x}
given by the tangent space to the
G
-orbit at
x
.

We note first that if a derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold chart arises from a smooth global Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}=(G,N,V,\sigma)
, then to endow
[N/G]
with a normal complex structure it suffices to write a decomposition into
G
-vector bundles
TN=\mathring{TN}\oplus\check{TN}
, where
\mathring{TN}_{x}
is
G
-trivial while
\check{TN}/(\mathfrak{g}\times N)
is a complex
G
-vector bundle. Similarly, to endow
[V/G]
has a normal complex structure it suffices make
V
into a complex
G
-vector bundle. We will call the data of such a decomposition of
TN
together with the complex structures on
\check{TN}
and on
V
the data of a
normal complex structure on a (smooth) global Kuranishi chart.

4.3.2
Normally complex structure on a single Kuranishi chart
T_{\Lambda_{d}}
.

Recall that in the topological Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}=(PU(d+1),T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}},\mathcal{V}_{d}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1},\sigma_{\Lambda_{d}}\oplus\sigma_{H}),
there is a topological submersion
\pi:T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}\to\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
. Moreover, the tangent microbundle of
T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
splits as
T_{\mu}^{vt}T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}\oplus\pi^{*}T_{\mu}\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
. We have lifts of each of these summands to (topological) vector bundles; namely,
\pi^{*}T_{\mu}\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
lifts to
\pi^{*}T\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
, while
T_{\mu}^{vt}T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
lifts to the vector bundle
T^{vt}T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
fiber at
((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)\in T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
given by the kernel of the surjective Fredholm operator (
74
).

Moreover, after smoothing, the tangent bundle of the smoothed chart is isomorphic to (as a topological vector bundle) of this vector bundle, so it suffices to decompose this vector bundle into a direct sum of a
G
-trivial and a complex
G
-vector bundle. The vector bundle
V_{d}
is complex. Moreover, after gluing a pair of
u
-independent Cauchy-Riemann operators (the linearized Cauchy Riemann operators associated to a pair of fixed caps of the Hamiltonian chords at the endpoints) onto the family of operators
((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)\mapsto D_{u}
, we can deform the entire family of these operators to a family complex linear operators as the complex antilinear-term is order
0

[
Fuk+09
]
. This implies that
\mathbb{R}^{m}\oplus T^{vt}T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
is a complex-linear
PU(d+1)
-vector bundle for some large
m
, where
PU(d+1)
acts trivially on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
. This in turn implies that there is some
PU(d+1)
-trivial copy of a trivial vector bundle
\mathbb{R}^{m}\subset T^{vt}T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
such that the complement is a complex
PU(d+1)
-subbundle. Thus,
T^{vt}T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
has the desired decomposition.

Furthermore
T\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
splits as
\theta\oplus(\pi_{2})^{*}T\Bl\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
(see Lemma
23
where
\pi_{2}
is denoted by
\pi
), where
\theta
is a
1
-dimensional vector bundle vector bundle given by the vertical tangent bundle of
\Theta_{-}
. We claim that
\theta
is
G
-trivial; indeed, the complex structure at
p_{-}
gives a preferred orientation of the fibers of
\Theta_{-}
, and thus a preferred trivialization of
\eta
, and one sees that
PU(d+1)
acts trivially on the fibers of
\eta
with respect to this trivialization. Moreover,
The bundle
T\Bl\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
is a complex
G
-vector bundle: indeed,
T\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
is a complex
G
-vector bundle since
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
is a complex manifold, and the complex extends to the inverse image of the blow-up locus by requiring that in a complex chart on
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
in which the stratification looks like
\mathbb{C}^{\ell}\times{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d-1}_{+}
, in the corresponding chart modelled on
\mathbb{C}^{\ell}\times(S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0})^{d-1}
on
\Bl\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
, the coordinate vector along the
i
-th
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
factor is sent by the complex structure to clockwise rotation along the
i
-th
S^{1}
-factor.

Thus, stabilizing the global Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
by the
PU(d+1)
-representation
\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
, we get a global Kuranishi chart
(G,N,\mathcal{V}_{d}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}\oplus\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1},\sigma_{\Lambda_{d}}\oplus\sigma_{H}\times id)
where
N=T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}\times\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}.
Now
TN/\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}\times N\simeq TT^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
has the desired decomposition into a
G
-trivial and an equivariantly complex vector bundle. Similarly,
\mathcal{V}_{d}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}\oplus\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
is now a complex
PU(d+1)
-vector bundle via the canonical complex structure on
\mathcal{V}_{d}
and the action of
\mathbb{C}
on
\mathbb{C}\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
. Thus the stabilization of any global Kuranishi chart produced by Lemma
32
is
canonically
normally complex oriented.

4.3.3
Normally complex structures and induction/restriction relations.

We now argue that the inductions, restrictions, and stabilizations of Proposition
4
become compatible with the normal complex structures described above after stabilizing
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
by
\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
.

We now argue that these canonical normal complex structures are compatible in the sense described below.

First, a product of normally complex oriented global Kuranishi charts is normally complex.

Second, when we take the restriction of
\mathfrak{T}_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}
(or any stabilization of this chart by a complex vector bundle) along the inclusion
U(d)\subset PU(d+1)
, there the corresponding restriction of the stabilization of
\mathfrak{T}_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}
by
\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
is the stabilization of the restriction by
\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
by Lemma
6
. In particular, smoothed stabilizations of
\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}
by complex vector bundles )as in (
80
), (
81
) become normally complex after stabilizing by
\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{i}+1}
for
i=1,2
. These latter charts are normally complex because the complement of
\mathfrak{u}_{d}
in
\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
canonically a complex vector space. In particular, the smoothings of the charts
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda_{d_{i}}}
and
\mathcal{K}_{i}
after stabilization by complex vector bundles and by
\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
will correspond to the same normally complex derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold.

Similarly, when we induce a smoothed stabilization of
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{3}
as in (
83
) by a complex bundle along the inclusion
U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})\subset PU(d_{1}+d_{2}+1)
to produce a corresponding smoothed stabilization of
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}}
, stabilizing both of these charts by
\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
will preserve the induction relation, will make them both normally complex and will make the corresponding normally complex derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifolds equal.

Next, we study the map (
86
) and its behavior upon stabilizing by
\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1}+1}\oplus\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{2}+1}
. First, we note that
V^{tr}_{d,d_{1}}/U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})
is the moduli space of
d_{2}
-dimensional subspaces containing a fixed line and transverse to another
d_{1}
-dimensional subspace containing that line, and this has a complex structure. In fact, this moduli space is biholomorphic to
i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1},d_{2}}
, where we view this latter space
canonically
as a complex vector space via the complex-structure on its upper-right entries; indeed, the fact that the exponential of a Hermitian matrix is Hermitian shows that
M\mapsto g_{id}(M)
induces such a biholomorphism.

Moreover, write
(i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1},d_{2}})^{\perp}\subset i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
for the complement; then the map
i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1}+1}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{2}+1}\mapsto(i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1},d_{2}})^{\perp}
,
(H_{1},H_{2})\mapsto H(H_{1},H_{2})
(
85
) is a linear isomorphism. Write
-i(i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1},d_{2}})^{\perp}
for the image of
(i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1},d_{2}})^{\perp}
under multiplicaton by
-i
; then we also have an isomorphism

\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1}+1}\oplus\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{2}+1}\to-i(i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1},d_{2}})^{\perp}
,
(H_{1}^{\prime},H_{2}^{\prime})\mapsto-iH(iH^{\prime}_{1},iH^{\prime}_{2})
, and the sum of this map with
H
is complex linear.

Thus, extending (
86
) to

\begin{gathered}\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1}+1}\times\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{2}+1}\times i\mathfrak{pu}^{d_{1},d_{2}}_{d+1}\times T^{\epsilon}_{1}\times T^{\epsilon}_{2}\to-i(i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1},d_{2}})^{\perp}\times T^{\epsilon,\epsilon}_{3}\\
(H_{1}^{\prime},H_{2}^{\prime},M,((u_{1},\Sigma_{1},F_{1}),\eta_{1}),((u_{2},\Sigma_{2},F_{2}),\eta_{2}))\mapsto(-iH(iH^{\prime}_{1},iH^{\prime}_{2}),(u_{1}\#u_{2},\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2},F_{1}\#_{g_{H}(M)}F_{2}),(\eta_{1}+g_{H}(M)\eta_{2}))\end{gathered}

(92)

induces, after stabilizing, a map preserving the decompositions into
U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})
-trivial bundles and the complex structures on tangent bundles modulo
\mathfrak{u}_{d_{1}}\times\mathfrak{u}_{d_{2}}
. The above map is naturally covered by the stabilization

\begin{gathered}\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1}+1}\times\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{2}+1}\times i\mathfrak{pu}^{d_{1},d_{2}}_{d+1}\times i\mathfrak{pu}^{d_{1},d_{2}}_{d+1}\times\mathcal{V}_{a}\times i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1}+1}\times\mathcal{V}_{b}\times i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{2}+1}\to-i(i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1},d_{2}})^{\perp}\times\mathcal{V}_{ab}\times i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}\\
(H_{1}^{\prime},H_{2}^{\prime},M,N,((u_{1},\Sigma_{1},F_{1}),\eta_{1}),\eta^{\prime}_{1},H_{1},((u_{2},\Sigma_{2},F_{2}),\eta_{2}),\eta^{\prime}_{2},H_{2})\mapsto\\
(-iH(H^{\prime}_{1},H^{\prime}_{2}),(u_{1}\#u_{2},\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2},F_{1}\#g_{H}(M)F_{2}),\eta_{1}+g_{H}(M)\eta_{2}),\eta_{1}^{\prime}+g_{H}(M)\eta_{2}^{\prime},H(H_{1},H_{2})+N).\end{gathered}

(93)

This map is fiberwise
\mathbb{C}
-linear as a map of vector bundles. The coefficients of the map may not depend smoothly on the base parameter since
H
does not depend smoothly on the underlying pre-Floer trajectory. Nonetheless, as we we will be inductively smoothing later, we will use this map to
induce
a smooth structure on the target, and then textend, so that the final map is smooth,

The remaining salient relation between these various charts is that
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon,\epsilon}_{3}
can then be stabilized by further by
\mathfrak{pu}^{d_{1},d_{2}}_{d+1}
, which is naturally a complex vector bundle, to get the stabilization of
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon,\epsilon}_{3}
by
\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}
. We can then include it into the corresponding stabilization of
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{3}
and then induce along the map
U(d_{1})\times U(d_{2})\subset PU(d+1)
to produce the desired stabilization of
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda_{d}}^{\epsilon}
.

4.4
Compatibly smoothing Kuranishi charts

We now describe how to make make the smoothings of the distinct Kuranishi charts compatible with one another. First we introduce a construction which will allow us to utilize a slightly upgraded version of stable
G
-smoothing (Theorem
5
): in essence, proving a multi-parameter version of uniqueness of smoothings up to concordance and stabilization.

Definition 22
.

Given a Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}=(G,T,E,s)
, we define the collaring of the Kuranishi chart
Coll(\mathcal{K})
to be
(G,Coll(T),Coll(E),Coll(s))
.

Let
M
be a topological
G-\langle k\rangle
-manifold and with a lift of the tangent microbundle. Consider
M\times[0,1]^{\ell}
, which is a topological
G-\langle k+\ell\rangle
-manifold.

Definition 23
.

We say that a
parameterized smooth structure
on
M\times[0,1]^{\ell}
is a
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-equivariant smooth structure on the
\langle\ell\rangle
-manifold
\mathcal{D}(M)\times[0,1]^{\ell}
for which the projection to
[0,1]^{\ell}
is a submersion of
\langle\ell\rangle
-manifolds.

Lemma
4
and the functoriality of doubling immediately imply

Lemma 33
.

A parametrized smooth structure on
