M\times[0,1]^{\ell}
gives the topological
G-\langle k+\ell\rangle
-manifold a canonical smooth structure characterized by the property that the pullbacks of smooth functions on
\mathcal{D}(M)\times[0,1]^{\ell}
to
M\times[0,1]^{\ell}
are smooth. With respect to this smooth structure, the projection to
[0,1]^{\ell}
is a submersion from a
\langle k+\ell\rangle
-manifold to a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold with
S_{f}=[n]
and
\tilde{f}(r)=r-n
(see Definition
1
).
∎

Definition 24
.

[
AM11
]

A
p-submanifold
of a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold
M
of dimension
m
is a subset
N\subset M
such that for any
x\in N
there is a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold chart
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S(x)_{+}}\times\mathbb{R}^{m+k-|S(x)|}\supset U\to V\subset M
such that
\psi^{-1}(V\cap N)=U\cap\mathbb{R}^{[k]\setminus S(x)_{+}}(T_{\psi})\times\mathbb{R}^{m+k-|S(x)|}
for some subset
T_{\psi}\subset[k-|S(x)|]
.

If a
p
-submanifold of a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold is naturally a topological
\langle k\rangle
-manifold with the induced stratification, then it inherits a unique smooth structure (as the stratification in fact determines the
T_{\psi}
in the definition above).

Proposition 8
.

Fix natural numbers
k,\ell
. Write
\vec{0}=(0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
and
\vec{1}=(1,\ldots,1)\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
.

Suppose that we have specified patermetrized smooth strutures on
M\times C_{i}
for some collection
C_{1},\ldots,C_{r}\subset[0,1]^{\ell}
of faces of the cube, as well as parameterized smooth structures on
M(S)\times[0,1]^{\ell}
for all non-maximal
S\in 2^{[k]}
, all of which which restrict to one another. Then there is a smooth structure on
M\times[0,1]^{k}\times R(\sigma)
, for some
G
-representation
R(\sigma)
, such that
M\times\vec{0}\times R(\sigma)
,
M\times\vec{1}\times R(\sigma)
, and
M(S)\times[0,1]^{k}\times R(\sigma)
are
p
-submanifolds and the smooth structures on them are induced from the given smooth structures.

Proof.

We follow
[
Las79
, pp. 302]
. We can find a
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-representation
R(\sigma)
such that we have smooth equivariant embeddings of
\mathcal{D}(M(S))\to R(\sigma)
which restrict to one another. After stabilizing
\sigma
further we can find smooth equivariant embeddings of
\mathcal{D}(M(S))\times[0,1]^{\ell}\to R(\sigma)\times[0,1]^{\ell}
which commute with the projections to
[0,1]^{\ell}
and induce the fiberwise smooth structures on each strata and restrict to one another.

After stabilizing
\sigma
further we can inductively (along the face lattice of the cube) extend the embeddings
\partial M\times C_{j}
into
R(\sigma)\times C_{j}
to smooth equivariant embeddings of
M\times C_{j}
for specified faces
C_{j}
of
[0,1]^{\ell}
.

After stabilizing
\sigma
further we can find a continuous equivariant embedding of
\mathcal{D}(M)\times[0,1]^{\ell}
to
R(\sigma)\times[0,1]^{\ell}
commuting with the projections, restricting to stabilizations of all previously chosen embeddings on the subsets where they have been defined. Moreover by additional stabilization we can arrange such that there is a neighborhood
U
of the image of
\mathcal{D}(M)\times[0,1]^{\ell}
equipped with a projection map
U\to\mathcal{D}(M)\times[0,1]^{\ell}
commuting with the projections to
[0,1]^{\ell}
such that the embedding into
U
and the projection defines a
G\times(\mathbb{Z}/2)^{k}
-microbundles over
\mathcal{D}(M)\times[0,1]^{\ell}
which are smooth vector bundles over the part of the domain with a smooth structure.

We can find a family of lifts of the tangent microbundle
\lambda_{t}:\xi\to\tau\mathcal{D}(M)
,
t\in[0,1]^{k}
, such that
\lambda_{\vec{0}}
and
\lambda_{\vec{1}}
agree with lifts of the tangent microbundle associated to the smooth structures specified on
\mathcal{D}(M)\times\vec{0}
and
\mathcal{D}(M)\times\vec{1}
, and such that there are embeddings
\iota^{S}_{t}:T\mathcal{D}(M)(S)\to\xi
such that
\lambda_{t}\iota^{S}_{t}\subset\tau\mathcal{D}(M)(S)\subset\tau\mathcal{D}
and such that are lifts of of the tangent microbundles of
\mathcal{D}(M)(S)\times t
associated to the given smooth structures.

Clearly
U
has a smooth structure. Thus, so does
\pi^{*}\xi
by by Lemma
8
since the latter is a vector bundle over a
\langle k\rangle
-manifold. We will show that an open neighborhood in
\pi^{*}\xi
is equivariantly homeomorphic
\mathcal{D}(M)\times[0,1]^{\ell}\times R(\sigma)
. In fact, one simply performs the construction in
[
Las79
, pp. 302]
which we explicate for convenience. Given a point
x\in\mathcal{D}(M)
, an element
t\in[0,1]^{k}
, and a point
v\in R(\sigma)
, we can think of this as a point of
TU|_{\mathcal{D}(M)\times t}
and apply the exponential map to get an element of
U
if
v
was sufficiently small. This gives us another element
y\times t\in\mathcal{D}(M)\times[0,1]^{k}
via the projection
U\to\mathcal{D}(M)\times[0,1]^{k}
, and an element of the fiber of
U
over
y\times t
. If
v
was sufficiently small then
(y,x)\in\tau\mathcal{D}(M)
will lie in the image of
\lambda_{t}
, and so applying
\lambda_{t}
gives us an equivariant homeomorphism between an open subset of the zero section of
\mathcal{D}(M)\times[0,1]^{k}\times R(\sigma)
and
\pi^{*}\xi
. Thus
\mathcal{D}(M)\times[0,1]^{k}\times R(\sigma)
has a smooth structure. The construction shows that the map to
[0,1]^{k}
is a submersion. The compatibility conditions follow from the fact that
U
is a vector bundle in the regions where smooth structures have been specified, and from the compatibility conditions imposed on
\lambda_{t}
.

∎

Lemma 34
.

There exist stabilizations
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{d}}
of
Coll(\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda_{d}})
such that
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{d}}
admit normal complex structures and such that for each
(d_{j})_{j=1}^{r},\sum_{j}d_{j}=d
, writing
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{d}}//G
for the associated normally complex derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold,
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{d}}//G)(S(d_{1},\ldots,d_{r}))
is, as a normal complex derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold, normally complex diffeomorphic to a stabilization of
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{d_{1}}}//G\times\ldots\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{d_{r}}}//G
by a complex vector bundle.

Proof.

We first replace all
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda_{d}}
by the stabilizations by complex vector bundles
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d},1}
arising from Lemma
32
, and then further stabilize them to
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d},2}
by
\mathfrak{pu}_{d}
such that they come equipped with canonical normally complex structures as in Section
4.3.2
. The collars of these new charts will admit normally complex structures after they are smoothed, as normally-complex structures are a condition on the tangent bundle and obstruction bundle of a global Kuranishi chart and collaring manifestly preserves this condition.

We now apply Propostion
8
inductively
d
and on the posets
[d-1]
for each
d
to extend our smoothings of
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d},2}
to
Coll(\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d},2})
and achieve the compatibility condition of the Lemma. The case
d=1
is already complete, as
Coll(\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{1},2})=\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{1},2}=:\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{1}}
. Assume that for all
1\leq\ell<d
, we have smooth Kuranishi charts
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{\ell}}
satisfying the condition. Now Proposition
4
and the discussion in Section
4.3.3
shows how to realize a stabilization of
Coll(\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}},2)(S_{(}d_{1},\ldots,d_{r}))
from
Coll(\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d_{1}},2})
, …,
Coll(\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d_{r}},2})
by taking products, stabilizations by complex vector spaces, taking open subsets, and equivalences. These previous charts have been smoothed earlier so these charts come equipped with smooth structures; we have smoothed the chart everywhere except the interior of the collar. By applying Proposition
2
inductively for each
S\in 2^{[d-1]}\setminus[d-1]
, there is a stabilization
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{d}}
of
Coll(\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}})
by a faithful complex
PU(d+1)
-representation such that the smooth structures specified everywhere except in the interior of the collar are extended to a smooth structure on the whole chart. In particular, we still have
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{d}}(S(d_{1},\ldots,d_{r}))
arising from
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{d_{j}}}
(which are themselves stabilizations of collars of
\mathcal{K}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d},2}
) by products, inductions, restrictions, stabiliations, and difeomorphisms. We conclude by passing to associated derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts.

∎

5
Floer complex from Kuranishi charts via strongly transverse perturbations

We now develop some axiomatics for compatibly perturbing the defining sections of a compatible system of normally complex derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts.

Categories of spaces and orbifolds

Let
Fin
denote the monoidal category with objects given by finite sets, morphisms given by injective maps, and monoidal product given by disjoint union of finite sets. We enlarge
Fin
to a category
Fin^{\prime}
by adding on the object
\bullet
, which can be thought of informally as the “set with
-1
element”. This object is defined by the properties that
Fin^{\prime}(\bullet,S)=Fin^{\prime}(S,\bullet)=\emptyset
, and
\bullet\otimes S=S\otimes\bullet=\bullet
.

Let
Top^{Fin^{\prime}}
be the monoidal category with objects given by pairs
(S,\mathcal{M})
with
S\in Fin^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{M}
a
2^{S}
-space, with
2^{\bullet}
spaces required to be empty, such that that the maximal open stratum of
\mathcal{M}
is nonempty if
S\neq\bullet
; with morphisms
(S,\mathcal{M}_{S})\to(T,\mathcal{M}_{T})
given by a map
f:S\to T
in
FinSet^{\prime}
, and a map
\bar{f}:\mathcal{M}_{S}\to\mathcal{M}_{T}
which is a homeomorphism onto its image
\mathcal{M}_{T}(f(S))
, which induces homeomorphisms
\mathcal{M}_{S}(S^{\prime})\to\mathcal{M}_{T}(f(S^{\prime}))
for
S^{\prime}\subset S
. The monoidal structure structure is given by
(S,\mathcal{M}_{S})\times(T,\mathcal{M}_{T})=(S\cup T,\mathcal{M}_{S}\times\mathcal{M}_{T})
. Note that
(\emptyset,\{*\})
is the unit object for this monoidal structure.

We declare the unique derived
\bullet
-orbifold to be the derived
\langle 0\rangle
-orbifold with the empty thickening and empty orbibundle, which we call the empty orbifold. Let
DerOrb^{\prime}
be the monoidal category consisting
of

•

Objects given by pairs
(S,\widetilde{\mathcal{K}})
, with
S\in Fin^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}
an
S
-orbifold, such that that the maximal open stratum of
\mathcal{M}
is nonempty if
S\neq\bullet
, and

•

morphisms
(S,\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{S})\to(T,\widetilde{\mathcal{K}})
are given by maps
f:S\to T
together with a diffeomorphism of
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{T}(f(S))
with a stabilization of
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{S}
by a vector bundle.

The monoidal structure is given by the monoidal structure on
FinSet^{\prime}
and the product of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifolds.

Similarly, we have the monoidal category
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}
with objects
(S,\widetilde{\mathcal{K}})
as in
DerOrb^{\prime}
but with
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}
equipped with a normal complex structure, and for which morphisms
(S,\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{S})\to(T,\widetilde{\mathcal{K}})
are as in
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}
but with
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{T}(f(S))
now identified via normally complex diffeomorpism from a stabilization of
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}
by a
complex
vector bundle. We have forgetful monoidal functors
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}\to DerOrb^{\prime}\to Top^{Fin^{\prime}}
. As usual, we will at times implicitly identify elements of
DerOrb^{\prime}
, respectively
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}
, with those corresponding to replacing the underlying
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold by an open subset
U
containing the zero set of the Kuranishi section, and replacing the vector bundle and the section by their restrictions to
U
.

Topological flow categories

Given a category
\mathcal{C}
enriched in one of
Top(Fin^{\prime}),DerOrb^{\prime}
, or
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}
, we will use slightly nonstandard notation and say that the morphism object representing maps from
\tilde{x}
to
\tilde{y}
is the tuple
(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}}),\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}))
, and we will call the elements of
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
the
morphisms
from
\tilde{x}
to
\tilde{y}
. Moreover, given a sequence
\tilde{x}_{0},\ldots,\tilde{x}_{r}\in\mathcal{C}
with
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x}_{j},\tilde{x}_{j+1})
nonempty, we will write
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}}(\tilde{x}_{0},\ldots,\tilde{x}_{r})\subset\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x}_{0},\tilde{x}_{r})}
for the image of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x}_{0},\tilde{x}_{1})}\sqcup\ldots\sqcup\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x}_{r-1},\tilde{x}_{r})}
induced by the morphism in
Fin
underlying the composition operation.

Definition 25
.

A topological flow category
\mathcal{C}
is a category enriched in
Top(Fin^{\prime})
such that the morphisms from
\tilde{x}
to
\tilde{x}
are the unit object in
Top(Fin^{\prime})
. We define
|\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})}|=:r(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
if
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq\emptyset
and
\tilde{x}\neq\tilde{y}
.

A
\Pi
-equivariant topological flow category
\mathcal{F}
is a topological flow category with a free
\Pi
-action on objects and morphisms. We say that
\mathcal{F}
is
proper
if
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
is compact for any pair
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in\mathcal{C}^{2}
, and if the set

(\mathcal{C}^{2}/\Pi)_{q}:=\{(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in\mathcal{C}^{2}|\tilde{x}\neq\tilde{y},\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq 0,r(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=q\}/\Pi

(94)

is finite for each natural number
q
. We set
(\mathcal{C}^{2}/\Pi)=\cup_{q=0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}^{2}/\Pi)_{q}
and
(\mathcal{C}^{2}/\Pi)_{<r}=\cup_{q=0}^{r-1}(\mathcal{C}^{2}/\Pi)_{q}
.

A grading on a
\Pi
-equivariant topological flow category
\mathcal{C}
is a map
\mu:Ob(\mathcal{C})\to\mathbb{Z}
such that

\mu(\tilde{x})-\mu(\tilde{y})-1=\mu(g\tilde{x})-\mu(g\tilde{y})-1
for each
g\in\Pi
.

Remark 10
.

To extend the method of this paper to the case of symplectic cohomology with quadratic Hamiltonians, it is necessary to weaken the notion of a proper flow category and to modify the various inductive constructions of this paper appropriately.

Flow categories equipped with charts

Definition 26
.

A
compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts
for a graded proper
\Pi
-equivariant topological flow category
\mathcal{C}
is a category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
enriched in
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}
equipped with a free action of
\Pi
with
Ob(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})=Ob(\mathcal{C})
and such that applying the functor
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}\to Top^{Fin^{\prime}}
recovers
\mathcal{C}
; and such that
vdim(\mathcal{F^{\prime}}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=\mu(\tilde{x}-\mu(\tilde{y})-1
for each
\tilde{x},\tilde{y}\in Ob(\mathcal{C})
.

Given such a
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, for any tuple
\tilde{x}_{0},\ldots,\tilde{x}_{r}
of objects of
\mathcal{F^{\prime}}
with
\mathcal{C}(x_{j},x_{j+1})\neq\emptyset
for
j=0,\ldots,r-1
, we have a map

Ind:(\sigma^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,\sigma^{\prime}_{r-1})\mapsto\sigma^{\prime}

(95)

from tuples of
(\sigma^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,\sigma^{\prime}_{r-1})
, where
s^{\prime}_{j}
is a strongly transverse section of
\mathcal{V}_{j}
for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(\tilde{x}_{j},\tilde{x}_{j+1})=(T_{j},\mathcal{V}_{j},\sigma_{j})
, to strongly transverse sections
\sigma^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{V}
(where
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(\tilde{x}_{0},\tilde{x}_{r})=(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{V},\sigma)
) given by taking products and taking direct sum with the identity section of the stabilizing complex vector bundles, and applying the diffeomorphism induced by the map in
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}
. By construction this map is
associative
in the sense that

Ind(Ind(\sigma^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,\sigma^{\prime}_{r_{1}}),Ind(\sigma^{\prime}_{r_{1}+1},\ldots),\ldots,Ind(\ldots,\sigma^{\prime}_{r-1}))=Ind(\sigma^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,\sigma^{\prime}_{r-1})

(96)

for all tuples
\tilde{x}_{0},\ldots,\tilde{x}_{r}
as above.

Definition 27
.

A
compatible system of perturbations
for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a choice, for every
\tilde{x},\tilde{y}\in\mathcal{C}
with
\tilde{x}\neq\tilde{y}
and
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq\emptyset
, of choice of strongly transverse section
\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
of
\mathcal{F^{\prime}}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
such that

Ind(\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x}_{0},\tilde{x}_{1}),\ldots,\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x}_{r-1},\tilde{x}_{r}))=\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x_{0}},\tilde{x}_{r})|_{T(\tilde{x}_{0},\tilde{x}_{r})(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}}(\tilde{x}_{0},\ldots,\tilde{x}_{r}))}

(97)

for any tuple
\tilde{x}_{0},\ldots,\tilde{x}_{r}
of objects of
\mathcal{F^{\prime}}
with
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x}_{j},\tilde{x}_{j+1})\neq\emptyset
for
j=0,\ldots,r-1
.

We say that a compatible system of perturbations is
\Pi
-equivariant when
g\cdot\sigma(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=\sigma(g\tilde{x},g\tilde{y})
for all
g\in\Pi
and all pairs
\tilde{x},\tilde{y}\in\mathcal{C}
.

A sub-flow-category of a
\Pi
-equivariant topological flow category
\mathcal{C}
is an enriched subcategory
\mathcal{C}_{2}\subset\mathcal{C}
closed under the
\Pi
-action, where
\mathcal{C}_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\subset\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
is an inclusion of connected components for each
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Ob(\mathcal{C}_{2})
. By restricting to open subsets of the derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts, if
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts for
\mathcal{C}
then there is a corresponding compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}
for
\mathcal{C}_{2}
, which (after passing to open susbsets of morphism objects of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
) is a subcategory of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
with morphisms given by connected components of morphisms in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.

Existence of compatible systems of perturbations

Proposition 9
.

There exists a
\Pi
-equivariant compatible system of perturbations for any proper
\Pi
-equivariant topological floer category
\mathcal{C}
equipped with a compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
. Moreover, if
\mathcal{C}_{2}
is a sub-flow-category of
\mathcal{C}
such that the Kuranishi sections of the associated system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}
has
\sigma_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
already strongly transverse (where we write
\sigma_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
for the Kuranishi section associated to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
for
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
) then we can choose
\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})}=\sigma_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
.

Proof.

We induct on
r
. Suppose that we have chosen
\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
strongly transverse for
([\tilde{x}],[\tilde{y}])\in(\mathcal{C}^{2}/\Pi)_{<r}
, such that

•

Condition (
97
) holds for any tuple
\tilde{x}_{0},\ldots,\tilde{x}_{r}
as in (
97
) with

([\tilde{x}_{0}],[\tilde{x}_{r}])\in(\mathcal{C}^{2}/\Pi)_{<r}
, and

•

we have
g\cdot\sigma(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=\sigma(g\tilde{x},g\tilde{y})
for all
g\in\Pi
and all pairs
([\tilde{x},[\tilde{y}])\in(\mathcal{C}^{2}/\Pi)_{<r}
, and

•

we have
\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})}=\sigma_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
for
([\tilde{x}],[\tilde{y}])\in(\mathcal{C}^{2}/\Pi)_{<r}
.

This hypothesis is vacuously satisfied for
r=0
. We wish to apply Lemma
13
to each element of
(\mathcal{C}^{2}/\Pi)_{r}
. Write
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=(T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}),V(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\sigma(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
, and
\mathcal{C}_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=(T_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}),V_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\sigma_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
Write
T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=\sqcup_{a\in\pi_{0}(T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}))}T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})_{a}
for the connected components, and write
\sigma(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})_{a}=\sigma(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})_{a}}.
If
a\in\pi_{0}(T_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}))
set
\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{a}=\sigma_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})_{a}}
. Otherwise for
a\neq\pi_{0}(T_{2}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}))
, write

\partial T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})_{a}=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{C}\\
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq\emptyset,\mathcal{C}(\tilde{y},\tilde{z})\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})_{a}

(98)

where
T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})_{a}\simeq V(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})_{a}\to(T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\times T(\tilde{y},\tilde{z}))_{a}
is restriction of the stabilizing vector bundle to the corresponding family of connected components. (We are identifying
V(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})
with
T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})\subset T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
via the normally complex diffeomorphism.) We can then write

V(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})}=V(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})\oplus V(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\oplus V(\tilde{y},\tilde{z})

(99)

where the direct sum is really the direct sum of the vector bundles pulled back from
V_{\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z}}
,
T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}
, and
T(\tilde{y},\tilde{z})
respectively. We set
\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{V(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})_{a}}=Ind(\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}),\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{y},\tilde{z}))|_{T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})_{a}}
.
By (
96
) and the inductive hypotesis this is a well-defined section. We extend this section to the interior as a contnuous section
C^{0}
close to
\sigma(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})_{a}
, and we apply Lemma
13
to produce
\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})_{a}
. This defines
\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
by defining it on each connected component. We have now satisfied the inductive hypothesis for
r+1
.
∎

Given a compatible system of perturbations
\sigma^{\prime}
, Lemma
15
implies that there is a finite number of zeros of
\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})
and they all lie away from the isotropy locus. In this case we define
\#\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+};\sigma^{\prime})
to be the sum of the number of zeros of
\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})
counted with multiplicity if
\text{vdim }\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=0
, and otherwise we set
\#\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+};\sigma^{\prime})
.

Chain complex associated to a compatible system of perturbations

Given a compatible system of perturbations
\sigma^{\prime}
, one can define a chain complex
|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|
when a few additional decorations are specified.

Definition 28
.

A
Novikov group
is a triple
(\Pi,E,|\cdot|)
where
\Pi
is a finitely generated free abelian group,
E:\Pi\to\mathbb{R}
is a homomorphism called the
energy function
, and
\mu::\Pi\to\mathbb{Z}
is a homomorphism called the
grading
. We will use
\Pi
to refer to
(\Pi,E,\mu)
when the additional data are clear from the context. We will write
N_{\Pi}
for the positive generator of the image of the grading, unless
\mu(\Pi)=0
in which case we will say
N_{\Pi}=0
.
We have the group
\Pi_{e}=\Pi/\ker E
.

The group rings
\mathbb{Z}[\Pi]
,
\mathbb{Z}[\Pi_{E}]
, inherit filtrations from
E
, and we write
\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
,for the respective completions of these rings with respect to this filtration.

Lemma 35
.

Let
C_{1}^{\bullet}
be a cochain complex of free modules over
\mathbb{Z}
. Let
C_{2}^{\bullet}
be the
\mathbb{Z}/2N_{\Pi}\mathbb{Z}
-graded version of
C_{1}^{\bullet}\otimes_{R}\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, that is

C_{2}^{k}=\bigoplus_{r=k\mod 2N_{\pi}}C_{1}^{\bullet}\otimes\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}},k\in\mathbb{Z}/2N_{\Pi}\mathbb{Z}.

(100)

Then any
\mathbb{Z}/N_{\Pi}\mathbb{Z}
-graded cochain complex
D^{\bullet}
of free
\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
-modules quasi-isomorphic to
C_{2}^{\bullet}
must have at least the same number of generators as the minimum number of generators of a chain complex of free
\mathbb{Z}
-modules quasi-isomorphic to
C_{1}^{\bullet}
.

Proof.

Note that
\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is a PID with principal ideals given by
(n)
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}

[
HS95
, Chapter 4]
.

By using the condition that
d^{2}=0
, we can choose bases in
D^{\bullet}
such that all matrices of all the differentials in
D^{\bullet}
are diagonal matrices with nonnegative integer entries.

Thus
D^{\bullet}\simeq D_{2}^{\bullet}\otimes_{R}\Lambda^{E(\Pi)}_{\mathbb{Z}}
for an
\mathbb{Z}/N_{\Pi}\mathbb{Z}
-graded complex of
\mathbb{Z}
-modules
D_{2}^{\bullet}
. We can thus write the cohomology of
D^{\bullet}
as
H^{k}(D^{\bullet})\simeq(\Lambda^{E(\Pi)}_{\mathbb{Z}})^{k_{f}}\oplus\oplus_{i=1}^{k_{t}}\Lambda^{E(\Pi)}_{\mathbb{Z}}/(d^{k,i})
with
d^{k,i}|d^{k,i+1}
a sequence of positive integers at least
2
, and all the numbers
k_{f},k_{t}
and
d^{k,i}
are well defined. The number of generators of
D^{\bullet}
is then at least
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}/N_{\Pi}\mathbb{Z}}k_{f}+k_{t}
, which, since
D^{\bullet}
is isomorphic to
C_{2}^{\bullet}
, agrees with the minimal number of generators of
C_{1}^{\bullet}
.
∎

Definition 29
.

When
\Pi
is a Novikov group, a
\Pi
-equivariant flow category is a
\Pi
-equivariant flow category
\mathcal{C}
in the sense of Definition
25
, which is equipped with a grading
\mu
, as well as the additional data of an
energy function

E:Ob(\mathcal{C})\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying

E(g\cdot\tilde{x})=E(g)+E(\tilde{x})\text{ and }\mu(g\cdot\tilde{x})=\mu(g)+2\mu(\tilde{x})\text{ for }\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{C},g\in\Pi.

(101)

We say that
\mathcal{C}
as above is
E
-proper if it is proper and if for every pair of elements
x,y\in Ob(\mathcal{(}\mathbb{C}))/\Pi
, the sets

\{(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in\mathcal{C}|[\tilde{x}]=x,[\tilde{y}]=y,\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq 0,E(\tilde{x})-E(\tilde{y})<A\}/\Pi

(102)

are finite for all
A\in\mathbb{R}
. We call the quantity
E(\tilde{x})-E(\tilde{y})
the
energy
of elements in
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
.

We say that
\mathcal{C}
is
E
-positive if whenever
\tilde{x}\neq\tilde{y}
,
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq\emptyset
implies
E(\tilde{x})-E(\tilde{y})>0
.

From now on,
\Pi
will be a Novikov group.

If
\sigma^{\prime}
is a compatible system of perturbations associated to
\Pi
-equivariant
E
-proper flow-category
\mathcal{C}
, we have
\mathbb{Z}/2N_{\Pi}\mathbb{Z}
-graded cochain complex
|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}}
defined as follows. We set also

|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}}^{d}=\{\sum r_{\tilde{x}}\tilde{x}|r_{\tilde{x}}\in\mathbb{Z},\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{C},\mu(\tilde{x})=d+2N_{\Pi}\mathbb{Z},\#\{r_{\tilde{x}}\neq 0|E(\tilde{x})<A\}<\infty\text{ for all }A\}.

(103)

Each of these is a free
\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}
-module.

We define the differential by

d\tilde{y}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{C}\\
\mu(\tilde{x})=\mu(\tilde{y})+1\end{subarray}}\#\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+};\sigma^{\prime})\tilde{x}.

(104)

The fact that the category
\mathcal{C}
is
E
-proper and proper implies that the sums above lie in
|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\mu(\tilde{y})+1}
. Extending
d
by
\Lambda{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}
-linearity, we apply the second part of Lemma
15
to conclude

Lemma 36
.

|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}}^{d}
is a
\mathbb{Z}/2N_{\Pi}\mathbb{Z}
-graded cochain complex of free
\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}
-modules.

We note that
\Pi_{E}
is also a Novikov group, and we can take the quotient of
\mathcal{CC}
by
\ker E
to get a
\Pi_{E}
-equivariant proper
E
-proper flow category, and
\sigma^{\prime}
descends to a compatible system of perturbations for this flow category. We will then have

|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}=|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\otimes_{\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}.

(105)

Definition 30
.

We say that a
\Pi
-equivariant
E
-positive flow category is
gapped
if the minimum of
\{E(\tilde{x})-E(\tilde{y})\in\mathcal{C}|\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq 0,\tilde{x}\neq\tilde{y}\}
exists.

We define

|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}}=|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\otimes_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}.

(106)

Lemma 37
.

The complex
|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}}|
is the tensor product

|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z},0}}\otimes_{\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z},0}}\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}

(107)

where
|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z},0}}
is a
\mathbb{Z}/2N_{\Pi}
-graded complex of
\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z},0}
-modules.

Proof.

We note that
\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\subset\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. A basis for
|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|^{k}_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}|
is given by choosing an element
x\in Ob(\mathcal{C}/\ker E)
such that
\mu(x)=k\mod 2N_{\Pi}
for each
[x]\in Ob(\mathcal{C}/\Pi)
. We define a new basis for
|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}}|
to be given by
T^{-E(x)}(x)
. Now, this basis is independent of the choices of
x
made. The
E
-positivity of
\mathcal{C}
implies that with respect to this basis, the diferentials have entries with coefficients in
\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z},0}
, proving the claim.
∎

We can use flow categories to produce maps between chain complexes associated to other flow categories. We give two such constructions below; these can be verified by using the fact that the differential is defined entirely by looking at derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts of virtual dimension zero, and looking at the combinatorics of the virtual dimension
0
boundaries of virtual dimension
1
charts.

Lemma 38
.

Say
\sigma^{\prime}
is a compatible system of perturbations for a
\Pi
-equivariant
E
-proper proper flow category
\mathcal{C}
. Suppose also that there exists a pair of full sub-categories
\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}
which are preserved by the
\Pi
-action, and which satisfy
Ob(\mathcal{C})=Ob(\mathcal{C}_{1})\sqcup Ob(\mathcal{C}_{2})
, and
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x}_{2},\tilde{y}_{1})=\emptyset
whenever
\tilde{x}_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{2}
and
\tilde{y}_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
. Note that
\sigma^{\prime}
restricts to a compatible system of perturbations for
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
, which we will also denote by
\sigma
. Then we have that

|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|=Cone(f:|\mathcal{C}_{2}[1](\sigma^{\prime})|_{R}\to|\mathcal{C}_{1}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{R})

(108)

for
f
a map of chain complexes.
Here
\mathcal{C}_{2}[1]
is
\mathcal{C}_{2}
with a new grading
\mu^{\prime}
given by
\mu^{\prime}(\tilde{x})=\mu(\tilde{x})+1
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is gapped then the above formula is induced tensoring to
\Lambda^{\Pi_{0}}_{R}
from
\Lambda^{\Pi_{0}}_{R,+}
-linear versions of the corresponding chain complexes.

The following lemma is, in essense, an iterated version of the previous lemma:

Lemma 39
.

Say we are given a
\Pi
-equivariant
E
-proper proper flow category
\mathcal{C}
equipped with a system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts, which contains four full subcategories
\mathcal{C}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{C}_{4}
, preserved by the
\Pi
-action. Suppose that we only have morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
between objects which are monotonically non-strictly decreasing under the following order relations:
Ob(\mathcal{C}_{1})\leq Ob(\mathcal{C}_{2})\leq Ob(\mathcal{C}_{4}),Ob(\mathcal{C}_{1})\leq Ob(\mathcal{C}_{3})\leq Ob(\mathcal{C}_{4})
. Let
\sigma^{\prime}
be a compatible system of perturbations for
\mathcal{C}
, which naturally restricts to the same for the aforementioned subcategories.

Then there is a homotopy-commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\|\mathcal{C}_{4}[2](\sigma^{\prime})\|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\|\mathcal{C}_{3}[1](\sigma^{\prime})\|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\|\mathcal{C}_{2}[1](\sigma^{\prime})\|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\|\mathcal{C}_{1}[2](\sigma^{\prime})\|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}}

(109)

where the horizontal and vertical arrows are induced as in Lemma
38
by restricting to the corresponding full subcategories on pairs in
\{\mathcal{C}_{1},\ldots\mathcal{C}_{4}\}
.

Definition of Floer complex

The work of Sections
3
and
4
can be summarized by the statement that there is a compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts for a flow category with morphisms given by Floer trajectories. We spell out the details below.

We define
\mathcal{C}(H,J)
as the topological flow category with objects given by
\widetilde{Fix}(H)
, with
\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
, with
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})}=[\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{x})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{y})-1]
if
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq\emptyset
, and otherwise
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})}=\bullet
. Composition (whenever
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq 0
and
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{y},\tilde{z})\neq 0
) is given by the gluing of of flow trajectories with the corresponding maps on the
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})}
given by the isomorphism of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})}\sqcup\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\tilde{y},\tilde{z})}
with

\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})}\cup(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\tilde{y},\tilde{z})}+|\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})}|)\subset\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\tilde{x},\tilde{z})}.

(110)

We set
E(\tilde{x})
to be the usual energy
\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\tilde{x})
of a cap. We set
\mu(\tilde{x})
to be its Conley-Zehnder index.
The recapping action maps
\mathcal{C}_{Floer}(H,J)
into a
\Pi
-equivariant flow category (
2
).

We have

Lemma 40
.

The flow category
\mathcal{C}(H,J)
is a proper and
E
-proper
\Pi
-equivariant flow category admitting a compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts.

Proof.

The fact that this category is proper follows from Gromov-Floer compactness and Lemma
19
. The
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts are constructed in Lemma
34
.
∎

We define
CF^{\bullet}(H,J;\sigma^{\prime}):=|\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}
. .

Explicitly, this complex depended on

•

The integralization data
(\tilde{\Omega},N_{1},\bar{H})
;

•

The perturbation data
\Lambda_{d}
;

•

The stabilizing vector bundles arising during the smoothing process;

•

The compatible system of perturbations
\sigma^{\prime}
.

later in this paper, we will sketch a proof of how one can prove independence of the quasi-isomorphism class of this complex on the data above. However, this is not necessary for a verification of the integral Arnol’d conjecture, which we proceeed with below.

6
Continuation map and proof of integral Arnold conjecture

In this section, we explain how to use the techniques described above to prove Theorem
1
.
We will choose a
C^{2}
-small morse function
H_{0}
, a small time-dependent perturbation
H^{\prime}_{0}
of it, and a PSS map from the morse complex of
H_{0}
to the Floer complex of
H^{\prime}_{0}
, which will be an isomorphism. We will also construct continuation maps betwen the Floer complex of
H^{\prime}_{0}
and the Floer complex of our given Hamiltonian
H
and show that they are quasi-isomorphisms, by showing that they compose to maps homotopic to the continuation map from the Floer complex of a Hamiltonian to itself that is induced by an
s
-independent family of Hamiltonians.

6.1
Moduli spaces for continuation map

We let
\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
be the set of non-nodal points in
\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
lying on one of the
distinguished geodesics
of the universal curve. Thus we have a projection

\pi:\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)\to\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d);

(111)

The universal curve
\mathcal{C}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
over
\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
is the pullback of the universal curve
\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
over
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
via
\pi
, and its fibers over points of
\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
are
McLean-stabilized continuation domains
, namely, nodal curves mapping to
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}
and equipped with a certain geodesics as well as a
marked point

c
on the interior of one of the geodeiscs.

The space
\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
has the structure of a
\langle 2d-2\rangle
-manifold. The stratification is given as follows: think of
[2d-2]
as the spaces in between
2d-1
lines, with the middle line marked by a star. Otherwise, place a dot on the spaces in between
2d-1
-lines corresponding to the elements of
[2d-2]\setminus S
. Write
d(S)=(d_{1},d_{r_{1}},d^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime}_{1},\ldots d^{\prime\prime}_{r_{2}})
with
d_{1},\ldots,d_{r_{1}}
for the numbers of lines in the
j
-th contiguous segment, reading left to right, lying
left
of the contiguous segment containing the line marked by the dot; write
d^{\prime\prime}_{1},\ldots,d^{\prime\prime}_{r_{2}}
for the numbers of lines for the numbers of lines in the
j
-th contiguous segment, reading left to right, lying
right
of the contiguous segment containing the line marked by the dot; and write
d^{\prime}=d-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{1}}d^{\prime}_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}d^{\prime\prime}_{j}
. We set
\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S)=\emptyset
if
d^{\prime}\leq 0
. Otherwise, we set

\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)(S)\subset\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d^{\prime}_{1})\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}}\ldots\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}}\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d^{\prime})\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}}\ldots\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}}\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d^{\prime\prime}_{r_{2}})

(112)

to consist of those elements in
\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
arising from joining elements of the target of the inclusion above along the respective marked points. We write
S=S(d^{\prime}_{1},\ldots;d;\ldots,d^{\prime\prime}_{r_{2}})
for the stratum defined above.

We will denote the component of a McLean stabilized continuation domain as
S^{2}_{cont}=S^{2}_{j_{c}}
, where
1\leq j_{c}\leq r_{u}
in the notation of Section
3.2.1
.

Morse functions on the simplex and continuation data

Recall that the
n
-simplex
\Delta^{n}
with vertices labeled
0,\ldots,n
has a
canonical Morse function

[
Par16
]

f_{\Delta^{n}}
with critical points at the vertices of index a linear function of the vertex label. We will arrange for flow of the Morse function to increase the vertex label.

A
continuation datum
from
H_{+}
to
H_{-}
is a map
H^{c}:\Delta^{1}\to\mathcal{H}(M)
, where
\mathcal{H}(M)
is the space of
S^{1}
-dependent Hamiltonians on
M
, and
H^{c}(0)=H_{-},H^{c}(1)=H_{+}
, and
H^{c}
a locally constant function near the vertices. This implies that, for any Morse trajectory of
f_{\Delta^{1}}
from
0
to
1
, we get by pullback to the domain a family
H_{s}
of
S^{1}
-dependent Hamiltonians on
M
for
s\in\mathbb{R}
, which are constant in neighborhoods of infinity and are well defined up to
s
-translation. We can choose
H^{c}
so that these are only non-constant in a
s
-strip of size
1
. Make a fixed choice of Morse trajectory and and a corresponding
H_{s}
.

Write, as usual,
z=s+it
.

One then has the moduli spaces of Floer trajectories

\begin{gathered}\mathcal{M}^{cont}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=\{u:Z\to M\,|\,\partial_{s}u(z)+J_{z}(\partial_{t}u-X_{H_{s}})=0;\lim_{s\to-\infty}u(s,\cdot)=x(\cdot),\lim_{s\to+\infty}u(s,\cdot)=y(\cdot),\,\tilde{x}\#u=\tilde{y}\}\\
\text{ where }\tilde{x}\in\widetilde{Fix}(H_{-}),\tilde{y}\in\widetilde{Fix}(H_{+}).\end{gathered}

(113)

and their corresponding Gromov-Floer compactifications
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{cont}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
.

6.1.1
Convenient integral forms for the continuation map.

Recall that in the usual Floer theoretic setup, we have that for a Floer trajectory
u\in\mathcal{M}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})

E_{\Omega}(u)=\int_{Z}\|\partial_{s}u\|^{2}_{\Omega}=\int_{Z}u^{*}\Omega-\partial_{s}(H(u(s,t))dsdt+\int\Omega(X^{\Omega}_{H}-X^{\omega}_{H},\partial_{s}u)dsdt.

(114)

This identity follows from a pointwise computation, and the first integral is exactly
\int_{Z}u^{*}\Omega-d(Hdt)=\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{x}_{-})-\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{x}_{+})
. Since this computation was pointwise, the same computation implies that for
u\in\mathcal{M}^{cont}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})
,

E_{\Omega}(u)=\int_{Z}\|\partial_{s}u\|^{2}_{\Omega}=\int_{Z}u^{*}\Omega-(dH_{s})(\partial_{s}u(s,t))dsdt+\int\Omega(X^{\Omega}_{H_{s}}-X^{\omega}_{H_{s}},\partial_{s}u)dsdt.

(115)

We now have two distinct action functionals
\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{\pm}
defined using
H_{\pm}
. We then have that

\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{-}(\tilde{x}_{-})-\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{+})=\int_{Z}u^{*}\Omega-d(H_{s}dt)=\left\{\int u^{*}\Omega-(dH_{s})(\partial_{s}u(s,t))dsdt\right\}-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}H^{\prime}_{s}(u(s,t))dsdt

(116)

where
H^{\prime}_{s}(x)=\partial_{s}H(x)
for
x\in M
. We thus conclude that

\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{-}(\tilde{x}_{-})-\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{+})=E_{\Omega}(u)-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}H^{\prime}_{s}(u(s,t))dsdt-\int\Omega(X^{\Omega}_{H_{s}}-X^{\omega}_{H_{s}},\partial_{s}u)dsdt.

(117)

Note that the absolute value of the middle term is bounded on both sides by a rational constant
K

Now, we have open neighborhoods
U_{\pm}
of
Fix(\phi_{H_{\pm}})
constructed by Lemma
16
. Taking
U=U_{-}\cup U_{+}
is now an open neighborhood of
Fix(\phi_{H_{-}})\cup Fix(\phi_{H_{+}})
, and the conclusion of Lemma
16
still holds for this
U
. We take
\epsilon_{0}=\min(\epsilon_{0}^{-},\epsilon_{0}^{+})
where
\epsilon_{0}^{\pm}
is the constant in Lemma
17
for
H=H_{\pm}
and
U=U_{\pm}
.

Defining

S^{\epsilon_{1}}_{\omega,H_{s}}=\left\{\Omega\in\Omega^{2}(M)|\Omega\text{ symplectic },|\Omega(X^{\Omega}_{H_{s}}-X^{\omega}_{H_{s}},v)|<\epsilon_{1}\|v\|_{\omega}\text{ for all }s\in\mathbb{R},\|v\|_{\Omega}\geq\frac{1}{2}\|v\|_{\omega}\text{ for }v\in TM,\Omega|_{U}=\omega\right\}.

(118)

we can again pick a rational
\Omega\in S^{\epsilon_{1}}_{\omega,H_{s}}
for
\epsilon_{1}=\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}/8
. We define
L
as in the proof of Lemma
18
, The computation of (
38
) now gives

\int_{\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}}|\Omega(X^{\Omega}_{H_{s}}-X^{\omega}_{H_{s}},\partial_{s}u)|dsdt\leq\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}_{0}}(E^{cyl}_{\omega}(u)+(E^{cyl}_{\omega}(u))^{\frac{1}{2}})

(119)

where the new term is because we separately have to apply Cauchy-Schwartz to

\int_{[-1/2,1/2]\times S^{1}}|\Omega(X^{\Omega}_{H_{s}}-X^{\omega}_{H_{s}},\partial_{s}u)|dsdt

since we have not arranged Lemma
16
to apply for
H=H_{s}
. Plugging this into (
117
) we conclude that

\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{-}(\tilde{x}_{-})-\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{+})+K\geq 2E_{\omega}(u)-\frac{1}{8}(E^{cyl}_{\omega}(u)+(E^{cyl}_{\omega}(u))^{\frac{1}{2}}),\text{ and thus }\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{-}(\tilde{x}_{-})-\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{+})+K+\frac{1}{4}\geq E_{\omega}(u).

(120)

We define
\tilde{\Omega}
using Lemma
16
. Now our chosen
\Omega
still satisfies the conclusion of Lemma
18
for
\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}
replaced by
either
of
\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{+}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}^{-}
; we set
M^{\prime}_{\pm}
to be the minimum of the quantities
M^{\prime}
for each of these functionals. Choose
\epsilon_{x}
such that that
\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\Omega}(\tilde{x}_{\pm})+\epsilon_{x}\subset\mathbb{Q}
for
\tilde{x}_{\pm}\in Fix(\phi_{H_{\pm}})
, and choose
N_{1}
such that
K^{\prime}=N_{1}(K+\frac{1}{4})\subset\mathbb{Z}
and
N_{1}(\mathcal{A}^{\pm}_{\Omega}(\tilde{x}_{\pm})+\epsilon_{x})\subset\mathbb{Z}
. We fix
\Omega_{0}=N_{1}\tilde{\Omega}
and write
\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\pm}
defined by the formula (
41
) for
H=H_{\pm}
.

We have that

\{\tilde{x}_{\pm}\in Fix(\phi_{H_{\pm}}),u\in{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{cont}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{-}(\tilde{x}_{-})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{+})\leq K\}/\Pi

(121)

is compact for each
K
. We choose
\epsilon_{2}
to be the minimum of the quantities
\epsilon_{2}
in the paragraph above Lemma
20
for
H=H_{\pm}
, and we choose
\bar{H}:M\times\mathbb{R}_{s}\times S^{1}_{t}
to be equal to be defined as in (

) for

and
H=H_{-}
, similarly for
s>\frac{1}{2}
for
H=_{+}
, and otherwise extended smoothly. We have

Lemma 41
.

For every smooth map

u:\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}\to M,\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}u(s,t)=x_{\pm}(t)\text{(uniformly)},u\#\bar{x}_{-}=\bar{x}_{+}

(122)

with
x_{\pm}(t)\in Fix(H_{\pm})
,
define the
2
-form

\Omega^{c}_{u}\in\Omega^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}),\Omega_{u}(s,t)=u^{*}\Omega_{0}(s,t)-d(\bar{H}_{s})dt.

(123)

The form
\Omega_{u}
is zero near

. Moreover,
\int\Omega_{u}\in\mathbb{Z}
agrees with
\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{x})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{y})
, and if

\int_{Z}\Omega_{u}+K^{\prime}>0
.

6.1.2
The continuation moduli space.

We can now make
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{cont}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})
into a
[2(\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{-}(\tilde{x}_{-})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{+})+K^{\prime})-2]
-space. Recall that the elements of this spaces are tuples
u=(u^{-}_{1},\ldots u^{-}_{r_{1}},u^{c},u^{+}_{1},\ldots,u^{+}_{r_{2}})
where
u^{\pm}_{j}
is bubbled,
1
-level Floer trajectory from
\tilde{x}_{\pm}^{j-1}
to
\tilde{x}_{-}\pm^{j}
with respect to the Hamiltonian, where we have set
\tilde{x}_{-}^{0}=\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+}^{r_{2}}=\tilde{x}_{+}
, and where
u^{c}
is a solution to the continuation equation with bubbles attached from
\tilde{x}_{-}^{r_{1}}
to
\tilde{x}_{+}^{0}
. We assign to such a

to open stratum associated to the set

S=S(\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{-}(\tilde{x}_{-}^{0})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{-}(\tilde{x}^{1}_{-}),\ldots,;\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{-}(\tilde{x}^{-}_{r_{1}})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(\tilde{x}^{+}_{0})+K^{\prime};\ldots,\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(\tilde{x}^{+}_{r_{2}-1})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(\tilde{x}^{+}_{r_{2}})).

(124)

Thickenings

Write
S^{cont}_{d}\subset 2^{[2d-2]}
for the set of nonempty strata of
\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
. There is a natural map of posets
S^{cont}_{d}\to 2^{[d-1]}
which sends codmension strata to codimension
r
strata; with respect to this map,
\pi
of (

) is a submersion.
We have that

\mathcal{F}_{S(d^{\prime}_{1},\ldots;d^{\prime}_{r_{1}};d^{\prime};d^{\prime\prime}_{1},\ldots,d^{\prime\prime}_{r_{2}})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)=Ind(\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d^{\prime}_{1}},d^{\prime}_{1}),\ldots,\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d^{\prime}},d^{\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d^{\prime\prime}_{r_{2}}},d^{\prime\prime}_{r_{2}})).

(125)

is expressible as a sequence of generalized inductions and restrictions from a product, as in Section
3.2.2
, whenever

.

A perturbation datum over a substratum of
\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
is defined exactly as in Definition
10
. Formula
97
regarding compatibility of inductions along decompositions (
125
) continues to hold, making “compatibility of perturbation data” adapt to this setting as in Definition
11
, with
\mathcal{F}
replaced by
\mathcal{F}^{cont}
. Assume that now we have fixed a compatible choice of perturbation data
\Lambda^{cont}_{d}=(V^{cont}_{d},\lambda^{cont}_{d})
over
\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
for all
d
, extending a compatible set of perturbation data
\Lambda^{\pm}_{d}
over
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
chosen for
H^{+}
and
H^{-}
. We note that we can choose
\Lambda^{+}_{d}=\Lambda^{-}_{d}=:\Lambda_{d}
as we only need to make the pertubation data “large enough” to achieve regularity of the thickenings for the Floer moduli spaces.

For the remainder of this paper we
fix
a sequence of Hermitian line bundles
L_{k}
on
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{1}
for every integer
k
.

Definition 31
.

A framed bubbled pre-continuation trajectory is a tuple
(u,\Sigma,F)
, where

•

\Sigma
, the
domain
of the trajectory, is a genus zero nodal curve with two marked points
p_{-},p_{+}
, equipped with geodesic segments from
p_{j-1}
to
p_{j}

S^{2}_{J}
, with
S^{2}_{1},\ldots,S^{2}_{j},\ldots,S^{2}_{r_{u}}
the sequence of components from
p_{-}=p_{0}
to
p_{+}=p_{r_{u}}
. One of the geodesic segment also carries a marked point on
S^{2}_{cont}=S^{2}_{j_{c}}
.

•

The map
u:\Sigma\setminus\{p_{0},\ldots,p_{r_{u}}\}\to M
is a smooth function such that the integral
2
-form
\Omega^{c}_{u}\in\Omega^{2}(\Sigma)
defined subsequently satisfies

\int_{\Sigma_{\alpha}}\Omega^{c}_{u}+K^{\prime}>0\text{ for every unstable component }\Sigma_{\alpha}.

(126)

The form
\Omega^{c}_{u}
is defined to be
u|_{S^{2}_{\pi}}^{*}\Omega_{0}
(see (
123
)) on each component
S^{2}_{\pi}
of
\Sigma
that is not one of the
S^{2}_{j}
, and on each
S^{2}_{j}
with
j\neq j_{c}
equal to the extension by zero of the form denoted
\Omega_{u}
in (
123
) under any preferred biholomorphism
\phi_{j}:S^{2}_{j}\setminus\{p_{j-1},p_{j}\}\simeq\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}
. Finally, on
S^{2}_{j_{c}}
it is defined to be the extension by zero of the form denoted
\Omega^{c}_{u}
above under the unique biholomorphism
\psi_{u,j_{c}}
which maps
Z
to
S^{2}_{j_{c}}\setminus\{p_{j_{c}-1},p_{j_{c}}\}
such that
\psi_{u,j_{c}}
sends the marked point on the geodesic to a point to
s=0
.

•

Additionally, the map is required converge uniformly to Hamiltonian loops at the ends corresponding to the
p_{i}
under the biholomorphisms described above;

•

Moreover, writing
u^{*}g
for the pull-back of the metric on
M
by
u
, and
dV_{u^{*}g}
for the associated volume form (which will in general not be defined on the
p_{i}
), we have the volume of
\Sigma
with respect to
dV_{u^{*}g}
is finite.

•

The datum
F=(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d})
is a
\mathbb{C}
-basis for
H^{0}(L_{\Omega_{u}+K^{\prime}})
, where
L_{\Omega_{u}+K^{\prime}}
is the line bundle on
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{1}
which is obtained from
L_{\Omega}
by tensoring it with the line bundle which is trivial on every component except for
S^{2}_{j_{c}}
, and agrees with
L_{K^{\prime}}
on
S^{2}_{j_{c}}
. Note that
L_{\Omega_{u}+K^{\prime}}
has positive degree on every unstable component by Equation (
126
).

We continue to use notation as in the paragraph subsequent to Definition
9
for pre-continuation trajectories.

We now define thickenings
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{cont}_{d}}
to consist o pairs
((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)
where
(u,\Sigma,F)
is a framed bubbled pre-continuation trajectory, with
u_{F}\in\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
and
\eta\in(V^{cont}_{d})
, which satisfies

\begin{gathered}\partial_{s}u_{j}+J(\partial_{t}u_{j}-X_{H^{-}})+\lambda_{d}(\eta)\circ di_{F}=0\text{ for }1\leq j<j_{c},\text{ where }u_{j}=u|_{S_{j}}\circ\psi_{u,j}.\\
\partial_{s}u_{j}++J(\partial_{t}u_{j}-X_{H^{+}})+\lambda_{d}(\eta)\circ di_{F}=0\text{ for }j_{c}<j\leq r_{u},\\
\partial_{s}u_{j_{c}}+J\partial_{t}(u_{j_{c}}-X_{H_{s}})+\lambda_{d}(\eta)\circ di_{F}=0,\\
\bar{\partial}u|_{S^{2}_{\alpha}}+\lambda_{d}(\eta)\circ di_{F}=0\text{ for all other components }S^{2}_{\alpha}\neq S^{2}_{1},\ldots,S^{2}_{r_{u}}\end{gathered}

(127)

We then define
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda^{cont}_{d}}=(PU(d+1),T_{\Lambda^{cont}_{d}},\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda^{cont}_{d}}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1},\sigma_{\Lambda^{cont}_{d}}\oplus\sigma_{H})
defined exactly as in Definition
12
. We write
\mathcal{T}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda^{cont}_{d}}
to to be the sub-chart of
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{cont}_{d}}
for which
\|\eta\|<\epsilon
. We have that

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{cont}(d)
, the quotient of the zero section of the Kuranishi section of
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda^{cont}_{d}}
by the
PU(d+1)
-action, is the set of bubbled continuation trajectories in
M
(of
\Omega_{u}
-degree
d
). We write

{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{cont}(d)=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\pm}\in\widetilde{Fix}(\phi_{H_{\pm}}^{1})\\
\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{-}(\tilde{x}_{-})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{+}(\tilde{x}_{+})+K^{\prime}=d\end{subarray}}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{cont}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+}).

(128)

As before, we have that, for sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, the
PU(d+1)
action on
\mathcal{T}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda^{cont}_{d}}
has finite stabilizers, and that the induced topology on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{cont}(d)
agrees with the Gromov-Floer topology. The argument of Lemma
28
adapts without change to show that one can choose compatible perturbation data
\Lambda^{cont}_{d}
such that the extended linearized operators are always surjective on continuation solutions, which implies that
T_{\Lambda^{cont}_{d}}^{\epsilon}
is a
PU(d+1)-\langle d-1\rangle
-manifold with projection
\Pi
to
\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
a fiberwise-smooth
C^{1}_{loc}PU(d+1)
bundle.

In the boundary of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{cont}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})
, we have (broken bubbled) Floer trajectories for
H_{\pm}
appearing. We denote the moduli spaces of these trajectories via
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\pm}(\tilde{x}_{\pm},\tilde{y}_{\pm})
for
\tilde{x}_{\pm},\tilde{y}_{\pm}\in\widetilde{Fix}(H_{\pm})
.

6.1.3
Flow category for continuation map

We now construct a proper,
E
-proper,
\Pi
-equivariant graded flow category
\mathcal{C}(H^{c},J)
, where
(\Pi,E,\mu)
are a Novikov group and
E
is associated to
\Omega
as chosen in this section.

The objects of
\mathcal{C}(H^{c},J)
are
\widetilde{Fix}(H_{-})\sqcup\widetilde{Fix}(H_{+})
. The full subcategory on
\widetilde{Fix}(H_{-})
is
\mathcal{C}(H_{-},J)
, and the full subcategory on
\widetilde{Fix}(H_{+})
is
\mathcal{C}(H_{+},J)[-1]
, as needed to apply Lemma
38
. This fixes the gradings, the
E
-functions, and the
\Pi
-action. We set
\mathcal{C}(H^{c},J)(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{cont}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})
for
\tilde{x}_{\pm}\in\widetilde{Fix}(H_{\pm})
. We set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(H^{c},J)(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})}=[2(\mathcal{A}^{-}(\tilde{x_{-}})-\mathcal{A}^{+}(\tilde{x_{+}})+K^{\prime})-2]
. To define composition, we glue bubbled broken Floer trajectories to bubbled broken continuation map trajectories in the standard way; the composition on the sets
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}(H^{c},J)(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})}
is defined exactly as in (
110
) but with
\mathcal{C}_{(}H,J)
replaced by
\mathcal{C}(H^{c},J)
.

The methods of Section
4
adapt verbatim to this setting to show

Proposition 10
.

The flow category
\mathcal{C}^{cont}
admits a compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{cont}
.

Proof.

There is nothing new in the argument, except that we must make sure that the analog of Proposition
3
for
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{cont}_{d}}
still holds. However, as we use Morse trajectories to parameterize the continuation datum, exactly the same argument goes through. This is immaterial for a single continuation map, but becomes important in the subsequent Proposition
12
, where it becomes important that we use the gluing of Morse trajectories to parameterize gluing of continuation data, as does
[
Par16
, Appendix C]
.
∎

Now, fixing compatible systems of perturbations
\sigma^{\prime}_{\pm}
for
\mathcal{C}(H_{\pm},J)
, we use Proposition
9
to extend this to a compatible system of perturbations for
\mathcal{C}(H^{c},J)
, and then apply Lemma
38
to produce a map

c_{H_{-},H_{+}}:|\mathcal{C}(H_{+},J)(\sigma^{\prime}_{+})|\to|\mathcal{C}(H_{-},J)(\sigma^{\prime}_{-})|

(129)

called the
continuation map
. The construction of this map depended on the choice of
\Omega
and
\bar{H}
; the choice of
H_{s}
; the choices of perturbation data, the stabilizations performed in the repeated application of Proposition
8
; and on the compatible systems of perturbations.

We note that shifting
E|_{\widetilde{Fix}(H_{-})}\to E|_{\widetilde{Fix}(H_{-})}+C
for a sufficiently large constant
C
makes this category into an
E
-positive flow category. Write
c_{\Lambda^{univ}}=c\otimes_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}1:|\mathcal{C}(H_{+},J)(\sigma^{\prime}_{+})|_{\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\to|\mathcal{C}(H_{-},J)(\sigma^{\prime}_{-})|_{\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}}
for the corresponding map over the universal Novikov ring. By working through Lemma
38
and Lemma
37
, we see that this change of
E
modifies
c_{\Lambda^{univ}}
by multiplying it by it by
T^{C}
; in particular,
T^{C}c_{\Lambda^{univ}}
has coefficients in
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z},0}^{univ}
.

6.2
Compositions of continuation maps are homotopic

Let
H^{1},H^{2},H^{3},H^{4}
be four
S^{1}
-families of Hamiltonians with nondegenerate fixed points, none of which are constant Hamiltonian trajectories. Supose we choose continuation data from
H^{1}
to
H^{2}
, from
H^{1}
to
H^{3}
, from
H^{2}
to
H^{4}
, and from
H^{3}
to
H^{4}
.

A
homotopy datum

H^{h}
is a pair of maps
\Delta^{2}\to\mathcal{H}(M)
which glue together to a smooth map from a square, are locally constant near the vertices, and restrict to the continuation data as in the below diagram (the top left vertex corresponds to the
0
vertex on both simplices, and the bottom right to the
2
vertex):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{4}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{1}}

(130)

Any Morse flow line
\tau
on either of these simplices gives a family of Hamiltonians
H_{s,\tau}
which could come from a continuation datum.

Choose a
single

(\Omega,N_{1})
such that, together with choices of perturbed action functionals
\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{j}
for
j=0,1,2
, as well as choices of functions
\bar{H}_{j}
,
j=1\ldots 4
, as well as a smooth family of functions

\tau
in the interior of the simplices, all defined via the construction above Lemma
41
such that these functions all satisfy the conclusion of Lemma
41
and such that, viewing

as a map from the interior of the moduli space of Morse trajectories on the square to

\bar{H}_{j}
as in the diagram (
130
). For every (possibly) broken Morse trajectory
\tau
in the space of Morse trajectories on the square, we thus have defined an appropriate two-form
\Omega_{u,\tau}^{c}
which when integrated over a broken continuation trajectory gives the corresponding difference of integralized actions
\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{j}
.

With these choices, we have that for any solution of the continuation map equation for
H_{s,\tau}
for any fixed
\tau
, or for
H_{s}^{01}
or
H_{s}^{1,2}
from
\tilde{x}_{2}\in\widetilde{Fix}(H_{2})
to
\tilde{x}_{0}\in\widetilde{Fix}(H_{0})
, we have that

\int\Omega_{u,\tau}^{c}=\bar{A}^{2}(\tilde{x}_{2})-\bar{A}^{0}(\tilde{x}_{0})\in\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}|\ell\geq K^{\prime}\}

(131)

for some fixed
K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
. We then use
this

K^{\prime}
to define continuation maps described in the next paragraph.

Write the flow category used to define the continuation map
c_{H^{j},H^{i}}:|\mathcal{C}(H_{j},J)(\sigma^{\prime}_{+})|\to|\mathcal{C}(H_{i},J)(\sigma^{\prime}_{-})|
as
\mathcal{C}(H^{j},H^{i};J)
with
(j,i)
the sources and targets of arrows in (

), with associated systems of derived

-orbifold charts
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H^{j},H^{i};J)
and choices of compatible systems of perturbations
\sigma_{H^{j},H^{i}}^{\prime}
used to define these maps.

Proposition 11
.

The following maps are homotopic as maps of
\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
-modules:

c_{H^{4},H^{3}}c_{H^{3},H^{1}}\sim c_{H^{4},H^{2}}c_{H^{2},H^{1}}.

(132)

Proof.

We will construct a diagram of flow categories as in Lemma
39
. We choose
\mathcal{C}_{j}=\mathcal{C}(H_{j},J)
for
j=0,\ldots,3
. The full subcategories on pairs of these will be
\mathcal{C}(H^{j},H^{i};J)
in the natural way, with the appropriate grading shifts to fit the Lemma. This specifies the gradings, the
E
-function, and the derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts on the subcategory. It remains to specify the morphisms from
\mathcal{C}(H_{4},J)
to
\mathcal{C}(H_{1},J)
and build a compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts for the whole category.

The morphisms from
\mathcal{C}(H^{h};J)(\tilde{x}_{,}\tilde{x}_{+})
from
\tilde{x}_{-}\in\mathcal{C}(H_{4},J)
to
\tilde{x}_{+}\in\mathcal{C}(H_{1},J)
are defined to be the Gromov-Floer compactification of the space of pairs of a morse trajectory
\tau
in the interior of the square that is the domain
H^{h}
together with a solution of the continuation map equation for the continuation datum
H_{s,\tau}
, up to simultaneous
\mathbb{R}
-translation of both data. This space is naturally a
[2(\mathcal{A}(\tilde{x}_{-})-2\mathcal{A}(\tilde{x}_{+}2K^{\prime})-3]
-space: it can break into at most two continuation map trajectories, and we use the extra element of the labeling set to describe where the map is broken into a pair of compactified continuation map trajectories. The moduli spaces
\mathcal{C}(H^{h};J)(\tilde{x}_{,}\tilde{x}_{+})
admit continuous maps
\alpha
the moduli space of Morse trajectories in the square which specify which Morse trajectory defines the continuation datum that the corresponding solutions to the continuation map equation solve.

To equip these spaces with global Kuranishi charts we need to introduce another moduli space of domains akin to
\mathcal{F}^{cont}
; we will sketch the construction in outline. We let
\mathcal{F}^{cont,b}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
be the space of elements of
\mathcal{F}^{cont}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
with
two
additional marked points
\hat{p}^{1}
,
\hat{p}^{0}
on the geodesics, with
\hat{p}^{0}
closer to
p_{+}
than
\hat{p}^{1}
, and such that if
\hat{p}^{0}
and
\hat{p}^{1}
lie on the same component
S^{2}_{j_{c}}
, then their images under any biholomorphism
S^{2}_{j_{c}}\simeq Z
lie at least
10
apart. As before, these moduli spaces can be related via generalized inductions and restrictions, and have a natural notion of a compatible set of perturbation data. A pair of maps from
\mathcal{F}^{cont,b}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
to the moduli space of Morse trajectories (a interval) in each of the two simplices comprising the homotopy datum
H^{c}
, which map to the broken Morse trajectory exactly when
\hat{p}^{i}
are on different components, and which map to the Morse trajectory which bisects the square exactly when the
\hat{p}^{i}
are on the same component and like a distance
10
apart on a biholomorphism to the cylinder. These maps are required to satisfy the property that near the locus where
\hat{p}^{0}
and
\hat{p}^{1}
are on distinct components, if we choose biholomorphisms to the cylinder on these components for which these points get mapped to
s=0
, then as we glue these curves to a curve with a single component, the corresponding biholomorphisms to the cylidner map these points to those with
s
values agreeing with those where the gluing map for the corresponding Morse trajectories maps the mid-points of the pair of broken curves. For each of these maps, one defines thickenings
T_{bord,d,\pm}^{\epsilon}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})
of the preimages of
\alpha
in
\mathcal{C}(H^{h};J)(\tilde{x}_{,}\tilde{x}_{+})
of each half of the moduli space of Morse trajectories in the square, and the final thickening of
\mathcal{C}(H^{h};J)(\tilde{x}_{,}\tilde{x}_{+})
is simply a union of these two pieces. The thickenings consist of choices of a
pre-continuation-homotopy trajectory
which as in Definition
31
are simply choices of an element
u_{F}\in\mathcal{F}^{cont,b}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
together with a map
u
from the universal curve over
u_{F}
with the points
p_{0},\ldots,p_{r_{u}}
removed to
M
with the usual Hamiltonian endpoint trajectories, such that the
\int\Omega^{c}_{u,\alpha(u_{F})}
-degrees of the map agree with the degrees assigned to components of
u_{F}
, and such that if a component contains a pair of
\hat{p}^{i}
, we use the midpoint of the
\hat{p}^{i}
along the geodesic to determine the biholomorphism to the cylinder and we solve the continuation map equation for
H_{s,\alpha(u_{F})}
on that component; and otherwise we solve the continuation map equations corresponding to each piece of the broken morse trajectory on the corresponding components.

Using the methods of Sections 3 and 4, one establishes as in Proposition
10
:

Proposition 12
.

There exist compatible choices of perturbation data such that the linearizations of the defining equations of
T_{bord,d,\pm}^{\epsilon}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})
are surjective Fredholm operators on all solutions. The thickenings
T_{bord,d,\pm}^{\epsilon}(\tilde{x}-,\tilde{x}_{+})
are topological
PU(d+1)-\langle\langle 2(\mathcal{A}(\tilde{x}_{-})-2\mathcal{A}(\tilde{x}_{+}2K^{\prime})-3\rangle
manifolds, and a stabilization of collars of these charts can be smoothed such that the resulting derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts give a compatible system of normally complex derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts for
\mathcal{C}(H^{h};J)
.

We now note that the choices used to define
c_{H^{1}_{s}},c{H^{0}_{s}},
, and
c{H^{10,\epsilon}_{s}}
give us compatible systems of perturbations
\sigma^{\prime}_{0}
for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{cont,bord}|_{0}
and
\sigma^{\prime}_{1}

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{cont,bord}|_{1}
, which satisfy
|\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{cont,bord}|_{0}(\sigma^{\prime}_{0})|=Cone(c_{H^{1}_{s}}c_{H^{0}_{s}})
and
|\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{cont,bord}|_{1}(\sigma^{\prime}_{1})|=Cone(c{H^{10,\epsilon}_{s}})
. We thus have a homotopy equivalence of cones
Cone(c_{H^{1}_{s}}c_{H^{0}_{s}})\sim Cone(c{H^{10,\epsilon}_{s}})
. Because the perturbation data restricted to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{cont,bord}|_{0}(\widetilde{H}_{i})
agrees with that for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{cont,bord}|_{1}(\widetilde{H}_{i})
for

respecitvely, this homotopy equivalence of cones is in fact induced by a homotopy equivalence of maps.
c_{H^{1}_{s}}c_{H^{0}_{s}}\sim c{H^{10,\epsilon}_{s}}
.
∎

The identity continuation datum

Proposition 13
.

Choose
H_{s}=H
independent of
S
, i.e. let the continuation datum be a constant map from
\Delta^{1}
. Then
c_{H_{s}}:|\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\sigma^{\prime})|\to|\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\sigma^{\prime})|
is a quasi-isomorphism over
\Lambda_{0}
.

Proof.

The constant continuation trajectory is regular. Thus, we can choose the system of perturbations used to define
c_{H_{s}}
such that we preserve these constant perturbation data. For this perturbation datum, we moreover have that
c
is a map of
\Lambda_{0}
modules, since
\mathcal{C}_{cont}
is
E
-proper already, because the last term in (
) is zero. Analyzing the construction of
c_{H_{s}}
from Lemma
38
, we see that we can write
c_{H_{s}}
as
1+O(T^{\epsilon})
for some
\epsilon>0
. By the Nakayama lemma, this implies that
c_{H_{s}}
is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

We have proven:

Proposition 14
.

The quasi-isomorphism type of
|\mathcal{C}(H,J)(\sigma^{\prime})|
over
\Lambda^{univ}
is independent of
H
and
J
, as well as all auxiliary data used to define it.

6.3
Moduli spaces for PSS map

Finally, we show that if
H_{1}
is a small time-dependent perturbation of a
C^{2}
-small
strictly positive
Morse function
H
with distinct critical values, then
CF(H_{1},J)
is quasi-isomorphic to the Morse complex of
H
. To do this we will, following
[
PSS96
]
, define a map
PSS:CM(H)\to CF(H_{1},J_{1})
which is an isomorphism after setting the Novikov parameter to zero, and is thus a quasi-isomorphism of complexes.

For sufficiently small perturbations of
H
, we have a natural bijection
Crit(H)\simeq Fix(\phi_{H_{1}}^{1})
. We denote this bijection by writing that
x^{\prime}\in Crit(H)
corresponds to
x\in Fix(\phi_{H_{1}}^{1})
. Thinking of a critical point as a constant loop, we have a bijection between cappings

\begin{gathered}\widetilde{Crit}(H)=\widetilde{Fix}(\phi^{1}_{H})\simeq\widetilde{Fix}(\phi^{1}_{H_{1}})\\
\tilde{x}^{\prime}\xleftrightarrow{}\tilde{x}.\end{gathered}

(133)

For convenience, we will also choose
H_{1}
such that
\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\tilde{x}^{\prime})=\mathcal{A}_{\omega}(\tilde{x})
.

To define the domain of the perturbation datum we must define yet another moduli space of McLean-stabilized domains. Luckily, in this case the combinatorics are essentially identical to the combinatorics of the differential. We recall that
D_{k}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,k}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
consists of the stable maps which have linearly nondegenrate image. Let
\mathcal{F}^{PSS,R}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)\subset D_{3}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
consist those maps such that, writing
(p_{-},p_{+},p)
for the three marked points on
u\in D_{3}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
, we have that
(p,p_{-})
lie on the same component. As before, we can write
S_{1},\ldots,S_{r_{u}}
for the components of the domain of
u
where
S_{1}\ni p_{-}=p_{1}
,
S_{j}
is joined to
S_{j+1}
along
p_{j}
, and
S_{r_{u}}\ni p_{r_{u}}=p_{+}
. We can think of an element
u\in\mathcal{F}^{PSS,R}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell,d})
as a stable map where
S^{1}
is labeled by a geodesic from
p_{-}
to
p_{1}
through
p
, and is marked by
p
; thus,
S_{1}\setminus\{p_{-},p\}
is canonically biholomorphic to
Z
.

We define

\mathcal{F}^{PSS}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)\subset\bigcup_{d_{1}+d_{2}=d}\mathcal{F}^{PSS_{R}}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d_{1})\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell}}\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d_{2})

(134)

to consist of those pairs
(C,D)
for which
p_{+}\in C
is joined to
p_{-}\in D
, and such that the image of the joined map is not contained in any
(d-1)
-dimensional linear subspace.

This space is a
PU(\ell+1)
-
\langle d-1\rangle
-manifold in the same way that
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
is; there are no extra corners introduced by the
\mathcal{D}_{2}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d_{2})
factors, which are manifolds. In particular, we have that

\mathcal{F}^{PSS}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)(S(d_{1},\ldots,d_{r}))\subset\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d_{1})\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}}\ldots\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}}\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d_{r-1})\times_{{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d}}\mathcal{F}^{PSS}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d_{r})

(135)

is related to the spaces
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}},d_{1}),\ldots,\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{r-1}},d_{r-1}),\mathcal{F}^{PSS}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{r}},d_{r})
by restrictions, products, equivalences, and stabilizations, as in Proposition
4
,whenever
d_{1}+\ldots+d_{r}=d
.

A perturbation datum over a substratum of
\mathcal{F}^{PSS}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell},d)
is defined exactly as in Definition
10
. Formula
72
regarding compatibility of the inductions of perturbation data along decompositions (
135
) continues to hold, making “compatibility of perturbation data” adapt to this setting as in Definition
11
, with
\mathcal{F}
replaced by
\mathcal{F}^{PSS}
. Assume that now we have fixed a compatible choice of perturbation data
\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}
over
\mathcal{F}^{PSS}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
for all
d
, extending a compatible set of chosen perturbation data
\Lambda_{d}
over
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
.

We choose a
PSS datum
, namely a smooth map
H^{PSS}_{s}:\mathbb{R}_{s}\to\mathcal{H}(M)
such that
H^{PSS}_{s}=H_{1}
for
s\leq-1
,
\partial_{s}H^{PSS}(x)\leq 0
for all
s,x
; and
H^{PSS}_{s}=0
for
s>1
. This is possible since
H
is a strictly positive Morse function. With this choice of
H^{PSS}_{s}
, the estimate (
117
) shows that for a map

u:Z\to M;\partial_{s}u+J(\partial_{t}u-X_{H^{PSS}_{s}})=0;\lim_{s\to-\infty}u(s,t)=x_{-}(t);\lim_{s\to+\infty}u(s,t)=u_{+\infty}\in M;\tilde{x}=[u]

(136)

(where the last condition is simply that
u
is that
u
viewed as a map from
S^{2}\setminus\{p_{-}\}
is in the homology class of the capping
\tilde{x}_{-}
of the Hamiltonian orbit
x_{-}
) we have that

\mathcal{A}_{\Omega}(\tilde{x_{-}})\geq\epsilon>0

(137)

for some fixed
\epsilon
independent of
\tilde{x}_{-}
. As in Section , we use a process identical to that of Section
6.1.1
to choose an
(\tilde{\Omega},N_{1})
and as well as
\epsilon_{x}
for
x\in Fix(\phi_{H})
; and then we choose a
\bar{H}
as in section

and a
\bar{H}^{PSS}_{s}
which has conditions as in

for

, and still satisfies

and

for

. We then have the form
\Omega_{u}^{PSS}
defined identically to (

) with

replaced by
\bar{PSS}_{s}
. Thus we have

Lemma 42
.

If
u
is a solution to (
136
) then
\bar{A}(\tilde{x}-)+K^{\prime}>0
for some fixed
K^{\prime}
; and the set of
u
for which
\bar{A}(\tilde{x}_{-})=\int_{Z}\Omega_{u}^{PSS}<C
is compact for each

.

A
framed bubbled pre-PSS trajectory
is defined as in Definition
31
, with the same notion of equivalence, but with different markings: now we only have marked geodesic segments from
p_{j-1}
to
p_{j}
only up to

for some

, with the geodesic segment from

at

. As before we write
\psi_{u,J}
for the canonical biholomorphism
