We can use
\Omega_{u}
chosen based on
H_{1}
as in Section
3.1
to make sense of this definition, and the arguments of that section continue to show that
\int_{\Sigma_{\alpha}}\Omega_{u}
is integral on any component of a framed bubbled pre-PSS trajectory.

We can now define the thickenings
T_{\Lambda_{d}^{PSS}}
to consist of pairs
((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)
where
(u,\Sigma,F)
is a framed bubbled pre-PSS trajectory with
u_{F}\in\mathcal{F}^{PSS}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
, and
\eta\in(V^{PSS}_{d})_{u_{F}}
which satisfies

\begin{gathered}\bar{\partial}_{F}u|_{S_{j}}+\lambda^{PSS}_{d}(\eta)\circ di_{F}=0;\\
\partial_{s}\tilde{u}+J\partial_{t}(\tilde{u}-X_{H_{s}})+\lambda^{PSS}_{d}(\eta)\circ di_{F}\circ d\psi_{u,J}=0,\text{ where }\tilde{u}=u|_{S^{j}}\circ\psi_{u,J};\\
\bar{\partial}u|_{S^{2}_{\alpha}}+\lambda^{PSS}_{d}(\eta)\circ di_{F}=0\text{ for all other components }S^{2}_{\alpha}\neq S^{2}_{1},\ldots,S^{2}_{J}.\end{gathered}

(138)

We then define
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}}=(PU(d+1),T_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}},\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1},\sigma_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}}\oplus\sigma_{H})
defined exactly as in Definition
12
. We write
\mathcal{T}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}}
to to be the sub-chart of
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}}
for which
\|\eta\|<\epsilon
. We have that

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{PSS}(d)
, the quotient of the zero section of the Kuranishi section of
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}}
by the
PU(d+1)
-action, is the set of bubbled PSS trajectories in
M
(of
\Omega_{u}
-degree
d
). We write

{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{PSS}(\tilde{x}_{-})=\{u\in\bigcup_{d}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{PSS}(\overline{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{x}_{-}))|\lim_{z\to p_{-}}u(z)=x_{-},u\text{ defines the capping }\tilde{x}_{-}\}.

(139)

As before, we have that, for sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, the
PU(d+1)
action on
\mathcal{T}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda}
has finite stabilizers, and that the induced topology on
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{PSS}(d)
agrees with the Gromov-Floer topology. The argument of Lemma
28
adapts to show that one can choose compatible perturbation data
\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}
such that the extended linearized operators are always surjective on PSS solutions, which implies that
T_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}}^{\epsilon}
is a
PU(d+1)-\langle d-1\rangle
-manifold with projection
\Pi
to
\mathcal{F}^{PSS}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
a fiberwise-smooth
C^{1}_{loc}PU(d+1)
bundle.

Morse moduli spaces

We now need to couple the moduli spaces defined above to Morse-theoretic moduli spaces to define the PSS map. Given
x^{\prime}_{\pm}\in Crit(H)
, write
\mathcal{M}^{M}(x^{\prime}_{-},x^{\prime}_{+})
for the set of Morse gradient trajectories

\mathcal{M}^{M}(x_{-},x_{+})=\{\gamma:\mathbb{R}\to M|\dot{\gamma}=-\nabla f,\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\gamma(s)=x_{\pm}\}/\mathbb{R}

(140)

and write
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M}(x^{\prime}_{-},x^{\prime}_{+})
for its compactification by broken morse trajectories. Furthermore, let

{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M-out}(x_{+})
be the natural compactification of the moduli space of Morse trajectories starting from an arbitrary point of
M
and flowing into
x_{+}
:

\begin{gathered}\mathcal{M}^{M-out}(x_{+})=\{\gamma:[0,\infty)\to M|\dot{\gamma}=-\nabla f,\lim_{s\to\infty}\gamma(s)=x_{+}\}\\
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M-out}(x_{+})=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\geq 0\\
x_{0},\ldots,x_{k}=x_{+}\in Crit(f)\\
x_{i}\neq x_{j}\text{ for }i\neq j\end{subarray}}\mathcal{M}^{M-out}(x_{0})\times\mathcal{M}^{M}(x_{0},x_{1})\times\ldots\times\mathcal{M}^{M}(x_{0},x_{k})\\
\end{gathered}

(141)

We note that there is a bound on the number of breakings an element of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M}(x_{-},x_{+})
or of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M-out}(x_{+})
, namely
ind(x_{-})-ind(x_{+})-1
for
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M}(x_{-},x_{+})
and
2n-ind(x_{+})
for
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M-out}(x_{+})
. In particular, for generic Morse-Smale
H
, we have that
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M}(x_{-},x_{+})
is a
\langle ind(x_{-})-ind(x_{+})-1\rangle
manifold, while

{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M}(x_{-},x_{+})
is a
\langle 2n-ind(x_{+})\rangle
manifold. We have an evaluation map
ev:{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M-out}(x_{+})\to M
sending
(\gamma,\ldots)
to
\gamma(0)
.

We then define

T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}^{PSS},M}(x^{\prime}_{+})=T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}^{PSS}}\times_{M}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M-out}(x^{\prime}_{+})

(142)

where the fiber product is over the evaluation map
ev{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{M-out}(x^{\prime}_{+})\to M
and the evaluation map
u\mapsto u(p_{+})
for
((u,\Sigma,F),\eta)\in T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}}
. We define
\mathcal{T}^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}^{PSS},M}(x_{+})=(PU(d+1),T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}^{PSS},M}(x^{\prime}_{+}),\pi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d+1}),(\sigma_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d}}\oplus\sigma_{H})\circ\pi_{1})
where
\pi_{1}
is projection onto
T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}^{PSS}}
and the
PU(d+1)
action is only on that factor. We have that
T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}^{PSS},M}(x_{+})
is a
\langle d-1+(2n-x_{+}-1)\rangle
-manifold.

Let us write the quotient of the zero section of the Kuranishi section by the group action on
T^{\epsilon}_{\Lambda_{d}^{PSS},M}(x_{+})
as
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{PSS,M}(x_{+})
. Writing
\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+}
for the constant cap on
x^{\prime}_{+}
, let
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{PSS,M}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+})
be the subset of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{PSS,M}(x_{+})
for which the projection to the first factor lies in
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{PSS}(\tilde{x}_{-})
. We call elements of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{PSS,M}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})

PSS trajectories from
\tilde{x}_{-}
to
\tilde{x}_{+}
.

6.4
Definition of the PSS map

Morse Flow category

We first define a proper
E
-proper graded
\Pi
-equivariant flow category
\mathcal{C}^{Morse}
corresponding to Morse cohomology. The objects are
Ob(M)=\Pi Crit(H)
. We have that
\mathcal{C}^{Morse}(\pi\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{-},\pi\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+})={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}^{(}x_{-},x_{+})
for
\pi\in\Pi
and
\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{\pm}
the constant caps at
x^{\prime}_{\pm}\in Crit(H)
. Otherwise we set
\mathcal{C}^{Morse}(\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{-},\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+})=\emptyset
. We use the usual
\mu
and energy function
E
, thinking of the capped critical points as constant Hamiltonian trajectories. We set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}^{Morse}(\pi\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{-},\pi\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+})}=[ind(x^{\prime}_{-})-ind(x^{\prime}_{+})-1]
and otherwise we set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}^{Morse}(\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{-},\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+})}=\bullet
. Composition is given by gluing Morse trajectories and maps of sets directly analogous to (
110
). The recapping action makes
\mathcal{C}^{Morse}

\Pi
-equivariant.

By choosing a compatible system of framings for the Morse moduli spaces
[
CJS95
]
we can think of
{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(x_{-},x_{+})
as normally complex derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifolds in a trivial way, as they are smooth manifolds
[
BH01
]

[
Weh12
]
; thus we have a compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts
\mathcal{C}^{\prime Morse}
which
\mathcal{C}^{Morse}
. The process of choosing a compatible system of perturbations
\sigma^{\prime}
for
\mathcal{C}^{Morse}
is trivial as such a choice is unique, and we have that
|\mathcal{C}^{Morse}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}}
is the Morse cochain complex over
\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

PSS Flow Category

We now proceed to define a filtered flow category
\mathcal{C}^{PSS}
which will contain
\mathcal{C}_{Floer}(H_{1},J)
and
\mathcal{C}^{Morse}[-1]
as full subcategories satisfying the conditions of Lemma
38
. This specifies the gradings, the functions
E
(given by the usual symplectic actions), and the
\Pi
actions. It suffices to define
\mathcal{C}^{PSS}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+})
for all pairs
\tilde{x}_{-}\in\widetilde{Fix}(\phi_{H_{1}}^{1})
,
\tilde{x}_{+}\in\widetilde{Fix}(\phi_{H}^{1})
. We set
\mathcal{C}^{PSS}(\pi\tilde{x}_{-},\pi\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+})={\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+})
for
\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+}
the constant capping at
x^{\prime}_{+}
and
\pi\in\Pi
. We set
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{C}^{PSS}(\pi\tilde{x}_{-},\pi\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+})}=[\mathcal{A}(\tilde{x}_{-})-1+(2n-indx_{+})-1]
. Otherwise we set
\mathcal{C}^{PSS}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}^{\prime}_{+})=\emptyset
. The composition operation glues Morse trajectories or Floer trajectories to PSS trajectories in the usual way, while the composition operation on sets is as in (
110
), with
\mathcal{C}_{cont}
replaced by
\mathcal{C}^{PSS}
. This makes
\mathcal{C}^{PSS}
into proper,
E
-proper
\Pi
-equivariant flow category.

The methods of Sections 3 and 4 adapt to this setting to show that

Proposition 15
.

The flow category
\mathcal{C}^{PSS}
admits a compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts arising from the topological Kuranishi charts
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda^{PSS}_{d},M}
.

Applying Lemma
38
, we produce a map

PSS:|\mathcal{C}^{Morse}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\to|\mathcal{C}(H_{1},J)(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}.

(143)

Completion of existence of PSS map

We now argue almost identically to
13
. We note first that the flow category
\mathcal{C}^{PSS}
is
E
-positive by (
137
).
Thus we can tensor
PSS
up to a map

PSS^{univ}:|\mathcal{C}^{Morse}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\to|\mathcal{C}(H_{1},J)(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{univ}_{\mathbb{Z}}}.

(144)

The
E
-positivity of
\mathcal{C}^{PSS}
implies that
PSS^{univ}
is induced from a corresponding map
PSS^{univ}_{0}
of
\Lambda^{univ}_{0,\mathbb{Z}}
modules. Now, we claim that for sufficiently small perturbations
H_{1}
of
H
(which we choose to not change actions of corresponding fixed points), there is a unique nondegenerate PSS trajectory from
\tilde{x}
to
\tilde{x}^{\prime}
, where the caps are the constant caps at
x
and
x^{\prime}
. This holds because this would be true if
H=H_{1}
and
H^{PSS}_{s}
was constant then the
constant
PSS trajectory at the constant orbit
x=x^{\prime}
would do this. But this solution is nondegenerate and so persists upon a sufficiently small perturbation.

We have that
E(\tilde{x})=E(\tilde{x}^{\prime})
, and thus (c.f. Lemma
38
) we can write
PSS^{univ}_{0}
as
1+T^{\epsilon}PSS^{\prime}
for some map
PSS^{\prime}
of
\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
modules. By the Nakayama lemma this proves

Proposition 16
.

The map
PSS
of (
143
) is a quasi-isomorphism.

6.5
Proof of Theorem
1

We now combine the methods of this section to prove the main theorem.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

First, we replace the main Hamiltonian
H
of the theorem with a new Hamiltonian, which we will also denote
H
, so that the number of fixed points does not change but all of the corresponding orbits are nonconstant. We choose a
C^{2}
-small morse function
H_{0}
and a small perturbation
H^{\prime}_{0}
of
H_{0}
as in Section
6.3
which would allow us to define the
PSS
map. We fix a background almost complex structure
J
We consider the following pair of diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}

(145)

We choose continuation homotopy data filling out each of these pairs of diagrams, such that the continuation data associated to any arrow from a Hamiltonian to itself is the identity continuation datum; as well as a PSS datum to compare Morse theory of
H_{0}
and Floer theory of
H^{\prime}_{0}
. We can
(\tilde{\Omega},N_{1})
so that we can simultaneously define all of these maps, and then choose
\bar{H}_{s,\tau}
and
\bar{H}^{PSS}
as in the corressponding sections. Applying the constructions of the previous sections defines maps on Floer complexes on the Hamiltonians as in the diagrams above, but with the arrows reversed, which are homotopy commutative. By Proposition
13
the nontrivial continuation maps when composed with quasi-isomorphisms become homotopic to quasi-isomorphisms, so they themselves are quasi-isomorphisms. Thus the Floer complex of
H
is quasi-isomorphic to the Floer complex of
H^{\prime}_{0}
, which is quasi-isomorphic to the Morse complex of
H_{0}
by Proposition
16
. We concluse by Lemma
35
.
∎

7
Bifurcation analysis and bordisms of flow categories

We note that in the above proof of Theorem
1
, we have never established the independence of the Floer complex of the choice of systems of perturbations, nor of the choices of integralization data or the choices of
J
or the perturbation data and stabilizing vector bundles. Showing independence of
J
can be done as in the proof above, using continuation maps. While it is possible to show independence of the perturbation data or or the integralization data using continuation-like ideas, below we describe a different method to show independence of various data of Floer theoretic maps. Namely, we describe the idea of a
bordism of flow categories
and a
bordism of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts
, and outline how this can show independence of floer complexes of the choices of perturbations or of the choices of integralization data. In essence, adapting the
bifurcation method
for studying Floer theoretic operations to the setting of flow-categories can lead to more elegant proofs. We hope this will be helpful to symplectic topologists trying to explore the new invariants made possible by global charts in Hamiltonian floer homology.

Definition 32
.

For each of
\mathcal{D}=Top(Fin^{\prime}),DerOrb^{\prime},DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}
there is a corresponding monoidal category

\mathcal{D}_{bord}=Top(Fin^{\prime})_{bord},DerOrb^{\prime}_{bord}
,
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C},bord}
defined as follows.

The category
Top(Fin^{\prime})_{bord}
has objects given by
(S,X)\in Top(Fin^{\prime})
equipped with continuous
p:X\to[0,1]
such that
p^{-1}(0)\cup p^{-1}(1)=X(S\setminus\bar{p})
for some distinguished
\bar{p}\in S
. Morphisms from
(S,X_{S})
to
(T,X_{T})
are those in
Top(Fin^{\prime})
but with the map
\bar{f}:X_{S}\to X_{T}
required to commute with
p
and the map
f:S\to T
required to send the distinguished element
\bar{p}\in S
to the distinguished element
\bar{p}\in T
. The monoidal structure is given b
(S,X_{S})\times(T,X_{T})=(S\cup_{\bar{p}}T,X_{S}\times_{p}X_{T})
where
S\cup_{\bar{p}}T
means we have identified the two copies of the distinguished element in the disjoint union, and the stratification on
X_{S}\times_{p}X_{T}
is
X_{S}\times_{p}X_{T}(R)=X_{S}(R\cap S)\times X_{T}(R\cap T)
for
R\subset S\cup_{\bar{p}}T
.

The categories
DerOrb^{\prime}_{bord}
and
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C},bord}
have objects pairs
(S,X)
which are elements of
DerOrb^{\prime}
or
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}
, respectively, with
\bar{p}\in S
a distinguished element, and, writing
X=(T,V,\sigma)
, with an additional submersion
p
from the
\langle S\rangle
-orbifold
T
to the
\langle 1\rangle
-orbifold
[0,1]
for which
S_{f}=S\setminus\{\bar{p}\}
, and the map of sets
S\setminus S_{f}=\{\bar{p}\}\to[1]
is the unique such map. In particular, this implies that
T(S\setminus\bar{p})=p^{-1}(0)\cup p^{-1}(1)
. Maps are as in
DerOrb^{\prime}
or
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}
, respectively, but the maps on sets must preserve distinguished elements and the maps on underlying
\langle k\rangle
orbifolds must commute with the maps
p
. The monoidal structure is given by

(S_{1},(T_{S_{1}},V_{S_{1}},\sigma_{S_{1}}))\times(S_{2},(T_{S_{2}},V_{S_{2}},\sigma_{S_{2}}))=(S_{1}\cup_{\bar{p}}S_{2},(T_{S_{1}}\times_{p}T_{S_{2}},V_{S_{1}}\times V_{S_{2}}|_{T_{S_{1}}\times_{p}T_{S_{2}}},\sigma_{S_{1}}\times\sigma_{S_{2}}|_{T_{S_{1}}\times_{p}T_{S_{2}}})).

(146)

There are natural monoidal forgetful functors
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C},bord}\to DerOrb^{\prime}_{bord}\to Top(Fin^{\prime})_{bord}
. Moreover, for each of these categories
\mathcal{D}
, there are a pair of monoidal restriction functors
\mathcal{D}_{bord}\to\mathcal{D}
, denoted
X\mapsto X|_{0}
and
X\mapsto X|_{1}
, which restict all the data to
p^{-1}(0)
or to
p^{-1}(1)
, respectively. These restiction functors commute with the forgetful functors.

Definition 33
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is graded, proper, and
\Pi
-equivariant.

A
bordism of flow categories
is a category
\mathcal{C}_{bord}
enriched in
Top(Fin^{\prime})_{bord}
. We define
|S_{\mathcal{C}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})}|=r(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
. The notions of
\Pi
-equivariance and properness, and gradings carry over.

A bordism of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts over a graded proper
\Pi
-equivariant bordism of flow categories
\mathcal{C}_{bord}
is a category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}
enriched in
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C},bord}
equipped with a free action of
\Pi
such that applying the forget functor
DerOrb^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C},bord}\to Top(Fin^{\prime})_{bord}
recovers
\mathcal{C}_{bord}
, and such that
vdim(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}))=\mu(\tilde{x})-\mu(\tilde{y})
for each
\tilde{x},\tilde{y}\in Ob(\mathcal{C}_{bord})
with
\mathcal{C}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq\emptyset
and
\tilde{x}\neq\tilde{y}
.

Note that
\mathcal{C}_{bord}|_{0}
,
\mathcal{C}_{bord}|_{1}
are both flow categories, and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}|_{0}
,
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}|_{1}
are compatible systems of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts for these flow categories, respectively.

If
\Pi
is a Novikov group, the notions of a
\Pi
-equivariant bordism of flow categories are modified as they are for flow categories, and the notions of
E
-positivity and
E
-properness extend to this case via the same definition.

Proposition 17
.

Say
\Pi
is a Novikov group.

Given a bordism of perturbation data
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}
over a
\Pi
-equivariant
E
-proper
E
-positive topological flow category
\mathcal{C}
, and a pair of choices of compatible systems of perturbations
\sigma^{\prime}_{0}
,
\sigma^{\prime}_{1}
for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{0}:=\mathcal{C}_{bord}|_{0}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}:=\mathcal{C}_{bord}|_{1}
respectively, there is a homotopy equivalence

\bar{f}:|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime}_{0})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\to|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime}_{1})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}

(147)

and similarly for the complexes over
\Lambda^{\Pi^{0}_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Proof.

This statement is directly analogous to the proof of the invariance of the Novikov complex computing the homology of a closed
1
-form on a manifold by analyzing a generic family of closed
1
-forms in the same cohomology class. We follow the strategy of
[
Hut02
]
, which was implemented for several variants of Hamiltonian Floer homology in the semipositive setting by
[
Lee05
,
Lee05a
]
. Alternatively,
[
Fuk+09
]
uses a variant of this method to prove invariance of Floer homology under deformations of
J
and of other auxiliary data chosen in that method, and
[
Fuk+09
, Section 7.2.14]
contains several interesting comments about various implementations of this general idea.

Let us recall the finite-dimensional situation. Let
\alpha\in\Omega^{1}(M)
be closed
1
-form and
V
the vector field dual to
\alpha
for some metric on
M
. Assume moreover that the zeros of
V
are nondegenerate, as are its closed orbits, and the stable and unstable manifolds of all zeros of
V
intersect transversely. Let
C_{i}
be the set of zeros of
V
of index
i
. Choosing a minimal abelian cover
pr:\tilde{M}\to M
on which the pullback of
\alpha
becomes exact, we have a Novikov group
(\Pi,E,\mu)
where
\Pi
is the covering group of
\tilde{M}
,
\mu
is identically zero, and
E(\pi)=\int_{\alpha}[\gamma_{\pi}]
where
\gamma_{\pi}:S^{1}\to M
is a loop representing a class in
H_{1}(M)
which maps to
\pi
under the surjection
H_{1}(M)\to\Pi
. We can then form the (cohomological)
Novikov complex

CN(\alpha)^{\bullet}
of
\alpha

[
Hut02
]
; this is generated as a module over
\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}

by the zeros of
V
, and the differential counts rigid flow lines of
V
between its zeros. We write the set of zeros of
V
as of index
i
as
C_{i}
, and the set of lifts of these zeros to
\tilde{X}
i.e. the set of index-
i
zeros of
pr^{*}V
, as
\tilde{C}_{i}
. Given a one-parameter family of closed
1
-forms
\alpha_{\tau}
with corresponding vector fields
V_{\tau}
, the following bifurcations can occur:

(a)

A degenerate flow line from
\tilde{p}\in\tilde{C}_{i}
to
\tilde{q}\in\tilde{C}_{i-1}
.

(b)

A degenerate closed orbit;

(c)

A flow line from
\tilde{p}\in\tilde{C}_{i}
to
\tilde{q}\in\tilde{C}_{i}
,
pr(\tilde{p})\neq pr(\tilde{q})
;

(d)

A flow line from
\tilde{p}
to
\pi\tilde{p}
for some element
\pi
in the Novikov group
\Pi
;

(e)

Birth or death of two critical points of two critical points at a degenerate critical point.

The first two bifurcations do not change the Novikov complex. The last bifurcation adds a pair of canceling to the Novikov complex. The second and third bifurcations change the Novikov complex by a change of basis, in the sense that, writing
d^{j},d^{\prime}_{j}:CN^{j}\to CN^{j+1}
for the differentials before and after the bifurcation, we have that
d^{j}=d^{\prime j}
for
j\neq i,i-1
, and
d^{\prime}_{i-1}=Ad_{i-1}
,
d^{\prime}_{i}=d_{i}A
, where
A:CN^{i}\to CN^{i}
is some
\Lambda^{\Pi}_{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear isomorpism.
Concretely, (c) corresponds to changing the basis such that
\tilde{p}
is replaced by
\tilde{p}\pm\tilde{q}
, and (d) corresponds changing the basis of the Novikov complex for which
\tilde{p}
is multiplied by an invertible power series
(1+h+\ldots)
.

We will see that there for bordisms of flow categories there are a corresponding set of bifurcations, and the analysis can be performed as in
[
Hut02
]
because Kuranishi charts give finite-dimensional models of all phenomena involved.

Note that
Ob(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord})=Ob(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{0})=Ob(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1})
, and that as
\mathbb{Z}/N_{\Pi}\mathbb{Z}
-graded modules the domain and the codomain of
\bar{f}
agree. Since
\mathcal{C}_{bord}
is proper we can assume for the purposes of this proof that
\#(Ob(\mathcal{C})/\Pi)<\infty
.

As before, write
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=(T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}),V_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}),\sigma_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}))
.

First, using an induction as in Proposition
9
, we modify the continuous functions
p:T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
to functions
p^{\prime}:T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
which are smooth, are submersions along
T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{i}
for
i=0,1
, and still make
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}
into a bordism of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts for
\mathcal{C}_{bord}
. This is possible since we can take
p^{\prime}
to be linear functions of the distances to
T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})_{i}
for
i=0,1
in open neighborhoods of these boundaries and inductively interpolate by smooth functions on the rest of the thickenings without introducing any additional
0
s or
1
s. We then inductively stabilize all charts by trivial complex vector bundles and extend
p^{\prime}
to smooth functions on the stabilized thickenings in order to arrange for the functions
p^{\prime}
to be submersions.
With this modification, we will have that for all
t\in[0,1]
,
\mathcal{C}_{bord}|_{t}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
is a smooth derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold chart given by
p^{-1}(t)\subset T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
(with the restrictions of the vector bundle and the Kuranishi section to this smooth sub-orbifold). These charts satisfy the needed compatibility conditions whenever they exist, and so for all
t\in[0,1]
, we have that
\mathcal{C}_{bord}|_{t}
admits a system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}|_{t}
.

Now, we would like to apply a modification of Proposition
9
to find a “
\Pi
-equivariant compatible system of perturbations
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}
for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}
” such that
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})(i,x)=\sigma^{\prime}_{i}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})(x)
for
i=0,1
and
\tilde{x},\tilde{y}\in Ob(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord})
with
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
(whenever
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq\emptyset
, which may or may not hold for any particular pair
\tilde{x},\tilde{y}
). However, the method of Proposition
9
does not adapt to this setting because, while the product of a collection of FOP-transverse functions is transverse, the
fiber product
of a collection of FOP-transverse functions can very easily not be transverse. However, if one of the FOP-transverse functions in the collection has no zeros, then the fiber product will have no zeros and so will be transverse. This allows for the induction of Proposition
9
to go through for all pairs
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
such that
\text{vdim }T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})<0
.

Write
Pairs_{0}
for set of pars
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
with
vdimT_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=1
and
Pairs_{-1}
for the set of such pairs with
vdimT_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=0
. The first challenge is that for pairs
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs_{-1}
, there can be codimension-1 boundary components of
T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
which are fiber products
T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{z})\times_{[0,1]}T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{z})
where
(\tilde{x},\tilde{z})
and
(\tilde{z},\tilde{y})
are in
Pairs_{-1}
. As such, if
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{z})
and
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{z},\tilde{y})
have (isolated, by inductive assumption) zeros with equal values of
p
, then
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
would be forced to have a zero in a boundary component, which is not acceptable by inductive assumption. Nonetheless, we can perform the induction for all pairs
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs_{-1}
while
weakening
the transvesrality assumption to transversality of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}
on the interior of these moduli spaces, together with the condition that all bounady zeros arise as fiber products of transverse zeros on interiors of moduli spaces. Now, if we did not have the requirement that
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}
is
\Pi
-equivariant, then by choosing
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
generically for
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs_{-1}
, we would be able to arrange that no pair of interior zeros of these sections have equal values of
p
, and thus that the above fiber products are empty, and that the resulting sections are FOP transverse. However, when the sections
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
are required to be
\Pi
equivariant, an interior zero of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\pi\tilde{x})
or
\pi\in\Pi
generates a sequence of boundary zeros for
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\pi^{r}\tilde{x})
for all
r=1,2,\ldots.
, where we note that if
(x,\pi x)\in Pairs_{-1}
then
(x,\pi^{r}x)\in Pairs_{-1}
. By requiring that all interior zeros of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
have distinct values of
p
, we can arrange that these are the only violations of FOP-transversality of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
for
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs_{-1}
.

We then choose perturbations
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
for
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs_{0}
, satisfying the induction conditions of Proposition
9
. If a boundary component of
T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
for
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs_{0}
is a fiber product of thickenings none of which have negative virtual dimension, then they all must have virtual dimension zero except for one of them. By inductive assumption, we assume that the perturbation chosen for that virtual dimension
1
component is FOP transverse, which is possible as that chart has no boundary components. We extend
\sigma^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
to a neighborhood of the boundary exactly as in Lemma
13
, and then extend via an FOP-transverse section on the interior. As such, we have
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})^{-1}(0)
, for
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs_{0}
, is a
1
-manifold with boundary, with boundary either lying on
T_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})_{i}
for
i=0,1
, or arising from a joining of an interior zero of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x}^{\prime},\tilde{y}^{\prime})
for some
(\tilde{x}^{\prime},\tilde{y}^{\prime})\in Pairs_{0}
with either

(A)

a sequence of self-glued trajectories arising from an interior zero of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{z},\pi\tilde{z})
with
(\tilde{z},\pi\tilde{z})\in Pairs_{-1})
, or

(B)

with a single zero of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime},\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime})
for some pair
(\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime},\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime})\in Pairs_{-1}

We can consider the restrictions of these perturbations
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}|_{t}
to
T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{t}
. By perturbing
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
on the interior of
T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
for all
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
, we can arrange for the restrictions
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{t}
to be FOP-transverse on
T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
for all
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs_{0}
for all
t\in[0,1]
such that
t
is not in some countable set
T_{bad}\subset[0,1]
. For any
t\notin T_{bad}
, we can then apply the induction of Proposition
9
to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}|_{t}
to define
|\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}|_{t}(\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}|_{t})|
; note, however, that this complex is entirely determined by the choices of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
for
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs(0)
made previously, and this step is only needed to verify that the putative differential squares to zero.

The elements of
T_{bad}
consist of those
t\in[0,1]
such that there exists a
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs_{-1}
and a
u\in\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})^{-1}(0)
with
p(u)=t
; or those for which
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{t}
is not FOP-transverse at some interior point for some
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\in Pairs_{0}
; and only one of these alternatives occurs for any given
t\in T_{bad}
. By
E
-properness of
\mathcal{C}_{bord}
, to compute the differential up to
\Lambda^{E_{0}}
it suffices to analyze finitely-many moduli spaces. Thus, for every
E_{0}
we can compare terms in the differential associated to
\mathcal{C}_{bord}|_{t^{\prime}}
up to order
T^{E_{0}}
by analyzing a finite number of moduli spaces; in particular, there is an
\epsilon
such that the terms in the differential up to order
T^{E_{0}}
all have the same value for
t^{\prime}\in[t-\epsilon,t)
and a different value for
t^{\prime}\in(t,t+\epsilon]
. To understand this change in the chain complex corresponding to these
t\in T_{bad}
, it suffices to study the manifolds

R(\tilde{x},\tilde{y};t-\epsilon,t+\epsilon)=\{u\in\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})^{-1}(0)|p(y)\in[t-\epsilon,t+\epsilon])\}

(148)

as they control whether the number of points of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})_{t-\epsilon}^{-1}(0)
agree or not with
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})_{t+\epsilon}^{-1}(0)
for sufficiently small
\epsilon
. But the second kind of point
t\in T_{bad}
in the classification at the beginning of this paragraph does not change the number of points of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{t\pm\epsilon}
since
R(\tilde{x},\tilde{y};t-\epsilon,t+\epsilon)
gives a bordism between the points on the two sides.

The other kind of points
t\in T_{bad}
make it possible for one or neither of the alternatives (A), (B) above to occur. If neither occurs then clearly
R(\tilde{x},\tilde{y};t-\epsilon,t+\epsilon)
is still a bordism and the counts are unchanged. Otherwise, the cases (A), (B) corresponds respectively to the cases (d), (c) of the finite dimensional model, and one can argue as in
[
02
]
. In case (B), we must either have
\tilde{x}^{\prime}=\tilde{x}
,
\tilde{y}^{\prime}=\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}
and
\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime}=\tilde{y}
; or
\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}=\tilde{x}
,
\tilde{y}^{\prime\prime}=\tilde{x}^{\prime}
, and
\tilde{y}^{\prime}=\tilde{y}
. Let us assume the first case without loss of generality; then as
t^{\prime}
increases through
t
, orientation computations as in
[
laudenbach
, (g)]
(see
[
Hut02
, Lemma 3.4]
) show that the number of zeros of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})|_{t^{\prime}}
either increases or decreases by the number of zeros of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{z})|_{t}
in a way corresponding to the basis change
\tilde{z}\mapsto\tilde{z}\pm\tilde{y}
. Reversing the roles of
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
and
(\tilde{y},\tilde{z})
shows that for this bifurcation, for any fixed
E_{0}
there is an
\epsilon
such that terms in the diferential up to order
T^{E_{0}}
change as in case (c) of the finite dimensional case as
t^{\prime}
passes from
t^{\prime}-\epsilon
to
t^{\prime}+\epsilon
.

To deal with the subcase of (A) above corresponding to the finite-dimensional case (d), we note that the only obstruction to the above argument is that the
\Pi
-equivariance of
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}
, which makes the non-transverse self-gluings of (A) possible. However, as in
[
Hut02
]
we break
\Pi
-equivariance of the perturbation
\sigma^{\prime}_{bord}
in a small region of
t^{\prime}\in[t-\epsilon,t+\epsilon]
, instead (say) making the perturbation equivariant for
N\Pi\subset\Pi
for some large
N
; for sufficiently large
N
this reduces cases (A) to case (B) and one sees that this corresponds to performing a basis change like
\tilde{x}\mapsto(1\pm\pi\pm O(\pi^{2}))\tilde{x}
in the sense that for any fixed
E_{0}
there is an
\epsilon>0
such that terms in the differential up to order
T^{E_{0}}
will undergo precisely this change as
t^{\prime}
passes from
t^{\prime}-\epsilon
to
t^{\prime}+\epsilon
.

As the Novikov ring is complete, we can conclude that the homotopy-equivalence
|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime}_{0})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\to|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime}_{1})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}
can be constructed from an infinite convergent composition of basis changes corresponding to cases (A) and (B) above.

∎

Using the above lemmata, we can prove

Proposition 18
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq\emptyset
and
\tilde{x}\neq\tilde{y}
implies that
E(\tilde{x})-E(\tilde{y})\leq 0
. Then the homotopy type of
|\mathcal{C}(\sigma^{\prime})|_{\Lambda^{\Pi_{E}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}
only depends on the underlying compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{bord}
be the “trivial bordism of flow categories on
\mathcal{C}
”; this has
Ob(\mathcal{C}_{bord})=Ob(\mathcal{C})
, and
\mathcal{C}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=[0,1]\times\mathcal{C}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
whenever
\tilde{x}\neq\tilde{y}
and
CC(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\neq\emptyset
, and the map
p:\mathcal{C}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\to[0,1]
is simply the projection. We inherit the
\Pi
-action, grading, and energy function from
\mathcal{C}
. We apply Proposition
17

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}
, the trivial bordism of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts on
\mathcal{C}_{bord}
, which has
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=([0,1]\times T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}),[0,1]\times V(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}),\sigma_{bord}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
where

and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=(T(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}),V(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}),\sigma(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}))
with

.
∎

Proposition 19
.

The quasi-isomorphism type of
CF^{\bullet}
does not depend on the choices of
\tilde{\Omega}
,
N_{1}
,
h
or the perturbation data and stabilizing vector bundles used to define it.

Proof.

(Sketch).

This argument is a coherent, categorical, and slightly clarified version of
[
AMS21
, Proposition 6.31]
.

There are spaces
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}},d_{2})
for
\ell_{1}\geq d_{1}
and
\ell_{2}\geq d_{2}
consisting of stable maps with two marked points
p_{\pm}
on their domains, with target
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}}\times{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}}
such that the projected maps to each factor are also stable, and such that the domains are equipped with geodesic markings as in Section
3.2.1
.
There is a pair of maps
f_{i}:\mathcal{F}^{dbl}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}},d_{2})\to\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{i}},d_{i})
for
i=1,2
, which send a stable map to its two projections. Notice, however, that the product map
f_{1}\times f_{2}
is not an inclusion: given a pair of stable maps with isomorphic domain types, one must choose an
isomorphism of the domains
to choose an element of the fiber of
f_{1}\times f_{2}
. The universal curve over
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{2}},d_{2})
is denoted
\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{2}},d_{2})
.

Now,
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}},d_{2})
is a
2^{[d_{1}-1]\times[d_{2}-1]}
-stratified space with stratification as follows. Given
S\subset[d_{1}-1]\times[d_{2}-1]
, we write
S_{1}\subset[d_{1}-1]
and
S_{2}\subset[d_{2}-1]
for the projections. We say that
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}},d_{2})(S)=\emptyset
if, writing
S=\{(n_{1},m_{1}),(n_{2},m_{2}),\ldots,(n_{r},m_{r})\}
with
n_{1}<n_{2}<\ldots<n_{r}
, we do not have that
m_{1}<m_{2}\ldots<m_{r}
. If this condition is satisfied we set
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}},d_{2})(S)=f_{1}^{-1}(S_{1})\cap f_{2}^{-1}(S_{2})
. By a construction analogous to Lemma
23
, these spaces are smooth
(PU(\ell_{1}+1)\times PU(\ell_{2}+1))-\langle[d_{1}-1]\times[d_{2}-1]\rangle
-manifolds.
There are analogous spaces
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}},d_{2};p,\pm)
for which the image of
p_{\pm}
must go through a given point
p\in{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}}\times{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}}
. These are restrictions of
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}},d_{2})
to a
(U(\ell_{1})\times U(\ell_{2}))
-space along the inclusion
U(\ell_{1})\times U(\ell_{2})\subset PU(\ell_{1}+1)\times PU(\ell_{2}+1)
.

Write
d_{1}=d^{\prime}_{1}+d^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
d_{2}=d^{\prime}_{2}+d^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, choose
p_{1}\in{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d^{\prime}_{1}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}}
and
p_{2}\in{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d^{\prime}_{1}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{2}}
, and set
p=(p_{1},p_{2})
. Let
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{i}}
be the complementary subspaces to
{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d^{\prime}_{i}}\subset{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{i}}
in the sense of Section
.
Given
S_{1}=\{n_{1}<\ldots<n_{r}\}\subset[d_{1}-1]
and
S_{2}=\{m_{1}<\ldots<m_{r}\}\subset[d_{2}-1]
we define
S_{1}.S_{2}=\{(n_{1},m_{1}),\ldots,(n_{r},m_{r})\}
. We further define

\mathcal{F}^{tr}_{S(d^{\prime}_{1},d^{\prime\prime}_{1}).S(d^{\prime}_{2},d^{\prime\prime}_{2})}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}},d_{2})\subset\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}},d_{2})

(149)

analogously to the definition of

) as maps from a domain with two components where the map from the first component has degree

. We have have that
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{\ell_{2}},d_{2})(S(d^{\prime}_{1},d^{\prime\prime}_{1}).S(d^{\prime}_{2},d^{\prime\prime}_{2}))
is an induction of

along

, and that

is a generalized induction of

along

after modifying the set labeling the stratification by applying the inclusion

as is and adding
(d^{\prime}_{1},d^{\prime}_{2})
to elements of

.

Thus the analog of Lemma
24
with the the new spaces
\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d;{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d},d)
holds.

Recall that we have two different compatible choices of peturbation data peturbation data
\Lambda^{1}_{d}=(V^{1}_{d},\lambda^{1}_{d})
and
\Lambda^{2}_{d}=(V^{2}_{d},\lambda^{2}_{d})
. We also have a pair of choices
(\tilde{\Omega}^{1},N^{1}_{1},h^{1})
and
(\tilde{\Omega}^{2},N^{2}_{1},h^{2})
.

We define
\mathcal{T}^{dbl,bord}
to consist of tuples
(t,(u,\Sigma,F_{1},F_{2}),\eta_{1},\eta_{2})
were
t\in[0,1]
,
\Sigma
is a domain with two marked points
p_{\pm}
and geodesic markings as before,

is a map,
(u,\Sigma,F_{i})
are framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectories defined with respect to
\tilde{\Omega}^{i},N^{1}_{i},h^{i}
, (of corresponding degrees

) and
\eta_{i}\in(V^{i}_{d})_{u_{F_{i}}}
, satisfying

\bar{\partial}_{F}u+t\lambda_{d}^{1}(\eta_{1})\circ di_{F_{1}}+(1-t)\lambda_{d}^{2}(\eta_{2})\circ di_{F_{2}}=0,

(150)

up to equivalence. An equivalence of such data only exist when the data
t\in[0,1]
agree, and in that case an equivalence is an equivalence o the
doubly framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectories

(u,\Sigma,F_{1},F_{2})
, namely isomorphism between domains intertwining the markings and the maps
u
, together with isomorphisms of the line bundles
L_{\Omega_{u}^{1}}
and
L_{\Omega_{u}}^{2}
over isomorphisms of domains which send the bases
(F_{1},F_{2})
to the new bases. We define
\mathcal{T}^{dbl,bord}_{\Lambda_{d},\epsilon}
to be the subset with
\|\eta_{1}\|<\epsilon
and
\|\eta_{2}\|<\epsilon
.

The set of equivalence classes of doubly framed bubbled pre-Floer trajectories can be viewed as a subset of the set of choices of pairs of integers
(d_{1},d_{2})
, an element
u_{F_{1},F_{2}}\in\mathcal{F}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{2}},d_{2})
, and a smooth map from the fiber of the universal curve over this space satisfying several conditions. As before we can equip this set with the topology induced from the Hausdorff topology on the closures of these graphs in
\mathcal{C}({\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{1}},d_{1};{\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}}^{d_{2}},d_{2})\times M
.

Over
\mathcal{T}^{dbl,bord}_{\Lambda_{d}}
there is a vector bundle with fiber over
(t,(u,\Sigma,F_{1},F_{2}),\eta_{1},\eta_{2})
given by
(V^{1}_{d})_{u_{F_{1}}}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{1}+1}\oplus(V^{1}_{d})_{u_{F_{2}}}\oplus i\mathfrak{pu}_{d_{2}+1}
. This vector bundle has a section
\sigma=\sigma_{\Lambda^{1}_{d_{1}}}\oplus\sigma^{1}_{H}\oplus\sigma_{\Lambda^{2}_{d_{2}}}\oplus\sigma^{2}_{H})
where
\sigma^{i}_{H}
is given by
((u,\Sigma,F_{1},F_{2}),\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mapsto H_{i}(u,\Sigma,u_{F_{i}})
, where
H_{i}
is
H
defined using
\Omega_{i}
for
i=1,2
.

The group
PU(d_{1}+1)\times PU(d_{2}+1)
acts on everything in sight, and we have that
\mathcal{M}(d_{1},d_{2})=\sigma^{-1}(0)/PU(d_{1}+1)\times PU(d_{2}+1)
. For sufficiently small
\epsilon,\mathcal{T}^{dbl,bord}_{\Lambda_{d}}
is a
\langle 1+(d_{1}-1)\times(d_{2}-1)\rangle
-manifold because adding
\eta_{1}
or
\eta_{2}
individually to the moduli problem was already enough to make the linearized Cauchy-Riemann operators surjective. As in Lemma
26
, the stailizer groups are finite, and the argument of Lemma
27
adapt to this context essentially without change, and we thus have that
\mathcal{K}^{dbl,bord}=(T,V,\sigma)
are topological Kuranishi charts on

{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(d_{1},d_{2})=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{x}_{\pm}\in\widetilde{Fix}(H)\\
\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{i}(\tilde{x}_{-})-\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{i}(\tilde{x}_{+})=d_{i}\text{ for }i=1,2\end{subarray}}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}(\tilde{x}_{-},\tilde{x}_{+})\times[0,1].

(151)

Restricting these charts to
t=0
or
t=1
manifestly makes them into inductions of the Kuranishi charts constructed using
(\tilde{\Omega}^{i},N^{1}_{i},h^{i})
for
i=2,1
, respectively, as in

[
AMS21
, Proposition 6.31]
.

The methods of Section 3 and
4
allow one to use these to define a compatible system of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{bord}
on the trivial bordism of flow categories
\mathcal{C}(H,J)_{bord}
, in such a way that this gives a bordism of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts between the previously chosen systems of derived
\langle k\rangle
-orbifold charts
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(H,J)|_{2}
associated to
(\tilde{\Omega}^{i},N^{1}_{i},h^{i})
for
i=1,2
, respectively. We conclude by applying Propositions
17
and
18
.

∎
